- **Evidence:** Read server/index.ts:735-760 (the LegalHold branch and the terminal handler) and server/utils/errors.ts:1-60 including the ApiErrorResponse interface and its requestId rationale; read the res-status-raw ratchet baseline (498).

#### [medium/S] The 'a tool may not report an effect it did not have' gate reads two of at least five model-driven dispatch surfaces

- **Where:** `tests/unit/paxToolsReportRealEffects.test.ts:72 (`TOOL_SWITCHES`)`
- **Defect:** The register is `["server/ai/tools.ts", "server/ai/supportAgent.ts"]` — 57 + 91 cases. Not in the population: server/services/solene/dispatchToolExecutor.ts (18 cases, including a real `bash` spawn and `git_commit`), server/ai/vaService.ts, server/services/solene/chat/turnRunner.ts, and server/services/paxAskExecutors.ts. The test's own header is the best writing in the repo about why populations must be enumerated — it documents that this exact file shipped reading one file and was wrong within a day. It is now right about two and silent about three. This is not a hypothetical: the file it added after the first miss (supportAgent.ts) held two handlers of the identical shape.
- **Fix:** Add the three-to-four remaining switches to TOOL_SWITCHES with a per-member vacuity assertion (each named file must yield ≥1 parsed case, so a parser that stops matching one file fails rather than reading as clean). Better: derive the list — glob every file under server/ai/** and server/services/** containing a `switch (toolName)`-shaped dispatch and fail when a file matches the shape and is not in the register, so adding a sixth switch is what breaks.
- **Comparable:** This is mutation-testing discipline (Stryker) applied to a policy gate — the repo already does the mutation half brilliantly. What is missing is the derived-population half, which is how OpenAI's and Anthropic's own tool-registry lints avoid it.
- **Evidence:** Read tests/unit/paxToolsReportRealEffects.test.ts:1-80 and grepped its TOOL_SWITCHES; counted `case "` occurrences per candidate file (tools 57, supportAgent 91, dispatchToolExecutor 18) and enumerated dispatch-shaped files under server/ai, server/services/solene and server/services/pax*.

#### [medium/S] The constitution describes four founder doors; the shipped app has 81 founder routes

- **Where:** `CLAUDE.md lines 73-79; tests/unit/founderFourDoors.test.ts:20 (`FOUNDER_ROUTE_BASELINE = 81`); client/src/App.tsx (81 `path="/founder…"`)`
- **Defect:** CLAUDE.md states the canonical model is "exactly four primary doors plus one deliberate admin namespace." The ratchet baseline and the actual route table both say 81. The ratchet is well-designed — down-only, with a helpful failure message when the count drops — and the consolidation from ~88 is real progress. But the document a new engineer reads first asserts a state the code is 20× away from, with no indication of the gap. That is the same class of defect the repo polices everywhere else: a canonical statement whose adoption is unverified. It also matters practically — an engineer told "four doors, put it behind one" has 81 plausible parents to choose from.
- **Fix:** One sentence in CLAUDE.md: state the target (4 doors), the current count (81, per FOUNDER_ROUTE_BASELINE), and that the gap is being ratcheted down — the same way the table-count ratchet honestly states "the mature-machine north star is <=450 tables; today's count is far above it." That note is what makes the schema ratchet trustworthy and its absence is what makes this section misleading.
- **Comparable:** The repo's own table-count.json is the model: it names the target, admits the current distance, and explains each bump. Applying that honesty convention to the prose sections of CLAUDE.md costs nothing.
- **Evidence:** Read CLAUDE.md's founder-doors section; read tests/unit/founderFourDoors.test.ts:20 and its failure messages at :99-110; counted `path="/founder` in client/src/App.tsx (81).

#### [medium/M] The backend's directory structure is organized by AI-agent codename rather than by domain

- **Where:** `server/services/ (528 flat .ts files + 61 subdirectories: andrei/, beatrice/, iris/, krieger/, lena/, solene/, soren/, zernio/, rosy-river references)`
- **Defect:** Eight of the 61 service subdirectories are named after internal AI-agent personas, not after the domain they serve. The repo already knows codenames are a liability — there is a dedicated lint, eslint-rules/no-founder-codenames-in-customer-jsx.cjs, to keep them out of customer UI — yet they remain a primary axis of the backend's module tree. Alongside them sit 528 files directly in server/services/ with no grouping at all, including near-collisions like server/ai/paxModelTier.ts and server/services/paxModelTier.ts (two different concerns, same filename; the header of one has to explain the other), and aiRouter.ts vs modelRouter.ts. A new engineer looking for "where is customer health computed" or "where does a lead get enriched" has no structural affordance; they grep, find two answers (findings 9 and 12), and pick one.
- **Fix:** Do not rename everything at once. Do two cheap things: (a) add a short `server/services/README.md` mapping each codename directory to the domain it owns; (b) forbid new top-level files in server/services/ via the existing boundary lint, so growth is forced into a domain directory. Then fold codename directories into domain directories opportunistically as their code is touched.
- **Comparable:** Shopify's packwerk-enforced component boundaries and Spotify's domain-oriented service layout both make the directory the unit of ownership. The cheap, high-yield half is the 'no new files at the root' rule — it stops the flat layer growing without a migration.
- **Evidence:** Listed server/services (528 top-level .ts, 61 directories) and read the directory names; read both paxModelTier.ts headers (which document the split explicitly); read eslint-rules/ contents.

#### [medium/M] Two data-access layers with no boundary between them: a 528-method storage god-object and direct db access from 92 route files

- **Where:** `server/storage.ts (1,664 lines, ~528 interface methods, 34 `*Repo` mixins via `Object.assign` at :1628 and an `extends` chain at :1626) + 9,057 lines of server/storage/*Repo.ts; 92 route files importing `db``
- **Defect:** The mixin extraction is well-run and the line-count ratchet drives it down, but it has produced two layers that coexist rather than one that replaced the other: 617 distinct `storage.*` call sites on one side, and on the other 92 of 273 route files importing `db` directly with 51 calling `db.select(`. scripts/check-boundaries.mjs enforces only the client/shared/server axis (S1-S3, C1-C2) — nothing forbids a route handler from constructing SQL. That is the structural reason finding 1 exists and the reason the org-scope lint's storage-and-services population misses so much. Additionally, the `DatabaseStorage extends 32 Repos {}` + `Object.assign` pattern means the type system knows the shape but the runtime composition is a separate, unchecked list.
- **Fix:** Add one boundary rule to check-boundaries.mjs: `server/routes*.ts` may not import from `server/db`. Ratchet it at the current 92 and drive down — each conversion moves a query into a repo where the org-scope lint already sees it, so this single rule pays down findings 1, 2 and 19 simultaneously. That ordering matters: it makes the tenant-isolation gate's population grow as a side effect of ordinary work.
- **Comparable:** Packwerk (Shopify) and eslint-plugin-boundaries both express 'layer A may not import layer B' as a first-class, ratchetable rule. This repo already has the scanner; it is three lines of configuration away.
- **Evidence:** Read server/storage.ts:1626-1630 (the extends chain and Object.assign wiring); counted repo files (34) and lines (9,057), interface methods (528), distinct `storage.*` call sites (617), route files importing db (92) and calling db.select( (51); read check-boundaries.mjs:1-40 for the rules it actually enforces.

#### [medium/L] A 17,967-line shared/schema.ts is the single most-edited file in a parallel-agent development model

- **Where:** `shared/schema.ts (17,967 lines, 464 pgTables); shared/schema/*.ts (85 files, 260 more)`
- **Defect:** 464 of the 728 tables live in one module that is imported across server, shared and the client-bundle boundary. The split into shared/schema/*.ts started and stalled at 36% — and the split is not by domain either (it is 85 mostly-single-feature files, roughly 40 of which are `solene-*`). Under the wave model described in CLAUDE.md — six agents with exclusive file sets working in parallel — a single 18k-line file that every schema change must touch is the guaranteed merge-conflict point and the one file that cannot be given to one agent exclusively. It is also the reason `npm run check` needs a 6 GB heap.
- **Fix:** Finish the split along domain lines (leads, properties, deals, finance, comms, ai, founder) with a barrel that preserves today's import paths, so it is mechanical and non-breaking. Do it as its own wave with no behaviour change, and lower the schema-related ratchets in the same commit. This is the highest-leverage refactor available because it directly unblocks the parallel-agent model the team depends on.
- **Comparable:** Prisma and Drizzle both encourage per-domain schema modules for exactly this reason; Linear's and Figma's schema layers are split by product area with a generated barrel. The mechanical, behaviour-free split is the standard first move.
- **Evidence:** `wc -l shared/schema.ts` (17,967); counted pgTables in the monolith (464) and in shared/schema/*.ts (260 across 85 files); read drizzle.config.ts's note that the old single-file glob "made drizzle-kit blind to every table in shared/schema/*.ts (74 files) — which is how 90+ tables shipped with no migration".

#### [low/L] Type-safety escape hatches are frozen at a level that makes `strict: true` largely nominal on the server

- **Where:** `scripts/ratchets/as-any.json (1,253), colon-any.json (2,714), tests-typecheck.json (162), openai-bypass.json (82), self-fallback.json (137), sql-raw.json (38); tsconfig.json`
- **Defect:** tsconfig.json sets `strict: true`, which is right — but there are 1,253 `as any` and 2,714 `: any` occurrences frozen in server/**, plus 162 test files that still do not type-check, 38 raw-SQL sites, and 82 direct-OpenAI bypasses of the model router. The as-any ratchet's own description names the stakes correctly ("on money and multi-tenant paths that's where a mispriced charge or a cross-org data leak hides") — and CLAUDE.md records an `as any` having widened `leads.organizationId`, a NOT NULL tenant key, to `number | undefined`. The strictness knobs that would catch the next tier (`noUncheckedIndexedAccess`, `exactOptionalPropertyTypes`) are off. All of these are down-only, which is correct, but at current velocity none reaches zero inside the two-year horizon.
- **Fix:** Do not attack the aggregate. Scope it: define the money/auth/tenant file set (server/services/creditPool.ts, billing/**, webhookHandlers.ts, middleware/permissions + roleScope, storage/*Repo.ts) and set a hard zero-`as any` rule on just that set, enforced as its own ratchet at 0. That is achievable in a wave and puts the guarantee where the ratchet description says the risk is.
- **Comparable:** Stripe's and Datadog's TS codebases run `noUncheckedIndexedAccess` and ban `any` outright on payment paths while tolerating it elsewhere — the scoped-zero pattern rather than the global-average pattern.
- **Evidence:** Read all six ratchet JSON files' baselines and the as-any description; read tsconfig.json's compilerOptions in full (strict: true, no noUncheckedIndexedAccess / exactOptionalPropertyTypes).

## Head of Product Design

**AcreOS has a genuinely elite token layer that its call sites do not honor — three parallel meaning-systems for color collapse into decoration, one named typographic level renders in two different typefaces across the five doors, and the product's most important object (the Pax approval card) reads at a glance as one undifferentiated amber block whose Approve button can be pushed off-screen by its own content.**


### Already at or above the bar — do not "fix" these

- EmptyState (client/src/components/empty-state.tsx:48-67) makes `cta` a REQUIRED TypeScript prop with a documented `_noOp` escape hatch. That is a design principle enforced by the compiler, not by a doc. It is the single best thing in this design system — do not soften it.
- The token layer (client/src/index.css:1-250, tailwind.config.ts) is better than most Series-B products: five themes × light/dark, a role-named translucency scale (--surface-chrome/haze/veil/sheer/scrim) with a lint behind it, a semantic z-index scale mirrored in three places (Tailwind, CSS vars, a runtime registry), and separate heat / sentiment / density ramps. Do not flatten these.
- The `acrToken` color-mix wrapper (tailwind.config.ts:12-19) with its comment documenting that `bg-acr-pos/10` "silently compiled to NOTHING" before it — that is a team that debugs its own build output. Keep it; it just needs one more rule (finding #2).
- Motion discipline: motion-tokens.ts is the single source, animations.ts refuses inline magic numbers, and index.css:75-91 handles reduced motion with manual-override-wins over the OS preference. That last nuance is correct and rare.
- The `pointer-coarse:` custom variant (tailwind.config.ts plugins) driving Button touch targets by POINTER TYPE rather than viewport width (button.tsx:36-49), with the comment explaining the 768px-iPad bug it fixed. Most teams get this wrong for years.
- The global focus ring at index.css:2107-2115 — 2px background offset plus a 4px primary halo on every button/a/input/[role=button]. Genuinely strong and applied system-wide; the weak `ring-1` in the Button variant never actually ships because this overrides it.
- Shape-matched skeletons in Finance and Deals (finance.tsx:442-447 avatar circle + two lines + badge; finance.tsx:1223-1227 per-column table cells with right-aligned widths) plus `announce`/`announceText` props on Skeleton for screen readers. This is the standard the rest of the app should be held to.
- Zero hardcoded hex colors on any of the five door pages or the Pax/Today components — the page-hex lint is working. 527 `text-caption`/`text-micro` call sites vs 116 ad-hoc pixel sizes means that migration is ~82% landed.
- Scoped ErrorBoundaries on the Pax door (pax.tsx:604-616) that degrade only the chat subtree rather than blanking the door, with the reasoning written down. Same for the AI-unavailable banner replacing what used to be a full-page dead-end EmptyState (pax.tsx:376-410).

### Benchmarked against

- Linear — one rigorously enforced row spec per list. Their inbox/issue rows never mix two padding scales or two title sizes in the same vertical stack; a list is one object repeated. AcreOS's Decision Queue interleaves a p-6 / 15px-title Card with a p-4 / 14px-title PaxAskCard in the same <ul>.
- Superhuman — the triage surface is keyboard-complete and never navigates you away mid-triage. Single-key verbs act on the focused item in place and focus advances to the next one. AcreOS has J/K/Enter but Enter on the highest-value row (a pending Pax ask) teleports you to /ai instead of approving.
- Stripe Dashboard — exactly one metric numeral spec (tabular figures, one size ramp, one face) so numbers across every page read as one system. AcreOS uses four different combinations of size/weight/face/figure-style for money, three of them inside a single 4-up row on Deals.
- Height / Linear agent inbox — an agent proposal card separates state by shape and structure, not only by a text string; pending, running and failed are visually distinct objects. AcreOS's PaxAskCard renders pending, deciding and failed with identical border, fill and icon.
- Attio — an explicit density scale (comfortable/compact) owned by the component, not by the call site. AcreOS's Card primitive defaults to p-6 and is overridden 574 times across seven different padding values.
- Notion — page identity is always the first thing on the page and always one component. AcreOS has two competing header systems (ui/page-header.tsx for 12 mostly-founder pages, a hand-rolled .acr-cc-hero class idiom for 16 others), and Finance renders its H1 below a chart.
- Raycast — one glyph per entity, everywhere. Pax is drawn as Sparkles, Bot, and Zap depending on which file you are in, and two nav registry entries share the label "Pax" and the href /ai with different icons.

### Findings


#### [high/M] Every semantic soft-pill pair fails WCAG AA contrast — warn text on warn fill is 2.5:1, in 139 places

- **Where:** `client/src/index.css:263-268 (token definitions); client/src/components/today/DecisionQueue.tsx:52-59 (sourcePillStyles)`
- **Defect:** The three semantic tint pairs are all below AA for small text on the default homestead light theme. Computed against --acr-bg #EDE3D0: text-acr-warn (#B97A1E) on bg-acr-warn-soft (rgba(185,122,30,0.14)) = 2.47:1; text-acr-pos (#5A7340) on pos-soft = 3.54:1; text-acr-brand (#A04428) on brand-soft = 4.07:1. AA requires 4.5:1. The exact pair `bg-acr-warn-soft text-acr-warn` appears at 139 call sites, almost always on 11-12px badge text — the smallest type in the product. The team clearly holds itself to AA: index.css:257 carries the comment "darkened 7A6A50 → 6B5C42 — was below WCAG-AA 4.5:1 for small body text". That audit covered the neutral ink ramp and never looked at the semantic pairs, which is exactly CLAUDE.md's third law (a gate proves its property only over the population it reads).
- **Fix:** Add a `--acr-{pos,warn,neg,brand}-ink` token per theme: a darkened variant of each semantic hue intended for TEXT ON ITS OWN TINT, computed to clear 4.5:1 against the tint blended over both --acr-bg and --acr-surface in every theme × mode. Change the pill idiom from `bg-acr-warn-soft text-acr-warn` to `bg-acr-warn-soft text-acr-warn-ink`. Then add a contrast ratchet under scripts/ that enumerates every authored (bg-acr-X-soft, text-acr-Y) pairing found in client/src/**/*.tsx, blends the tint over each theme's --acr-bg and --acr-surface, and fails below 4.5:1 — put the ENUMERATION in the test so a new pairing that is not in the list is what breaks it.
- **Comparable:** Stripe Dashboard and Linear both ship a separate darker on-tint text token rather than reusing the fill hue as the label color. Stripe's status badges hold AA at 12px; Linear's label chips use a darkened foreground against the tint, never the tint's own hue.
- **Evidence:** Read index.css:244-275 for exact token values (--acr-bg #EDE3D0, --acr-surface #F7F0DD, --acr-warn #B97A1E, --acr-warn-soft rgba(185,122,30,0.14), --acr-pos #5A7340, --acr-brand #A04428). Contrast ratios computed by hand from the WCAG relative-luminance formula with the tint alpha-composited over both surfaces. Counted 139 co-occurring `bg-acr-warn-soft` + `text-acr-warn` sites by grep. Confirmed by grep that the only existing contrast-related artifact in the repo is tests/personas/fixMutedContrast.mjs — there is no contrast gate over the semantic pairs.

#### [high/S] 20 border classes compile to nothing — proven against the built stylesheet

- **Where:** `client/src/pages/today.tsx:574, 640, 689; client/src/pages/market-watchlist.tsx:103-104; client/src/pages/agent-detail.tsx:239-242; 20 sites total`
- **Defect:** `className="border-[color:var(--acr-brand)]/30"` produces NO CSS rule. Tailwind cannot inject alpha into a raw `var()` in an arbitrary value, so it emits the un-suffixed class only and silently drops the `/30` variant entirely. The result on Today: the "Welcome back" card (today.tsx:640) and the "Heading out?" card (today.tsx:689) have no border-color override at all and fall back to the Card primitive's default border; the "Review now" urgency pill (today.tsx:574) likewise has no red edge. This is the identical failure the tailwind config's own header comment documents for the token form ("before this wrapper, `bg-acr-pos/10` silently compiled to NOTHING") — `acrToken` fixed `border-acr-brand/30` and nothing fixed the arbitrary-value spelling, and no lint covers it.
- **Fix:** Replace all 20 with the token form the acrToken wrapper already supports: `border-acr-brand/30`, `border-acr-neg/30`, etc. (the built CSS proves these work — see evidence). Then extend scripts/lint-page-hex.mjs or add a sibling lint that fails on the regex `\[(color|border-color):var\(--acr-[a-z-]+\)\]/\d`, since this spelling can never carry an opacity modifier.
- **Evidence:** Verified directly against the shipped bundle. `tr '}' '\n' < dist/public/assets/index-DMOc5MPd.css | grep '^\.' | grep 'var(--acr'` returns 30 arbitrary-value selectors and ZERO of them carry a `/NN` suffix. The token form is present and correct: `.border-acr-brand\/30{border-color:color-mix(in srgb,var(--acr-brand) 30%,transparent)}`. The arbitrary form emits without alpha: `.bg-\[color\:var\(--acr-brand\)\]{background-color:var(--acr-brand)}`. No `.border-\[color\:var\(--acr-brand\)\]\/30` rule exists in the file.

#### [high/M] Today buries the Decision Queue under seven possible blocks — the door for "one thing to do" is a notification stack

- **Where:** `client/src/pages/today.tsx:551-855 (15 top-level sections; the queue is section 10)`
- **Defect:** Today renders, in source order: hero, first-run empty state, GettingStartedChecklist, "Welcome back", query-error, "Heading out?", MorningBrief, ReceiptsStrip, persona lede — and only THEN the Decision Queue (today.tsx:759). Six of the preceding blocks are conditional self-promoting cards, three of them (welcome-back, heading-out, referral nudge) are dismissible brand-tinted nudges with their own localStorage keys, and the GettingStartedChecklist is mounted unconditionally by design (today.tsx:630-637). A returning operator on a Tuesday morning in week two can plausibly render five of them at once, pushing the queue — the reason the door exists — below the fold on a laptop. Each card is individually well-reasoned; together they make the highest-intent surface in the product read as an inbox of the product talking about itself.
- **Fix:** Invert the composition: the Decision Queue moves directly under the hero, and everything currently above it that is not a decision becomes either (a) a single collapsed "what changed" strip above the queue that merges MorningBrief + Receipts into one line, or (b) content that appears BELOW the queue. Cap concurrent nudges at one — add a small priority arbiter (welcome-back > heading-out > referral) so at most one dismissible card can ever render per session. Keep the first-run empty state where it is; it is the one case where there is no queue to lead with.
- **Comparable:** Superhuman's Split Inbox and Linear's My Issues put the work first and unconditionally — announcements live in a separate, opt-in changelog surface, never interleaved above the list. Height does the same with its agent inbox.
- **Evidence:** Read today.tsx:548-855 in full; enumerated the 15 `{/* ── */}` section markers by grep and traced each render guard. Confirmed the queue mounts at line 759, after MorningBrief (730), ReceiptsStrip (735) and the persona lede (743).

#### [high/S] PaxAskCard renders pending, deciding, and failed as pixel-identical objects

- **Where:** `client/src/components/pax/PaxAskCard.tsx:186-197`
- **Defect:** The container's className ternary has three arms: executed → pos, {rejected|expired|revised} → muted, else → `border-acr-warn/40 bg-acr-warn-soft/40`. "Else" is pending, deciding AND failed. StatusIcon (line 186) is the same AlertCircle for all three and the same text-acr-warn tint. So an ask that Pax tried to execute and FAILED looks exactly like one that has simply not been tapped yet — identical border, fill, icon and icon color. The only difference is a 12px status string. On the approval surface, "this needs you" and "this broke" are the two states that must never be confused. The card also means a queue of six pending asks is a solid wall of amber, which spends the warn semantic at rest and leaves nothing louder for the state that actually warrants alarm.
- **Fix:** Give pending a NEUTRAL card (border-border, bg-card) with a single amber left-edge accent or leading dot — the way Linear marks an unread issue — so warn stays available for real trouble. Give `failed` its own arm: `border-acr-neg/40 bg-acr-neg-soft/40` with an XCircle icon. Give `deciding` a visible in-flight treatment (a spinner in the status line and a subtle reduced-opacity body), not just disabled buttons.
- **Comparable:** Height and Linear's agent inbox give a failed agent action a distinct destructive treatment and a retry affordance; a queued proposal is a neutral row with one accent element, not a fully tinted card.
- **Evidence:** Read PaxAskCard.tsx:186-197 and the outcomeLine switch at :103-120. Traced the `failed` assignment at :156 (`setDecision({ status: "failed" })`) and confirmed `showButtons` at :185 keeps failed in the same branch as pending.

#### [high/S] An approval card's own content can push its Approve button off screen

- **Where:** `client/src/components/pax/PaxAskCard.tsx:280-289 (blockquote); hosted non-compact at client/src/components/today/DecisionQueue.tsx:427-432`
- **Defect:** The frozen-text blockquote caps its height only in compact mode: `compact && "max-h-40 overflow-y-auto"`. DecisionQueue mounts PaxAskCard WITHOUT the `compact` prop, so on Today a drafted email body renders at full length with no truncation. A 400-word draft produces a card several screens tall whose Approve / Reject / Edit row (line 341) sits below all of it — and with several asks in the queue, the second ask's controls are effectively unreachable without deliberate scrolling. The card's entire job is one decision at a glance; here the decision is the last thing you can reach.
- **Fix:** Move the clamp out of the compact branch and apply it always: `max-h-40 overflow-y-auto` on the blockquote in every host, with a "Show full text" toggle beneath it when the content overflows (measure with a ResizeObserver or a simple scrollHeight check). Better still, move the action row ABOVE the body, or make it sticky to the bottom of the card, so the verb + recipient + buttons are one unbroken unit regardless of body length.
- **Comparable:** Superhuman and Gmail both truncate a quoted body to a fixed height with an expander and keep the action bar pinned. Height's approval cards cap the proposal body and expand on demand.
- **Evidence:** Read PaxAskCard.tsx:280-289 (the `compact &&` clamp) and DecisionQueue.tsx:427-432 (the mount, which passes only ask/onApprove/onReject/onRevise — no compact).

#### [high/M] The metric numeral has no single spec — four combinations across the doors, three inside one row on Deals

- **Where:** `client/src/components/today/CashStrip.tsx:88; client/src/pages/deals.tsx:549, 585; client/src/pages/finance.tsx:353, 1012; client/src/pages/maps.tsx:402, 627`
- **Defect:** The same class of figure is typeset four different ways. Today/CashStrip:88 — `text-lg font-semibold tabular-nums` (18px, 600, sans). Deals:549 — `text-xl md:text-2xl font-bold` (20→24px, 700, sans, NO tabular-nums, so counts jitter as they change). Deals:585 — `text-lg md:text-2xl font-bold font-mono tabular-nums`. Finance:353 — `text-2xl font-bold font-mono tabular-nums`. Finance:1012 — `text-2xl font-bold tabular-nums` with NO mono, sitting in the same drawer grid as :996 which IS mono, so "Current balance" and "Interest rate" are set in different typefaces one row apart. The Deals 4-up KPI row is the worst case: at <768px "Acquisitions: 12" is 20px Inter proportional while "Pipeline: $1.2M" beside it is 18px JetBrains Mono tabular. Numbers ARE this product; four numeral systems make the whole app read as several apps.
- **Fix:** Add one component — `<Metric value size="lg|md|sm" />` — or at minimum three composed utility classes (`.metric-xl`, `.metric-lg`, `.metric-sm`) in the TYPOGRAPHY HIERARCHY block of index.css next to `.acr-cc-greeting`, each locking size + weight + face + `font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums`. Decide once whether money is mono (recommended: yes, for column alignment) and apply it to every currency figure including Finance:1012 and Deals:549. Then ratchet: fail on any `text-(lg|xl|2xl|3xl)` combined with `font-(bold|semibold)` in a file under pages/ that is not one of the metric classes.
- **Comparable:** Stripe Dashboard has exactly one numeral treatment (tabular, one face) applied by component across every surface. Linear's numbers are one spec. Attio's number cells are one spec.
- **Evidence:** Read all seven sites listed. Confirmed deals.tsx:549 and :567 lack tabular-nums entirely while :585 and :605 have it; confirmed finance.tsx:996/:1004 use font-mono and :1012/:1018 in the same grid do not.

#### [high/M] Two page-header systems — the Map door's H1 renders at section grade and /settings/pax's H1 in the wrong typeface

- **Where:** `client/src/pages/maps.tsx:1215; client/src/pages/settings/pax-controls.tsx:355; contrast with client/src/pages/pax.tsx:564-576 and client/src/index.css:1533-1540`
- **Defect:** The named hero level has two spellings that are NOT equivalent. `.acr-cc-greeting` (index.css:1533) sets `font: 600 32px/1.15 var(--font-display)` + `letter-spacing: -0.03em` + color + max-width; --font-display in the default theme is FRAUNCES, a serif (fonts.css:118). The Tailwind alias `text-hero` sets only size, line-height and weight — the config comment at tailwind.config.ts says so explicitly: "Font family + letter-spacing MUST be set separately". Eight pages use bare `text-hero`, so their H1 renders 32px INTER at default tracking — a serif/sans mismatch on page titles within one brand. One of them is the brand-new wave-1 surface, pax-controls.tsx:355. Worse, pax.tsx:564-567 carries a comment explicitly warning about this exact trap ("`acr-cc-greeting` carries the Fraunces 600 / −0.03em identity that a raw `text-hero` would lose") — the lesson was learned, written down, and then re-broken. Separately, the Map door (maps.tsx:1215) uses `text-section-h2` as its `<h1>`: 18px/500 Inter, two levels below every other door and indistinguishable from an in-page section head. Map is the only one of the five doors with no page identity and no eyebrow. Same split at the section level: 22 `.acr-section-h2` occurrences vs 32 bare `text-section-h2`.
- **Fix:** Extract one `<DoorHeader eyebrow title soft actions />` component (there are already two competing systems — ui/page-header.tsx used by 12 mostly-founder pages, and the hand-rolled .acr-cc-hero markup in 16 files) and make it the ONLY way an H1 is rendered. Give the Map door a real hero above the sticky control bar, or accept a canvas-first exception deliberately and document it. Then delete the bare `text-hero` / `text-section-h2` Tailwind aliases outright — they exist only to be misused, since they cannot carry the identity — and lint for `<h1` without the component.
- **Comparable:** Notion, Linear and Stripe each render page identity through exactly one component; there is no way to author a page title that is off-spec.
- **Evidence:** Read index.css:1512-1600 (the TYPOGRAPHY HIERARCHY block), tailwind.config.ts fontSize.hero/section-h2 and its own warning comment, fonts.css:118-119 (--font-display: Fraunces vs --font-sans: Inter), pax.tsx:564-576, maps.tsx:1213-1220, pax-controls.tsx:355. Counted the split by grep: 8 bare `text-hero` H1s, 32 bare `text-section-h2`.

#### [medium/S] Ghost, outline, secondary and destructive buttons have no hover state at all

- **Where:** `client/src/components/ui/button.tsx:13-22`
- **Defect:** Only `default` (`hover:brightness-105 hover:-translate-y-px`) and `glass` define any hover. `ghost` is literally `"border border-transparent"`; `outline` is border + shadow-xs; `secondary` and `destructive` are fill + border. None gets a hover background, brightness or color change. shadcn's stock ghost is `hover:bg-accent hover:text-accent-foreground`; that was removed here and the `.hover-elevate` utility (index.css:1692-1702) is opt-in and not applied by the primitive. This affects 413 ghost, 946 outline and 253 secondary call sites — including the most-used tertiary affordances on Today: "Clear queue", "View all", "Restore N" (DecisionQueue.tsx:341-368) and PaxAskCard's "Edit" (:365). In a dense list a ghost button with no hover fill is indistinguishable from static text until you click it; hover is the primary cue that something is interactive on desktop.
- **Fix:** Add hover arms to the variants inside buttonVariants: ghost → `hover:bg-foreground/5` (reuse the .hover-elevate value so it stays one language), outline → `hover:bg-foreground/[0.03] hover:border-foreground/20`, secondary → `hover:brightness-[0.97]`, destructive → `hover:brightness-105`. All four are already behind Tailwind's `hoverOnlyWhenSupported` flag so touch is unaffected.
- **Comparable:** Linear, Raycast, Notion and Stripe all give tertiary/ghost controls an unmistakable hover fill — it is how a dense toolbar communicates affordance at all.
- **Evidence:** Read button.tsx:7-56 in full. Grepped index.css for any global `button:hover` or ghost rule — none exists; the only hits are `.hover-elevate` (opt-in class, index.css:1692) and the focus-visible rule at :2107. Counted variant usage: 413 / 946 / 253.

#### [medium/M] No density scale — the Card primitive's padding is decided by the call site 574 times, and it shows inside one list

- **Where:** `client/src/components/ui/card.tsx:68 (CardContent default p-6); client/src/components/today/DecisionQueue.tsx:495 vs client/src/components/pax/PaxAskCard.tsx:197`
- **Defect:** CardContent defaults to p-6, and call sites override it with p-4 (281×), p-3 (86×), p-0 (54×), p-5 (25×), p-2 (14×), p-8 (13×) — seven paddings for one primitive, with the default only the third most common. The visible consequence is in the Decision Queue: a regular row is `CardContent ... p-6` with a `text-[15px] font-medium` title (DecisionQueue.tsx:495, 508), and the ask row immediately above or below it is a PaxAskCard at `p-4` with a `text-sm` (14px) verb and a full colored border plus tinted fill instead of a 2px left priority edge. Two objects, one list, different spacing rhythm, different title size, different border language. There is no spacing rhythm to violate because none is defined; `text-[15px]` is itself off-scale (the fontSize scale defines caption/micro/hero/section-h2 and nothing at 15px).
- **Fix:** Add a `density` variant to Card (`comfortable` = p-6, `compact` = p-4, `dense` = p-3) and make the padding non-overridable from the call site; migrate the 574 overrides to the three values. Then make the Decision Queue one object: either PaxAskCard adopts the row chrome (2px left priority border, p-6, 15px title) or the regular rows adopt the ask card's. Pick one and delete `text-[15px]` in favor of a real scale step.
- **Comparable:** Attio ships an explicit density control with a defined scale. Linear has two row densities, both owned by the component. Neither lets a page author pick a padding.
- **Evidence:** Read card.tsx:25-80 for the defaults. Counted padding overrides by grep on `CardContent className="...p-N`: 281 p-4, 94 p-6, 86 p-3, 54 p-0, 25 p-5, 14 p-2, 13 p-8. Read DecisionQueue.tsx:490-520 and PaxAskCard.tsx:189-198 side by side.

#### [medium/S] The Decision Queue skeleton is roughly half the height of the row it stands in for

- **Where:** `client/src/components/today/DecisionQueue.tsx:374-378; client/src/components/today/CashStrip.tsx:55-61`
- **Defect:** The queue's loading state is three `<Skeleton className="h-16 w-full" />` blocks (64px each). The real row is a Card with `CardContent ... p-6` (48px of vertical padding alone) plus a badge row, a 15px title, a 12px description and an optional inline-resolve button row — realistically 130-160px — and a pax-ask row is taller still. So every Today load produces a visible content jump as 64px placeholders are replaced by ~150px cards. CashStrip is worse: a single `<Skeleton className="h-24 w-full" />` stands in for a three-column KPI card with icons, labels, values and sparklines. CLAUDE.md is explicit: "Use Skeleton components matching the content shape". Repo-wide there are 316 raw `h-N w-full` blocks against only 6 files importing the shaped SkeletonCard/List/Table components — the shaped primitives exist and are almost unused.
- **Fix:** Replace the queue skeleton with a shaped one: three ghost cards at the real row height with a small badge pill, a title bar at ~60% width and a description bar at ~85%. Replace CashStrip's block with a three-column grid of icon-dot + label bar + value bar + sparkline bar. Finance and Deals already do this correctly (finance.tsx:442-447, 1223-1227) — lift that pattern. Then measure: a CLS check on Today in the existing Playwright config would catch regressions.
- **Comparable:** Linear and Stripe both ship skeletons that are geometrically identical to the loaded row, so there is zero layout shift at hydration.
- **Evidence:** Read DecisionQueue.tsx:372-380 and CashStrip.tsx:55-61, then the real row markup at DecisionQueue.tsx:490-520 and the real CashStrip grid at :75-140. Counted 316 raw `<Skeleton className="h-N w-full` occurrences vs 6 files importing SkeletonCard/SkeletonList/SkeletonTable.

#### [medium/S] Keyboard Enter on a pending Pax ask navigates away instead of approving it

- **Where:** `client/src/pages/today.tsx:206-217 (actionUrl: "/ai"); client/src/components/today/DecisionQueue.tsx:262-268`
- **Defect:** The J/K/Enter layer is good work, but its Enter verb is `setLocation(item.actionUrl)`, and pax-ask rows are constructed with `actionUrl: "/ai"` and `inlineAction: { kind: "navigate" }`. So the sequence a power user will actually perform — land on Today, press J to reach the top item (asks are ranked first, rank -1), press Enter — teleports them to the Pax door instead of approving the thing the card was built to approve in place. They lose their position in the queue and have to answer it somewhere else. The single highest-leverage row on the page is the one row where the keyboard layer does the wrong thing. There are also no per-item action keys at all: no approve, no reject, no snooze, no done.
- **Fix:** Make `onOpen` dispatch on the row: for a `pax-ask` row, move DOM focus to that card's Approve button rather than navigating. Then add the verb keys Superhuman/Height users expect and show them in a `?` sheet: `a`/`⌘⏎` approve focused ask, `r` reject, `e` edit, `s` snooze, `d` done, `Esc` clear focus — all reusing the existing suppression rules in use-keyboard-layer.ts (editable target, modifiers, open dialog). After a decision, advance focus to the next row instead of dropping it to body.
- **Comparable:** Superhuman: the focused item is acted on in place by a single key and focus advances automatically; you never leave the triage surface. Height and Linear both bind ⌘⏎ to approve on an agent proposal.
- **Evidence:** Read today.tsx:199-218 (the paxAskItems map, actionUrl "/ai") and DecisionQueue.tsx:260-278 (useKeyboardLayer onOpen → setLocation). Read hooks/use-keyboard-layer.ts:1-60 — it implements J/K/Enter/Escape only, with no per-item verbs. Grepped components/today/ and components/pax/ for onKeyDown/useHotkeys: zero hits.

#### [medium/S] Four Decision Queue sources render three different colors and one identical label, "Pax"

- **Where:** `client/src/components/today/DecisionQueue.tsx:52-84`
- **Defect:** `sourcePillLabel` maps pax-priority, pax-suggests, pax-noticed and pax-ask all to the string "Pax". `sourcePillStyles` gives those same four sources three different colors — brand, POS-green, brand, WARN-amber. So the user sees a green "Pax" pill and an amber "Pax" pill in the same list with no way to learn what the difference means, and `sourceIcon` compounds it by drawing three of them with Sparkles and one with Zap. Meanwhile amber is shared between `pax-ask` (a routine approval request) and `portfolio-alert` (something is wrong), so the routine case and the alarm case are the same color. And priority is encoded on a SECOND, overlapping color channel — the 2px left border (`priorityBorderColor`, :80-84) — whose "low" value is `var(--acr-brand-soft)`, a 14%-alpha tint, while "medium" and "high" are full-opacity warn and neg. So low-priority rows have an effectively invisible edge while the other two are saturated, and a row can be amber-pill + red-edge. Semantic color is being spent as decoration and the label refuses to carry the distinction the color is trying to make.
- **Fix:** Collapse to one channel. Either the pill carries provenance in NEUTRAL chrome (one Pax glyph, one muted style, four honest labels — "Pax priority" / "Pax suggests" / "Pax noticed" / "Needs your tap") and the left edge alone carries urgency; or the pill carries urgency and provenance moves to a subtitle. Reserve amber for one meaning. Make the priority ramp consistent: use `acr-brand` (not `brand-soft`) for low so all three steps are the same saturation, or drop the low edge to `transparent` deliberately.
- **Comparable:** Linear encodes exactly one thing per color channel: priority is an icon glyph, status is a colored dot, labels are neutral chips. Nothing carries two overlapping color meanings.
- **Evidence:** Read DecisionQueue.tsx:52-84 in full. Confirmed --acr-brand-soft is rgba(160,68,40,0.14) at index.css:261 while --acr-warn (#B97A1E) and --acr-neg (#8E2B16) at :265/:267 are full-opacity hex.

#### [medium/S] Semantic color is used decoratively on neutral metrics — four KPI tiles, four color families, in one row

- **Where:** `client/src/pages/deals.tsx:541, 559, 575, 596`
- **Defect:** The Deals 4-up KPI row assigns icon-chip colors by aesthetics, not meaning: Acquisitions → `bg-acr-brand-soft`/`text-acr-brand`, Dispositions → `bg-acr-pos/10`/`text-acr-pos`, Pipeline → `bg-primary/10`/`text-primary`, Closed → `bg-acr-pos/10`/`text-acr-pos`. `--acr-pos` is the outcome-positive token but "Dispositions" is a neutral count, so green is spent on a metric with no valence — and then reused for "Closed", so two unrelated tiles share it. `primary` and `acr-brand` are used interchangeably for two different tiles even though in the homestead theme they are the same terracotta family, so the row mixes two token systems for one hue. index.css goes to real trouble to separate three meaning-layers (heat = activity intensity, pos/warn/neg = outcome sentiment, brand = identity) and documents each; the call sites ignore the separation.
- **Fix:** Make the KPI chip neutral by default (`bg-muted text-muted-foreground`) and allow a semantic tint ONLY where the number has a valence — e.g. green on "Closed value" because closing is the good outcome, nothing on "Acquisitions" or "Dispositions" because they are counts. Pick one of `primary` / `acr-brand` as the brand hue in customer surfaces and retire the other from page code. Add a short rule to the index.css color block: "a semantic token on a metric asserts that the metric has a good direction; if it does not, use muted."
- **Comparable:** Stripe Dashboard keeps metric chrome neutral and reserves green/red strictly for deltas and states with a direction. Linear does the same — color means something specific or it is absent.
- **Evidence:** Read deals.tsx:536-610 (all four tiles). Read the index.css heat/sentiment/brand token commentary at :159-200 and the homestead block at :244-275 confirming --acr-brand #A04428 and the semantic trio.

#### [medium/S] Pax is drawn with three different glyphs, and two nav entries share the label "Pax" and the href /ai with different icons

- **Where:** `client/src/lib/nav-items.ts:96 and :105; client/src/components/today/DecisionQueue.tsx:69-76; client/src/components/pax/pax-overflow-menu.tsx`
- **Defect:** nav-items.ts:96 defines `ai-hub` → label "Pax", icon Sparkles, href /ai. nav-items.ts:105 defines `ai-hub-chat` → label "Pax", icon Bot, href /ai. Two registry entries, same name, same destination, different marks. The command palette dedupes them by path (command-palette.tsx:236-250), so which icon a user sees in ⌘K is decided by array order rather than by design. Elsewhere DecisionQueue draws Pax as Zap for pax-priority and Sparkles for the other three Pax sources, and pax-overflow-menu uses both Zap and Bot. An assistant that is meant to be ambient fabric behind five doors needs exactly one recognizable mark; right now it has three, and the user cannot learn it.
- **Fix:** Pick one glyph for Pax (Sparkles is the strongest candidate — it is already the door icon and the most-used), export it as a single `PaxMark` from a shared module, and replace every Bot/Zap that stands for Pax with it. Delete the duplicate `ai-hub-chat` entry or give it a distinct label; two entries with the same label and href is a registry bug regardless of the icon.
- **Comparable:** Raycast, Linear and Superhuman each have one agent/AI mark used identically in nav, in cards and in menus.
- **Evidence:** Read nav-items.ts:93-110 and :138-154 (DEFAULT_SIDEBAR_ITEMS / MOBILE_DOORS). Read command-palette.tsx:236-250 including its own comment acknowledging "ALL_NAV_ITEMS contains multiple ids pointing at the same href (e.g. `ai-hub` and `ai-hub-chat` both → /ai)". Read DecisionQueue.tsx:69-76.

#### [medium/M] Responsive typography is inverted and ad-hoc — text and weight change at the md breakpoint, contradicting the hero's own scale-down

- **Where:** `client/src/pages/today.tsx:596, 645, 648, 693; client/src/index.css:1590-1598`
- **Defect:** Today's cards scale type and weight DOWN at md: `text-xl md:text-lg font-bold md:font-semibold` (empty-state h2, :596), `font-semibold text-base md:text-sm` (welcome-back h3, :645), `text-sm md:text-xs` (:648), `font-semibold text-sm md:text-[13px]` (heading-out h3, :693) — plus `p-6 md:p-5`, `p-4 md:p-3`, `gap-3 md:gap-2.5`, `w-12 h-12 md:w-10 md:h-10`. Meanwhile the design system scales the hero the OTHER way: index.css:1593 shrinks `.acr-cc-greeting` to 26px at ≤720px. So on a narrow viewport the hero gets smaller while the cards below it get bigger, and crossing 768px changes a heading's font WEIGHT (bold → semibold), which is a change of voice, not of density. `md:text-[13px]` also introduces an off-scale size that exists only at desktop. There are 51 `md:text-{xs,sm,base,lg}` downshifts across the client.
- **Fix:** Choose one density philosophy and express it as tokens rather than per-element `md:` pairs. If touch density is the goal (a defensible choice), define `--density-step` and two body classes (`.body-touch` / `.body-dense`) switched by `@media (pointer: coarse)` — matching the pointer-type discipline already used correctly for Button sizes — rather than by viewport width. Never vary font-weight across a breakpoint. Delete `md:text-[13px]`.
- **Comparable:** Linear and Stripe hold one type scale across breakpoints and vary only layout and spacing; neither changes a heading's weight responsively.
- **Evidence:** Read today.tsx:588-710 covering all four cards. Read index.css:1588-1600 (the ≤720px hero override). Counted 51 `md:text-(xs|sm|base|lg|[13px])` occurrences by grep.

#### [medium/S] Approving an ask announces nothing and destroys keyboard focus

- **Where:** `client/src/components/pax/PaxAskCard.tsx:141-160, 199-215, 341-378`
- **Defect:** When a user taps Approve, `run()` sets status to "deciding" then "executed"; the status text at :207-211 changes from "Waiting for your tap" to "Working…" to "Approved and sent", and `showButtons` goes false so the entire button row unmounts. There is no `aria-live` anywhere on the card (grep across components/today/ and components/pax/ finds live regions only in pax-overflow-menu.tsx:52-53 and pax-recourse-panel.tsx:270). So a screen-reader user gets no announcement that the most consequential action in the product succeeded — and because the focused Approve button is removed from the DOM, keyboard focus falls back to <body> and the user loses their place in the queue entirely. The card is otherwise carefully labelled (role="group", descriptive aria-labels on every button), which makes the omission stand out.
- **Fix:** Wrap the status line (:206-212) in `aria-live="polite"` and give `decision.note` (:335) `role="status"`; a failure should be `role="alert"`. On a settled decision, move focus deliberately — to the card container (make it `tabIndex={-1}` and focus it) or, better, to the next pending ask in the queue, which is also what the keyboard fix in the Enter finding wants.
- **Comparable:** Linear and Superhuman both announce the result of a destructive/irreversible action and move focus to the next item so keyboard flow is never interrupted.
- **Evidence:** Read PaxAskCard.tsx:141-215 and :341-378. Grepped `aria-live|role="status"|role="alert"` across client/src/components/today/ and client/src/components/pax/ — the only hits are pax-overflow-menu.tsx:52-53 and pax-recourse-panel.tsx:270.

#### [medium/S] The record-change diff shows raw field keys and JSON to the customer

- **Where:** `client/src/components/pax/PaxAskCard.tsx:293-313`
- **Defect:** When an ask is a record write rather than a message, the before → after diff renders `<dt>{key.replace(/_/g, " ")}</dt>` — which turns snake_case into spaced words but leaves camelCase completely untouched, so a field like `sellerFinancingApr` is presented to the operator verbatim. And any non-primitive value falls through to `JSON.stringify(value)` at :309, so an object or array is dumped as raw JSON inside the card. This is the one branch of the approval card the user must read most carefully — they are being asked to authorize a change to their own data — and it is the branch that speaks in database vocabulary. The rest of the component is exemplary about server-owned, human-formatted copy (the file header states "The client formats nothing about the ask itself"); this block quietly breaks that contract by formatting keys on the client.
- **Fix:** Move field labelling to the server alongside the rest of the ask summary (server/services/paxAskSummary.ts already owns verb/to/from/why/originPhrase) — return `change.fields: [{ label, before, after }]` with human labels and pre-formatted values, and have the card render them verbatim like every other line. If a value is genuinely structured, the server should render the human sentence, not hand the client JSON.
- **Comparable:** Stripe Dashboard's event/diff views show human field labels with formatted values and hide the raw payload behind an explicit "view raw" toggle.
- **Evidence:** Read PaxAskCard.tsx:291-313 and the file header comment at :12-17 stating that every line is server truth and the client formats nothing.

#### [medium/S] ⌘K still advertises "Automation" — a deleted surface that silently redirects to Workflows

- **Where:** `client/src/lib/nav-items.ts:103; client/src/App.tsx:969-971; client/src/components/command-palette.tsx:240-250`
- **Defect:** nav-items.ts:103 lists `{ id: "automation", label: "Automation", href: "/automation", description: "Automated rules and triggers" }`. App.tsx:969 makes /automation a `<Redirect to="/workflows" />` — the page was deleted in the "Nothing lies" wave because it had no execution engine. The command palette builds its Pages list from ALL_NAV_ITEMS (command-palette.tsx:240) and filters only FROZEN_ROUTES; /automation is not in that list (shared/feature-freeze.ts:30-35), so "Automation — Automated rules and triggers" is still a first-class ⌘K result that lands the user on a differently-titled page with no explanation. The palette otherwise handles this correctly for frozen routes and even documents why (command-palette.tsx:244-248) — this one slipped the population.
- **Fix:** Delete the `automation` entry from ALL_NAV_ITEMS (the redirect keeps old bookmarks working), or, if muscle memory matters, keep the redirect and add "Automation" as an ALIAS on the Workflows palette entry so typing it lands on a page whose title matches what the user searched for. Extend the palette filter to exclude any href that App.tsx resolves to a `<Redirect>`, so the next deletion cannot leave a stale palette entry.
- **Comparable:** Raycast and Linear both keep the command palette's index derived from live destinations, so a removed surface disappears from search in the same change that removes it.
- **Evidence:** Read nav-items.ts:103, App.tsx:965-975 (the Redirect and the deletion comment above it), command-palette.tsx:236-252, and shared/feature-freeze.ts:30-38 (FROZEN_ROUTES contains only /marketplace, /capital-markets, /vision-ai, /negotiation).

#### [low/S] 8px and 9px type ships on the Map door and in the Land Credit badge

- **Where:** `client/src/pages/maps.tsx:405, 582, 927; client/src/components/land-credit-badge.tsx:158, 174, 188; 23 sites total`
- **Defect:** There are 20 `text-[9px]` and 3 `text-[8px]` call sites. The Map door uses 9px for the parcel-type badge (:405), the "Absentee" owner badge (:582) and a status indicator (:927); the Land Credit badge renders its letter grade at 8px (:174). Below ~10px, letterforms in Inter lose their counters at typical device pixel ratios and become shape-recognition rather than reading — and these are labels a user is meant to actually read. The design system deliberately created `text-caption` (11px) and `text-micro` (10px) tokens precisely to end ad-hoc sizes (tailwind.config.ts fontSize comment: "Replace the prior `text-[11px]` / `text-[10px]` ad-hocs"), and that migration is 527 sites landed — but it stopped at 10px and left 23 sites below the floor.
- **Fix:** Set 10px (`text-micro`) as the hard floor. Raise all 23 sites to `text-micro`, and where that no longer fits, the answer is fewer words or a tooltip, not smaller type — e.g. the Land Credit grade at 8px should be an icon-scale change, not a font-size change. Add the sub-10px pattern to the lint that already governs ad-hoc pixel sizes so the floor is enforced rather than remembered.
- **Comparable:** Linear's smallest UI type is 11px; Stripe's is 12px; Apple HIG floors secondary labels at 11pt. None of them ship 8px reading text.
- **Evidence:** Grepped `text-\[9px\]|text-\[8px\]` across client/src: 23 hits, listed above. Read tailwind.config.ts fontSize (caption 11px, micro 10px) and its comment. Confirmed 527 text-caption/text-micro adoptions vs 116 remaining ad-hoc pixel sizes.

#### [low/S] A dead Tailwind class leaves one Map stat pill uncolored next to its two colored siblings

- **Where:** `client/src/pages/maps.tsx:1239`
- **Defect:** The "Owned" PortfolioStatPill is passed `color="border-sage-200 text-sage-700 dark:border-sage-800"`. There is no `sage` palette in tailwind.config.ts (the colors block defines background/foreground/border/input/card/popover/primary/secondary/muted/accent/destructive/ring/chart/sidebar/status/acr and nothing else) and no `--sage-*` in index.css. So all three classes compile to nothing and the pill renders with whatever border and text color it inherits, sitting directly beside "Active" (`border-acr-accent text-acr-accent`) and "Closed" (`border-acr-pos-soft text-acr-pos`) which are correctly tokenized. It is the only `sage-` usage left in the entire client.
- **Fix:** Change to `border-acr-brand/40 text-acr-brand` (or whichever token expresses "owned"), matching the two siblings. This is exactly the failure class the `acrToken` wrapper was written for — worth adding an unknown-color-class check to the existing lint family so a palette that does not exist fails the build rather than rendering invisibly.
- **Evidence:** Grepped `\b(bg|text|border|ring|from|to)-sage-[0-9]` across client/src — exactly one hit, maps.tsx:1239. Grepped tailwind.config.ts and index.css for `sage` — zero hits. Read maps.tsx:1222-1245 for the two correctly-tokenized sibling pills.

#### [low/S] Three design-system components have zero importers

- **Where:** `client/src/components/ui/mesh-gradient.tsx, client/src/components/ui/animated-list.tsx, client/src/components/ui/filters-chip.tsx`
- **Defect:** mesh-gradient, animated-list and filters-chip are exported from the ui layer and imported nowhere in the client; conversion-funnel and vertical-badge have one importer each. Unused primitives in a design system are worse than absent ones: they show up in autocomplete and in any future component inventory, and a new engineer will reasonably assume `filters-chip` is the sanctioned way to render a filter chip when the doors clearly do something else. This is the design-system equivalent of the "built but unwired" defect CLAUDE.md calls the codebase's most common failure.
- **Fix:** Delete the three zero-importer files. For conversion-funnel and vertical-badge, either inline them at their single call site or keep them and note in the file header that they are single-consumer — the same convention empty-state.tsx already uses to explain its archetypes.
- **Comparable:** Linear and Stripe both treat their component library as a closed set — an unused primitive is deleted, not parked, so autocomplete is always a list of sanctioned choices.
- **Evidence:** Grepped for `ui/<name>` importers across client/src/**/*.tsx: page-header 12, status-dot 3, deal-journey 2, conversion-funnel 1, vertical-badge 1, mesh-gradient 0, animated-list 0, filters-chip 0.

## Principal Frontend Engineer — client/src implementation quality

**The client has genuinely elite infrastructure (queryClient, manualChunks, PaxAskCard, the ratchet design) sitting on top of an unguarded React layer: the standard React/a11y/no-any lint rules are registered as no-op stubs, no ratchet's glob includes client/src, the app shell remounts on every navigation, and the shared WebSocket silently stops subscribing channels — so the three laws in CLAUDE.md are all violated inside client/src, where no gate is looking.**


### Already at or above the bar — do not "fix" these

- client/src/lib/queryClient.ts is the best file in the client and should not be touched: a 30s AbortSignal.timeout on every request with the 'indefinite spinner' rationale recorded, transparent Clerk 401 refresh-and-retry on both apiRequest and getQueryFn, an Idempotency-Key generated ONCE in the wrapper so the post-401 retry reuses it, `mutations.retry: false` with the double-charge reasoning spelled out, a tier-aware 429 upsell that quotes the real price diff from shared/billing, and prefetchRoute as the single sanctioned prefetch path with the envelope-poisoning incident (2026-06-10/11) documented inline.
- vite.config.ts manualChunks as a FUNCTION with the reasoning for it — tiny shared utils (clsx/tailwind-merge) split out so they don't force-preload vendor-pdf/vendor-charts, and heavy single-consumer libs deliberately left unnamed so they ride their lazy consumer's chunk. This is better chunking reasoning than most teams ever write down.
- client/src/components/pax/PaxAskCard.tsx is a model component: one card, four hosts, every string is server truth, per-status aria-label composed from the verb, sr-only label on the edit textarea, 44px touch targets that relax to 36px on pointer:fine, and it explicitly refuses to invent anything. Do not 'clean it up'.
- usePaxAskActions (client/src/hooks/usePaxNeedsYou.ts:186-238) — every tap is wrapped so a failure returns a server-worded outcome instead of throwing, and usePaxNeedsYouCount returns `null` (not 0) until the server has answered. That is exactly right for a badge.
- The ratchet framework itself (scripts/ratchet.mjs, scripts/lint-eslint-ratchet.mjs) — mandatory measured `minima.files` population floors, per-member vacuity canaries, and bidirectional (fail-on-improvement) semantics. The design is better than the industry norm; the problem is only which globs it points at.
- ErrorBoundary keyed on wouter's location (App.tsx:2229-2232) so a per-route crash self-clears on the next navigation, plus independent boundaries around PageShell's content region and each embedded tab (page-shell.tsx:96, 138).
- client/src/lib/csrf-fetch.ts — one global fetch interceptor that adds the double-submit header, respects explicit callers, skips cross-origin and non-mutating methods. Correct call versus patching 137 sites.
- server/static.ts pre-compressed br/gz serving with the HTTP/2 compression-negotiation bug documented, plus the sw.js no-cache carve-out. Real operational depth.

### Benchmarked against

- Linear — persistent app shell: nav chrome is mounted above the route boundary and never unmounts, so route transitions swap only the content region; AcreOS remounts a 950-line Sidebar per navigation and papers over the state loss with localStorage.
- Vercel Dashboard / Next.js App Router — nested layouts + per-segment loading.tsx, so each route's skeleton matches that route's shape; AcreOS paints one KPI-grid silhouette for all 301 routes.
- Stripe Dashboard — every mutation surfaces a typed server error; nothing reports success on an unchecked response. AcreOS has 40 mutating fetches whose result is never read.
- Slack / Linear realtime — one connection owned by a singleton store with an explicit subscription registry replayed on every reconnect, plus jittered backoff and visibility gating; AcreOS's useRealtime keeps per-hook `connected` state so only the socket's owning hook ever subscribes.
- TanStack Query's own docs / Sentry, Shopify Polaris apps — a query-key factory that every call site imports (enforced by lint). AcreOS ships the factory (query-keys.ts) with zero importers and 1,327 inline key literals.
- GitHub / Figma web — source maps uploaded to the error tracker then deleted from the public asset dir; AcreOS ships 55 MB of .map files into dist/public/assets and serves them via express.static.
- Notion / Superhuman — refetch-on-focus so a tab left open never shows stale money numbers; AcreOS disables refetchOnWindowFocus globally.

### Findings


#### [high/M] The active ESLint config registers react-hooks (and every a11y/TS rule) as no-op stubs — rules-of-hooks and exhaustive-deps are unenforced across 268k LOC

- **Where:** `eslint.config.js:70-77 (stub plugin), eslint.config.js:38-45 (ignores), .eslintrc.json (dead legacy config)`
- **Defect:** eslint.config.js is the active flat config. It declares `"react-hooks": { rules: { "exhaustive-deps": { meta: { schema: [] }, create: () => ({}) }, "rules-of-hooks": { ... create: () => ({}) } } }` — literally empty rule implementations, added so legacy `// eslint-disable-next-line react-hooks/exhaustive-deps` comments don't error. The consequence is that `react-hooks/rules-of-hooks` — the one React rule that is never negotiable — has never run on this codebase, `exhaustive-deps` has never run (the 26 disable comments in client/src are inert decoration), and the flat config also never ports jsx-a11y/*, react/*, or @typescript-eslint/no-explicit-any from .eslintrc.json. eslint-plugin-react-hooks@7.1.1, eslint-plugin-jsx-a11y and eslint-plugin-react are all installed (package.json:245) and doing nothing. The eslint ratchet (scripts/lint-eslint-ratchet.mjs) is green because it only measures the five acreos/* rules, all baselined at 0 — a textbook case of CLAUDE.md's first law: the gate measures the right count of the wrong property.
- **Fix:** Port the real ruleset into eslint.config.js: `react-hooks/rules-of-hooks: error` (no baseline — fix the violations), `react-hooks/exhaustive-deps: warn` under a new per-rule ratchet baseline, jsx-a11y's recommended set, and `@typescript-eslint/no-explicit-any: warn` ratcheted. Delete the stub plugin. Add the three new rule names to scripts/eslint-rules-baseline.json with MEASURED counts and add each to the canary list in lint-eslint-ratchet.mjs so a rule going silent fails rather than reads as clean.
- **Comparable:** Every serious React shop (Vercel, Linear, Airbnb's own config) treats rules-of-hooks as an error-level, non-baselined gate; it catches conditional-hook bugs that produce non-deterministic crashes no test reliably reproduces.
- **Evidence:** Read eslint.config.js in full — the stub plugin at lines 70-77 and the ignores at 38-51. Read scripts/eslint-rules-baseline.json (five acreos/* keys, all 0). Confirmed eslint-plugin-react-hooks 7.1.1 is installed via node_modules/eslint-plugin-react-hooks/package.json. `grep -rn "exhaustive-deps" client/src | wc -l` = 26 inert disable comments.

#### [high/S] client/src is outside the population of EVERY TypeScript-erasure ratchet — 217 `as any` and 560 `: any` are completely ungated

- **Where:** `scripts/ratchets/as-any.json (globs: ["server/**/*.ts"]), scripts/ratchets/colon-any.json (same), client/src/hooks/use-realtime.ts:169`
- **Defect:** Both erasure ratchets scan `server/**/*.ts` only. Of the 17 ratchet configs, exactly one (self-fallback.json) includes client globs. So the entire React app — including money surfaces (finance.tsx, borrower-portal.tsx), tenant-scoped list pages and the Pax approval path — accumulates `as any` with no gate at all. The as-any.json description argues the case for the ratchet in terms of 'a mispriced charge or a cross-org data leak', and then scopes it to half the codebase. This is CLAUDE.md's third law verbatim: the rule proves its property only over the population it reads, and the population is invisible in the green result. It is not theoretical here — `client/src/hooks/use-realtime.ts:167-172` passes `{ queryKey, refetchInterval, onSuccess } as any` to useQuery. `onSuccess` was REMOVED from useQuery in TanStack Query v5, and package.json pins ^5.101.2 (installed: 5.101.2). The callback never fires; the notification badge can only ever count events that arrive over the socket while mounted and never initialises from /api/realtime/alerts/count. The `as any` is exactly what let a v4 API survive the v5 migration.
- **Fix:** Add client-scoped ratchet configs (as-any-client.json, colon-any-client.json) with globs `client/src/**/*.{ts,tsx}`, measured baselines (217 / 560 as of this read) and measured `minima.files` floors, then drive them down starting with money, tenant-key and auth paths. Separately: delete `useNotificationCount` (it has zero call sites) or rewrite it against the v5 API.
- **Comparable:** n/a — this is an internal-consistency defect, not a missing industry pattern.
- **Evidence:** Enumerated every scripts/ratchets/*.json glob programmatically. Counted `as any` = 217 and `:\s*any\b` = 560 across client/src with the same regexes the ratchets use. Read use-realtime.ts:164-186 and confirmed the installed react-query version is 5.101.2. `grep -rn useNotificationCount client/src` returns only the definition.

#### [high/M] The shared WebSocket silently stops subscribing channels: only the hook instance that opened the socket ever sees `connected`, and nothing re-subscribes after a reconnect

- **Where:** `client/src/hooks/use-realtime.ts:74-121 (connect + effect), client/src/hooks/use-websocket-channel.ts:36-41 (subscribe gated on `connected`), server/websocket.ts:344-355 (broadcast requires subscribedChannels.has)`
- **Defect:** `connect()` guards with `if (globalWs && globalWs.readyState === WebSocket.OPEN) return;` while `connected` is per-hook-instance state set only inside that instance's own `ws.onopen`. Two consequences, both live. (1) Later mount: any consumer that mounts after the socket is already OPEN — every React.lazy one does, e.g. NotificationBanner (App.tsx:2206), PaxCopilotRail (App.tsx:2201) — bails on the guard, so its `connected` stays false forever, so use-websocket-channel.ts:38 never calls `subscribe(channel)`. server/websocket.ts:352 only delivers to clients where `client.subscribedChannels.has(channel)`, so that consumer's channel is dead and it silently degrades to its 5-minute poll fallback (notification-center.tsx:88-93, layout-sidebar.tsx:1018-1024). A notification can take five minutes to appear. (2) Same-tick mount: the guard tests OPEN, not CONNECTING, so N consumers mounting in one commit each construct their own WebSocket and overwrite `globalWs`; N-1 sockets are orphaned and never closed (the unmount cleanup is deliberately a no-op, use-realtime.ts:114-119) — directly contradicting use-websocket-channel.ts:12's claim of 'no duplicate connections'. (3) After any disconnect, `ws.onclose` sets `connected=false` only on the owning instance; every other instance still believes it is connected and never re-runs its subscribe effect, so subscriptions are permanently lost for them even though the socket comes back.
- **Fix:** Move the connection to a module-level store with a real subscriber model: keep a `Set<string>` of desired channels and a `Set<() => void>` of connection-state listeners; `connect()` should bail on CONNECTING as well as OPEN; every hook instance registers a listener so all of them see state transitions; and `ws.onopen` should replay the whole desired-channel set to the server (that replay is the piece that is missing entirely). Add jittered backoff and gate reconnection on `document.visibilityState`/`navigator.onLine` while you are in there (see the separate backoff finding).
- **Comparable:** Linear and Slack both own the socket in a singleton store with an explicit subscription registry that is replayed on every reconnect; React hooks are thin readers of that store, never the owner of connection state.
- **Evidence:** Read client/src/hooks/use-realtime.ts and use-websocket-channel.ts in full, and server/websocket.ts:290-356. Enumerated the four consumers: notification-banner.tsx:64, notification-center.tsx:99, layout-sidebar.tsx:1031, usePaxNeedsYou.ts:105 (the last is called by BOTH usePaxNeedsYou and usePaxNeedsYouCount, so it mounts multiple instances per page).

#### [high/S] useWebSocketChannel double-fires the callback on founder:activity and delivers cross-channel events with no channel filter

- **Where:** `client/src/hooks/use-websocket-channel.ts:43-62 (wildcard listener) and :65-101 (six typed listeners), client/src/hooks/use-realtime.ts:38-54 (dispatchEvent)`
- **Defect:** `dispatchEvent` fans every message out to BOTH the type-specific handler set and the `'*'` handler set. useWebSocketChannel already registers a `'*'` handler that filters by `payload._channel`. Then, when `channel === "founder:activity"`, it additionally registers six typed handlers for `notification`, `agent_alert`, `agent_proposal`, `trust_promotion`, `action_executed`, `self_healing_executed`. Any of those six event types therefore invokes `callbackRef.current` TWICE — NotificationBanner (notification-banner.tsx:64) pushes the same notification into state twice and shows the banner twice. Worse, the typed handlers apply NO channel filter (they cannot: use-realtime.ts:41-45 passes typed handlers the bare `event.payload` without `_channel`), and they synthesise `channel` as the requested channel regardless of where the event came from — so a `notification` broadcast on any other channel is delivered to a founder:activity subscriber as if it were a founder:activity event.
- **Fix:** Delete the second effect (use-websocket-channel.ts:65-101) entirely — the wildcard path already covers those six types correctly and with a real channel filter. If typed handlers are wanted for perf, make use-realtime's dispatchEvent pass `_type`/`_channel` to typed handlers too and filter on `_channel` in the handler.
- **Evidence:** Read both files end to end. dispatchEvent (use-realtime.ts:38-54) dispatches to `listeners.get(event.type)` and then to `listeners.get('*')` unconditionally; use-websocket-channel.ts registers into both sets for the founder:activity channel. server/services/notificationDispatcher.ts:343 broadcasts type `"notification"` on `founder:activity`, so the duplicate path is a real production event, not a hypothetical.

#### [high/L] The app shell is not persistent — the 950-line Sidebar unmounts and remounts on every route navigation

- **Where:** `client/src/App.tsx:712 (single Suspense around the whole Switch), client/src/components/page-shell.tsx:104-125 (`<Sidebar />` rendered inside PageShell), client/src/components/layout-sidebar.tsx:873-1822 (Sidebar, 950 lines)`
- **Defect:** Every page renders its own `<PageShell>`, and PageShell renders `<Sidebar />`. Both live inside the single `<React.Suspense fallback={<RouteFallback />}>` at App.tsx:712 that wraps the entire `<Switch>`. Navigating from /leads to /deals therefore unmounts the whole subtree — Sidebar included — and mounts a fresh one. Concretely on each navigation: the 950-line Sidebar re-runs its mount effects, its `useWebSocketChannel(org:{id})` listener is torn down and re-registered (which, given the connection bug above, can leave the org channel unsubscribed), the mobile drawer state (`isOpen`, layout-sidebar.tsx:877) resets, and `expandedModules` is re-hydrated from and re-written to localStorage on every nav (layout-sidebar.tsx:990-1016) — that localStorage round-trip exists precisely to paper over this remount. It also means a chunk-download shows RouteFallback in place of the real chrome, so the user momentarily loses the nav they were pointing at.
- **Fix:** Hoist the chrome above the route boundary: render `<Sidebar />` + `<PageTopbar />` once in AppContent (outside the Suspense), and reduce PageShell to the content column with its own Suspense/ErrorBoundary. This is the single highest-leverage frontend change available — it removes the remount, makes route transitions swap only the content region, lets the fallback be content-shaped, and eliminates the localStorage rehydration hack.
- **Comparable:** Linear, Notion, Stripe Dashboard and Next.js App Router layouts all keep the nav chrome mounted across navigations; only the content region suspends. AcreOS is the only one of these that redraws a facsimile of its own sidebar.
- **Evidence:** Read App.tsx:702-720 and 1847 (the Suspense open/close spanning the whole Switch), page-shell.tsx:85-145 (Sidebar inside the shell), and layout-sidebar.tsx:873-1016 (component start, isOpen useState, expandedModules useState + persisting effect).

#### [high/M] Forty mutating fetch() calls never check the response — the UI reports effects that did not happen

- **Where:** `client/src/components/pax-knowledge-panel.tsx:62-90 and :96-107, client/src/components/pax-project-panel.tsx:87,105,116,129, client/src/components/layout-sidebar.tsx:217-234, client/src/components/founder/DelegationManager.tsx:29, client/src/components/note-assignments-card.tsx:189, client/src/components/settings/notification-quiet-hours.tsx:70`
- **Defect:** pax-knowledge-panel.tsx's three mutations each do `await fetch(url, { method: "PATCH"|"DELETE" ... })` with no `res.ok` check and no return value. A 403 or 500 resolves the promise, so react-query treats it as success, runs `onSuccess`, invalidates the cache — and the user sees the row re-appear with no error at all. The upload path (`uploadFile`, :96-107) is worse: it POSTs, ignores the result, invalidates, and the `finally` clears the spinner, so a rejected upload is indistinguishable from a successful one. layout-sidebar.tsx:217-234 goes further and wraps dismiss/acknowledge in `try { ... } catch {}` — a failed dismiss is swallowed entirely. A programmatic scan finds 40 mutating raw fetches with no status check within 22 lines. This is the client-side form of the exact defect class CLAUDE.md names ('a tool may not report an effect it did not have', schedule_background_job), and paxToolsReportRealEffects.test.ts's population is server tool switches — no gate has ever read a client mutation handler.
- **Fix:** Route every mutation through `apiRequest` from lib/queryClient (it calls throwIfResNotOk, so a non-2xx becomes an ApiError that the global MutationCache onError already turns into a correct toast with the server's message and docsUrl). Then extend the client lint population: add a check that a `mutationFn` body containing `fetch(` also contains `.ok`/`throwIfResNotOk`, enumerate the mutation-bearing files in the test the way CLAUDE.md prescribes for TOOL_SWITCHES, and baseline it.
- **Evidence:** Read pax-knowledge-panel.tsx:58-107 and layout-sidebar.tsx:205-235 directly. Scanned all of client/src for `fetch(` with a POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE method within 14 lines and no `.ok`/status check within 22 lines: 263 mutating raw fetches total, 40 with no check.

#### [high/S] Production source maps are emitted into the served asset directory and never deleted after the Sentry upload

- **Where:** `vite.config.ts:92 (`sourcemap: "hidden"`), script/build.ts:83-92 (Sentry inject + upload, no cleanup), server/static.ts:160-172 (express.static over distPath with no filter)`
- **Defect:** `sourcemap: "hidden"` suppresses the `//# sourceMappingURL` comment but still WRITES the .map files. script/build.ts injects debug ids and uploads to Sentry and then does nothing else — there is no delete step. server/static.ts mounts `express.static(distPath)` with no extension deny-list, so every `.map` under /assets/ is publicly fetchable at a URL derivable from the script filename in the HTML. The local dist confirms the shape: dist/public/assets holds 55 MB of maps including index-BbMJL8zI.js.map at 5.2 MB and vendor-map-*.js.map at 5.5 MB — full original TypeScript with the comments that name the security gates, the founder-only surfaces and the tenant-isolation reasoning.
- **Fix:** Delete `dist/public/**/*.map` (and the matching .map.gz/.map.br the compression plugin emits) at the end of script/build.ts, immediately after the Sentry upload succeeds — Sentry keeps its own copy, so debugging is unaffected. Belt-and-braces: add a `.map` 404 guard in server/static.ts before the express.static mount, and assert in the build that no .map survives into dist/public.
- **Comparable:** GitHub, Figma and Vercel all upload maps to the error tracker as a build step and delete them from the public asset directory in the same step.
- **Evidence:** Read vite.config.ts build options, script/build.ts:60-92 (grepped for unlink/rm/rimraf near the Sentry block — none), and server/static.ts:160-185 (no dotfiles/extension filter). Listed dist/public/assets and measured 55 MB total with the .map files present. I did not fetch the production URL, so 'served on acreos.io' is inferred from the code path rather than observed.

#### [high/M] The canonical query-key registry has zero production callers, and its own two key shapes are mutually incompatible

- **Where:** `client/src/lib/query-keys.ts (whole file, esp. :30-36 and :86-120), eslint.config.js:97-104 (`acreos/use-mutation-must-invalidate` pointing at it)`
- **Defect:** `grep -rl '@/lib/query-keys' client/src` returns ZERO files. `QK` and `invalidateRelated` are referenced only inside query-keys.ts itself and in its own docblock example (:131). Meanwhile client/src contains 1,327 inline `queryKey: [` literals and 668 raw `invalidateQueries` calls. This is CLAUDE.md's second law exactly: authoritative semantics with no production adoption is not canonical, and adding a new consumer to `RELATED` (the file's stated purpose — 'a new dashboard widget reading from leads should be a one-line append') would reach nothing. The `acreos/use-mutation-must-invalidate` ESLint rule's comment says it 'pairs with client/src/lib/query-keys.ts which provides invalidateRelated' — pairing with a function nobody calls. Second, the registry is internally inconsistent in a way that would break it if adopted: `QK.leads.detail(id)` is the segmented form `["/api/leads", id]` while `QK.leads.sms(id)` is the single-string form `` [`/api/leads/${id}/sms`] ``.
