The trap is documented three times in this very file — for /api/docs (:1572-1576), for the public e-sign routes (:1579-1583), and for the transparency report (:1586-1592, "the exact trap this comment block documents") — and then repeated a fourth time 700 lines later, with a comment asserting the opposite of what the routing does.
- **Fix:** Move the `/api/data-api` mount above line 1595, beside the other pre-catch-all public mounts. Better: stop relying on ordering — replace the bare `/api` catch-all with explicit path prefixes for `epicServicesRouter`, which removes the trap for every future mount rather than for this one. Note the TENSION with the DO-NOT-DO list: "no public API before ~50 customers" means nothing should be done to make this reachable NOW. The correct move today is to make the mount HONEST — either move it and gate it behind the expansion-ladder flag (`requireLadderFlag`), as `/api/marketplace` already is at :1373, or delete it and its comment. A mount whose comment claims an auth mode it does not have is the thing being fixed, not the availability of the API.
- **Comparable:** The general control is a routing table test rather than comments: enumerate every mount, classify its auth mode, and assert the declared mode matches the effective one under the real middleware order. The repo already built exactly this kind of gate for a sibling property (scripts/check-route-shadowing.mjs, baseline 0, which reasons about global registration order) — extending it to auth-mode reachability is the same machinery.
- **Evidence:** Read server/routes.ts:1570-1600 (three explicit warnings about the catch-all) and :2305-2315 (the /api/data-api mount). Read server/auth/clerkAuth.ts:293-332 to confirm isAuthenticated terminates rather than falling through.

#### [medium/M] A 5-connection pool per process against 9-wide concurrent fan-outs on the app's primary door

- **Where:** `server/db.ts:76-82 (`max: DB_POOL_MAX ?? 5`, `connectionTimeoutMillis: 10_000`); consumer at server/routes-today.ts:1095 (Promise.all of 9)`
- **Defect:** `GET /api/today` issues a nine-way `Promise.all` while `runPortfolioHealthJob` has already made several sequential queries, and the handler makes further reads at :1253/:1278/:1362 plus a write at :1531. A single Today render can therefore demand more concurrent connections than the entire process pool holds, so ONE user's render can queue every other in-flight request behind `connectionTimeoutMillis`. Requests that lose the race wait up to 10s and then throw — surfacing as a 500 through `Errors.internal`, indistinguishable from a real bug. The pool size is a deliberate, well-reasoned choice for pgBouncer transaction mode (documented at db.ts:7-23) and should not simply be raised; the problem is that the hot path's concurrency was never budgeted against it.
- **Fix:** Budget concurrency per request rather than growing the pool: cap fan-out width (`p-limit(3)` around the Today gather), and collapse the four whole-table reads into the small indexed queries described in the Today finding, which removes most of the demand. Add a pool-saturation metric (`pool.waitingCount`, `pool.totalCount`) to the existing Prometheus registry in server/metrics.ts and alert on sustained waiting — right now saturation is invisible and will present as unexplained 500s.
- **Comparable:** The standard practice at this size is an explicit per-request connection budget plus pool-wait metrics — pgbouncer's `SHOW POOLS` scraped into Prometheus, or `pg_stat_activity` wait events. Vercel/PlanetScale-era guidance is the same: fix the fan-out, don't grow the pool, because behind a transaction-mode pooler the pool is the wrong dial.
- **Evidence:** Read server/db.ts:1-120 (pool config and the pgBouncer rationale) and server/routes-today.ts:1085-1105, 1226-1240, 1253, 1278, 1362, 1531. fly.toml:89-91 confirms the app VM is shared-cpu-4x/1GB, so vertical headroom is limited too.

#### [medium/S] Bulk lead/property/deal updates write unvalidated free-text into status and tags

- **Where:** `server/routes-bulk.ts:62-71 (leads/update); same shape at :129 (properties/update) and :169 (deals/update)`
- **Defect:** The handler correctly allowlists WHICH fields may be updated and correctly validates `assignedTo` against org membership, but the VALUES pass straight through: `if (updates.status) allowedUpdates.status = updates.status;` accepts any string, and `if (updates.tags) allowedUpdates.tags = updates.tags;` accepts any JSON. `leads.status` is a plain `text` column that the rest of the system filters on by literal (`status NOT IN ('dead','closed')` at routes-founder-bridge.ts:146, `status === "closed" || status === "dead"` at routes-today.ts:746, and the funnel/KPI aggregations). One bad bulk update — a typo, a bad CSV mapping, a client bug — silently moves up to 500 leads to a status no query recognises, and they vanish from every funnel, every Today queue and every conversion metric with no error and no way to notice. This is representative rather than unique: across server/, 299 of 531 mutating handlers that read `req.body` do so without any zod schema.
- **Fix:** Define the status enums once in shared/ (they already exist implicitly in the filters) and parse the bulk body with zod: `z.object({ ids: z.array(z.number().int().positive()).min(1).max(MAX_BATCH), updates: z.object({ status: LeadStatus.optional(), assignedTo: z.string().nullable().optional(), tags: z.array(z.string().max(64)).max(50).optional() }) })`, returning `Errors.validationFailed`. Then close the class rather than the instance: add a lint that fails any `app.post|put|patch` handler which reads `req.body` and has no `safeParse` in its body, baseline the 299, and ratchet down — the same shape as the existing `res-status-raw` ratchet.
- **Comparable:** Stripe and Shopify reject unknown enum values with a typed 400 naming the field and the permitted set; the generic control is schema-at-the-boundary (zod/tRPC/OpenAPI request validation) so no handler can be written without one. AcreOS already has zod on 233 handlers and `Errors.validationFailed` wired to a client toast that renders field-level detail — the machinery is built and half-adopted.
- **Evidence:** Read server/routes-bulk.ts:30-125 in full (the allowlist and permission gating are genuinely good; only the value validation is missing). Scanned all 761 mutating route handlers under server/: 531 read `req.body`, 299 of those contain no `safeParse`/`.parse(`/`validateBody` call.

#### [low/S] runNegotiationAssistant is an unwired LLM tool loop whose tools read properties with no org predicate — the leak that ships the day it is wired

- **Where:** `server/services/negotiationOrchestrator.ts:663-676 (signature takes organizationId), :765-780 (get_property_valuation), :782-800 (get_comparable_sales), :802-818 (get_negotiation_thread)`
- **Defect:** `runNegotiationAssistant(organizationId, threadId, userMessage)` accepts an org and never uses it inside `executeTool`. `get_property_valuation` resolves `db.query.properties.findFirst({ where: eq(properties.id, parseInt(args.property_id)) })` from a model-supplied argument; `get_comparable_sales` runs `db.query.properties.findMany({ where: eq(properties.status, 'sold'), limit: args.max_results })` across every tenant and returns sale prices, acreage, county and price-per-acre; `get_negotiation_thread` resolves a thread and its moves by bare id. Nothing calls this function today — grepping the whole repo for `runNegotiationAssistant` returns only its own declaration — so no customer is harmed, and I am NOT claiming an exploit. It matters because it is the finished, plausible-looking shape that a future wave will wire in an afternoon, and because it is invisible to every existing gate: the tenancy lint does not parse `db.query.*`, and the reachability gate scores it as dead code rather than as dangerous code.
- **Fix:** Either delete it (the DO-NOT-DO north star is a smaller codebase, and `generateCounterOffer`/`analyzeSellerPsychology`/`generateNegotiationScript` at :61/:116/:543 are the ones actually reached from routes-core-ai.ts:441-535), or thread `organizationId` into `executeTool` and add `eq(properties.organizationId, organizationId)` to all three reads — with the comparables tool refusing rather than silently widening when the org has no sold properties, per refuse-not-fabricate. Do not leave it as-is.
- **Comparable:** The control an elite team applies to agent tools is that every data-reading tool takes its tenant scope from the SERVER-side session, never from a model argument — the pattern AcreOS already gets right in `executeSupportTool`'s `query_org_data` (server/ai/supportAgent.ts:4085-4180, where every branch carries `eq(table.organizationId, org.id)` and `sql.raw` is confined to a hardcoded field whitelist). That is the template; this file predates it.
- **Evidence:** Read server/services/negotiationOrchestrator.ts:663-940 (the tool definitions, `executeTool`, and the agentic loop at :881-930). Grepped server/, client/ and shared/ for `runNegotiationAssistant` — one hit, the declaration itself. Confirmed the reachable siblings by reading server/routes-core-ai.ts:430-540.

## Head of Security — authorization, tenant isolation, and the AI confused-deputy surface

**Authentication and the Pax approval kernel are genuinely strong, but authorization below the middleware is not: six route handlers reach another tenant's rows with no organization predicate (two of them read data out), the SSRF guard's IPv6 branch is unreachable code, the 74-case support tool switch never got the permission-ladder gate that tools.ts has, and `/api/docs/openapi.json` publishes a deny-list-filtered map of the whole app to anonymous callers.**


### Already at or above the bar — do not "fix" these

- The Pax approval kernel's gate ORDER in server/ai/tools.ts:1180-1290 is the best security code in this repository. Stripping model-supplied `_approved` before dispatch (so approval is a server-side option only the human-tap endpoint can set), failing CLOSED when Pax controls cannot be read, freezing unapproved actions as content-hashed pending rows with 24h expiry, and the deliberate kernel→pause→scope→FCRA ordering with each step's reasoning written down — this is elite work. Do not refactor it.
- WebSocket handshake authorization (server/websocket.ts:90-135, :298-316) is correct in every dimension I tested: the claimed userId must match the cookie's own user, org membership is re-verified against ownership OR an active team seat, `founder:activity` is gated on a session-derived flag, and the full-id channel match closes the `org:1` → `org:19` prefix hole. Nothing here needs touching except the producer-side channel names (finding 16).
- `viewerReadOnlyGate` (server/middleware/viewerReadOnlyGate.ts) chose deny-by-default and wrote down WHY sixty `requirePermission` calls would have been the wrong fix — 'the polarity is the design.' That reasoning is exactly right and should be the model for the OpenAPI reflector's deny-list (finding 11).
- `gateExemptions.pathIsExempt` (server/middleware/gateExemptions.ts) — segment-boundary containment shared across all three posture gates, with the explicit property that the rule can only ever NARROW an exemption. This is what a security predicate should look like.
- MCP endpoint authentication (server/mcp/auth.ts) — per-org API key resolved through the same hashed lookup as the public API with a timing-safe re-compare, revocation and expiry honoured, and the static key bound to a single MCP_ORG_ID so it can no longer roam orgs. The header documents both credential paths and their org binding.
- Signing-token key rotation (server/services/signingTokens.ts) — a `<kid>.<hmac>` keyring with a documented rotation flow, backwards-compatible legacy fallback, and timingSafeEqual. The public signing route (server/routes-public-sign.ts:209+) then layers document expiry, roster membership, a replay guard derived from the signatures table rather than a self-written marker, and an independent server-side E-SIGN §101(c) consent gate.
- Secret hygiene. I grepped for hardcoded sk_live/pk_live/AKIA/ghp_/xoxb/private keys across server, client, shared and scripts: zero real hits — every match is a test fixture or a redaction pattern. `server/services/solene/` carries an active credential-sanitization layer with its own test corpus.
- XSS discipline. All 11 `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` sites in client/src are DOMPurify-wrapped or render internal chart/JSON-LD config. The CSP (server/middleware/security.ts) uses a per-request nonce in production, keeps `object-src 'none'`, and scopes frame-ancestors to a narrow, justified embed allow-list.
- `trust proxy` is set to exactly 1 (server/index.ts:183) with server/utils/clientIp.ts documenting why not 2 — rate limits and audit IPs cannot be spoofed by a client-supplied X-Forwarded-For. CORS (security.ts:130-180) is a strict allow-list with credentials only on matched origins.
- The borrower portal re-pins `note.organization_id === session.organization_id` on every request in one middleware (server/routes-borrower.ts:386-433) rather than making each route remember the AND clause, destroying the session on mismatch. Access tokens are 32 random bytes; cookies are httpOnly + sameSite=strict.
- `shared/governance/constitution.ts` — machine-readable hard-stops tagged by HOW each is enforced, with a ratchet on the count of unenforced ones. The spend-ceiling entry's history (a mutation-verified test, the deleted private getTier, a single AUTONOMOUS_SPEND_CEILING_CENTS constant serving three former literals) is a genuinely rare standard of rigour.
- The tenancy lint (scripts/check-org-scoped-fetch.mjs) with its scan-population floors and down-only registers is the right instrument even though it is currently red and has the blind spot in finding 19. Its four-register design — including a 'declarations whose body could not be located: 0' assertion so blindness cannot report itself as progress — is better than most companies' equivalents.

### Benchmarked against

- Stripe — restricted API keys plus 2FA re-entry for pricing/payout changes; the pattern worth stealing is step-up authentication scoped to the ACTION, not just the login session (directly applicable to finding 10, where /api/founder/pricing has no second factor)
- GitHub — 'sudo mode' re-prompts for the second factor on a rolling window before security-sensitive operations; and a hand-curated OpenAPI document that is a product artifact, never a reflection of the live router (findings 10 and 11)
- Supabase / Postgres RLS — the tenancy predicate lives in the database, so a forgotten WHERE clause returns zero rows instead of another tenant's; the stealable half here is the polarity, not the technology: make the unscoped query unreachable rather than lint for it (findings 1, 2, 12)
- Linear and Notion — every record resolves through a tenant-bound repository where the workspace id is a required constructor argument; there is no callable shape that omits it (finding 12, the 97 optional-tenant-key methods)
- Slack link unfurling and GitHub webhook dispatch — resolve-once-then-pin-the-IP outbound fetching instead of validating a URL string and handing the string to fetch; closes both the IPv6-literal bypass and the DNS-rebinding TOCTOU that validateUrl's comment claims to have closed (finding 7)
- AWS IMDSv2 — session-token + hop-limit as defence for the thing SSRF filters keep failing to protect; the lesson is that a host-shaped deny-list is the wrong layer (finding 7)
- Intercom Fin and Zendesk agent copilot — the assistant executes privileged actions under the requesting person's own role and is never a higher-privileged principal than the user talking to it (finding 8, executeSupportTool's missing scope gate)
- Django's tenant-bound default manager — removes the unscoped queryset from the API surface rather than documenting that you should not use it (findings 12 and 13)

### Findings


#### [critical/S] Cross-tenant read: GET /api/custom-fields/values/:entityType/:entityId has no organization predicate anywhere in the query

- **Where:** `server/routes-integrations.ts:1439-1447 → server/storage/customizationRepo.ts:78-92`
- **Defect:** The handler calls `storage.getCustomFieldValues(entityType, entityId)` with no org argument. The repo method joins `custom_field_values` to `custom_field_definitions` filtering only on `customFieldDefinitions.entityType` and `customFieldValues.entityId` — no `organizationId`. `entityId` is a global serial (a lead/property/deal id), so `GET /api/custom-fields/values/lead/4211` returns every organization's custom-field values recorded against lead #4211, along with each org's field definition names. Custom fields are exactly where tenants put the data no stock column holds — seller motivation notes, purchase price ceilings, attorney contacts. The root cause is schema: `custom_field_values` (shared/schema.ts:5290-5297) carries no tenant key at all, so the org-scoped-fetch lint's "org-scoped tables" population never included it — the gate is green because the table was invisible to it, which is precisely the third law in CLAUDE.md.
- **Fix:** Immediately: scope the query — join through `customFieldDefinitions.organizationId = :orgId` inside `getCustomFieldValues`, and make `organizationId` a REQUIRED first parameter so no call site can omit it. Structurally: add `organization_id NOT NULL` to `custom_field_values` (backfill from the definition), index `(organization_id, entity_type, entity_id)`, and add the table to the lint's org-scoped set so the gate can actually see it. Also verify the caller owns `entityId` for the named `entityType`, using the existing `assertEntityBelongsToOrg` helper in server/utils/orgScope.ts.
- **Comparable:** Linear and Notion both resolve every record through a tenant-scoped repository where the workspace id is a required constructor argument, not an optional method parameter — there is no callable shape that omits it. Supabase pushes the same invariant into Postgres RLS so a forgotten WHERE clause returns zero rows instead of another tenant's.
- **Evidence:** Read server/routes-integrations.ts:1439-1447 and server/storage/customizationRepo.ts:78-92 in full; confirmed the WHERE clause contains only entityType + entityId. Confirmed shared/schema.ts:5290-5297 has no organizationId column. Confirmed `assertEntityBelongsToOrg` exists and is tested (tests/unit/orgScope.test.ts).

#### [critical/S] Cross-tenant write + tenant reassignment: PUT /api/campaigns/:id has no ownership check and accepts `organizationId` in the body

- **Where:** `server/routes-campaigns.ts:711-721 (call at :714) → server/storage/campaignRepo.ts:32-39; schema at shared/schema.ts:3647-3649`
- **Defect:** Two independent defects compound. (1) The handler calls `storage.updateCampaign(Number(req.params.id), validated)` and omits the third, OPTIONAL `organizationId` parameter, so the UPDATE's WHERE is `eq(campaigns.id, id)` alone — any authenticated user can mutate any organization's campaign by guessing a serial id. The route two definitions below it (`/api/campaigns/:id/send-direct-mail`, line 748) does it correctly with `storage.getCampaign(org.id, campaignId)`, so the correct shape was in the author's hands. (2) `updateCampaignSchema = insertCampaignSchema.partial()` (line 13) and `insertCampaignSchema` omits only `id, createdAt, updatedAt` — NOT `organizationId`. So the body can carry `organizationId`, and the unfiltered `...updates` spread writes it: an attacker can move a victim's campaign into their own org, or push their own campaign into a victim's org (where it will render in the victim's campaign list and can be wired to their sending identity). Only 3 of 224 `createInsertSchema` declarations in shared/schema.ts omit organizationId — leads, properties and deals — so this mass-assignment shape is available on almost every other `.partial()` update route.
- **Fix:** Pass `org.id` as the third argument at line 714 and make the parameter REQUIRED in campaignRepo.updateCampaign (it has 2 other call sites — server/routes-integrations.ts:1298 also omits it). Separately, either add `organizationId: true` to the `.omit()` on insertCampaignSchema, or route the payload through `omitProtectedFields` from server/utils/updatePayload.ts. Then add a schema-level test that fails if any `createInsertSchema` on a table with an `organization_id` column leaves that key in the derived type.
- **Comparable:** Rails' `strong_parameters` and Django REST's explicit serializer field lists both make mass assignment opt-in per field. Stripe's API rejects unknown/immutable parameters outright rather than silently persisting them.
- **Evidence:** Read server/routes-campaigns.ts:1-40 and 700-760; read server/storage/campaignRepo.ts:32-39 (confirmed `if (organizationId) conditions.push(...)`); read shared/schema.ts:3647-3649 (confirmed only id/createdAt/updatedAt omitted); counted `createInsertSchema` (224) vs `organizationId: true` in omit lists (3) with grep.

#### [high/S] Cross-tenant read via a write endpoint: POST /api/va/briefings/:id/read returns another org's briefing body

- **Where:** `server/routes-ai.ts:1767-1774 → server/storage/vaRepo.ts:270-278`
- **Defect:** `storage.markBriefingRead(briefingId)` is called with one argument; the repo's tenant predicate is behind `if (organizationId)` and is therefore skipped. The UPDATE ends with `.returning()` and the route does `res.json(updated)` — so this is not merely a cross-tenant write, it exfiltrates the full `va_briefings` row of whichever org owns that id. A VA daily briefing is a written summary of the org's pipeline, overdue tasks and deal state. An attacker enumerating ids 1..N harvests other tenants' operating summaries one POST at a time, and marks them read (destroying the victim's unread signal) as a side effect.
- **Fix:** Pass `req.organization.id` as the second argument and make it required in vaRepo.markBriefingRead. Prefer the pattern used correctly a few files over (server/routes-crm-extras.ts:404-410): fetch org-scoped first, 404 if absent, then mutate. Also stop returning the row from a 'mark read' endpoint — respond `{ ok: true }`.
- **Evidence:** Read server/routes-ai.ts:1767-1774 and server/storage/vaRepo.ts:270-278 directly; confirmed `.returning()` and `res.json(updated)`.

#### [high/S] Cross-tenant read/write: PUT /api/notifications/:id/read ignores the tenant-scoping the repo was hardened to accept

- **Where:** `server/routes-analytics.ts:360-368 → server/storage/automationRepo.ts:114-130`
- **Defect:** `storage.markNotificationRead(id)` omits both the optional `organizationId` and the optional `userId`. The repo comment at lines 115-119 explicitly records that these were added by two prior audits (F-23-4, F-17-1) precisely 'so a caller cannot flip another org's notification by guessing its id' — and then made them optional, so this call site silently opted out of the fix. The route responds with the returned row, so the notification title/body of another tenant (which routinely names a lead, a property address or a payment amount) is read back to the attacker. This is the clearest instance of the systemic pattern: the security fix exists, the call site doesn't use it, and nothing in the type system objects.
- **Fix:** `storage.markNotificationRead(id, org.id, userId)` at line 363, and change the repo signature to `(id: number, organizationId: number, userId: string)` — required, not optional. Return `{ ok: true }` rather than the row.
- **Evidence:** Read server/routes-analytics.ts:355-372 and server/storage/automationRepo.ts:114-130; the sibling route `/api/notifications/read-all` at line 372 correctly passes `org.id` and `userId`.

#### [high/S] Cross-tenant write: POST /api/ai/nudges/:id/dismiss can silence every other tenant's Pax nudges

- **Where:** `server/routes-ai.ts:1111-1120`
- **Defect:** `db.update(paxNudges).set({ dismissedAt: new Date() }).where(_eq(paxNudges.id, parseInt(req.params.id)))` — the WHERE clause is a bare primary key on a table whose schema declares `organizationId: integer("organization_id").notNull()` (shared/schema.ts:2621-2623). Any authenticated user can loop ids and dismiss the entire platform's Pax nudges. This is not a data leak, it is a denial-of-signal attack on the product's core promise: the autonomy program's whole value is that Pax surfaces what needs attention, and this endpoint lets any customer turn that off for every other customer, silently and irreversibly (nothing un-dismisses).
- **Fix:** Add `eq(paxNudges.organizationId, req.organization.id)` to the WHERE. Given this route sits on the autonomy surface, add a tenant-boundary unit test alongside the existing tests/unit/paxCannotEnumerateTenants.test.ts rather than relying on the lint, since a `db.update` inside a route file is outside the lint's storage/services walk.
- **Evidence:** Read server/routes-ai.ts:1111-1120 and shared/schema.ts:2621-2629 directly.

#### [high/S] Cross-tenant write: POST /api/custom-fields/values and /bulk accept an arbitrary entityId

- **Where:** `server/routes-integrations.ts:1450-1465 and 1468-1490`
- **Defect:** Both handlers correctly verify that `definitionId` belongs to the caller's org (`storage.getCustomFieldDefinition(org.id, definitionId)`), then call `storage.setCustomFieldValue(definitionId, entityId, value)` with an `entityId` taken straight from the body and never checked against anything. Combined with the unscoped read in finding 1, this is a complete cross-tenant read/write pair on custom-field data: org A can plant text on org B's lead #4211 and — because the read is also unscoped — org B's UI will render it. That is stored content injection into another tenant's CRM, attributable to nobody.
- **Fix:** Validate the entity before writing: `assertEntityBelongsToOrg(definition.entityType, entityId, org.id)` (server/utils/orgScope.ts) and 404 on failure. The bulk route must do this once, outside the loop, and refuse the whole batch rather than partially applying it.
- **Evidence:** Read server/routes-integrations.ts:1450-1490 and server/storage/customizationRepo.ts:94-112; confirmed setCustomFieldValue's only predicates are definitionId + entityId.

#### [high/S] SSRF filter bypass: isPrivateIPv6() is unreachable for literal IPv6 hosts, so ::ffff:-mapped loopback/link-local passes validateUrl

- **Where:** `server/middleware/fileUploadSecurity.ts:335-390 (literal short-circuit at :369-371; dead function at :314-325); exploitable at server/routes-integrations.ts:1765-1817 and server/jobs/webhookJobHandler.ts:46-60`
- **Defect:** validateUrl checks a literal host only against `PRIVATE_HOSTNAME_PATTERNS` and `BLOCKED_EXACT_IPS`, then short-circuits: `isPrivateIPv4`/`isPrivateIPv6` are called ONLY inside the `if (!isLiteralIPv4 && !isLiteralIPv6)` DNS branch. So `isPrivateIPv6` — the function that knows about `::`, `fe80::/10` beyond the literal `fe80:` prefix, and `::ffff:` v4-mapped addresses — never runs on the input it was written for. I verified with Node's WHATWG URL parser that `http://[::ffff:127.0.0.1]/` normalizes to hostname `::ffff:7f00:1`, `http://[::ffff:169.254.169.254]/` to `::ffff:a9fe:a9fe`, and that `[::]/` and `[fe9a::1]/` survive verbatim — none matches any entry in PRIVATE_HOSTNAME_PATTERNS (`/^::1$/`, `/^fc00:/`, `/^fd[0-9a-f]{2}:/`, `/^fe80:/`) or BLOCKED_EXACT_IPS. A second bug compounds it: `isPrivateIPv6`'s mapped-address regex is `/^::ffff:([0-9.]+)$/`, which would not match the normalized `::ffff:7f00:1` even if it were called. `POST /api/webhooks/test` (line 1766) runs validateUrl, then `fetch(url, {method:'POST'})`, then returns `{ status, ok }` to the caller — a status oracle for internal port scanning on the app host and the ability to POST a signed JSON body at internal services. `browse_web` is not affected because browserAutomation.ts:797 rejects every IPv6 host outright; the webhook paths have no such second layer. The header comment at lines 264-274 claims all of this is blocked, so the gate reads as green.
- **Fix:** Move the private-IP evaluation before the short-circuit: run `isPrivateIPv4(host)` / `isPrivateIPv6(host)` on every literal, and fix the mapped-address handling to parse the last 32 bits of any `::ffff:` address rather than regex-matching dotted quads. Better: stop validating a string and then handing the string to fetch — resolve once, reject the resolved set, and connect to the PINNED address via a custom `lookup` in the agent, which also closes the DNS-rebinding TOCTOU the header comment claims (incorrectly) to have closed. Add the four literals above to a test corpus and mutate the guard to watch it fail.
- **Comparable:** Slack's link unfurler and GitHub's webhook dispatcher both resolve-then-pin rather than validating a URL string; AWS's answer to the same class was IMDSv2's session tokens plus a hop limit. Neither relies on a hostname-shaped deny-list.
- **Evidence:** Read fileUploadSecurity.ts:264-390 in full and traced the control flow. Ran `new URL()` in Node to confirm the four normalizations. Read routes-integrations.ts:1765-1817 (confirmed status is returned) and browserAutomation.ts:720-810 (confirmed the IPv6 blanket block that protects browse_web only).

#### [high/M] The permission-ladder gate was installed on tools.ts and never on executeSupportTool's 74-case switch

- **Where:** `server/ai/supportAgent.ts:1329-1420 (gate order) vs server/ai/tools.ts:1246-1283; caller at server/ai/paxSupportResolver.ts:295`
- **Defect:** tools.ts:1248 reads `scopeForIntent(toolName)` and refuses a caller who lacks the declared scope — the comment at 1235-1245 states why: 'the REST door for an operation could require tenant_pii_write while the Pax door for the SAME operation required nothing at all, and a member could reach it by typing a sentence.' `executeSupportTool` — the second dispatch switch, 74 top-level cases, driven by a model in conversation with a paying customer — has the pricing hard-stop, the Pax-controls read, the approval kernel and the pause gate, but NO scope check at all (grep for userHasScope/scopeForIntent/permissionContext in that file returns nothing). Worse, `paxSupportResolver.ts:295` calls it as `executeSupportTool(name, args, org, ticketId)` with no options object, so there is no `userId` to hold to a scope even if the gate were added. Concretely: `get_payment_history` and `get_subscription_details` are on PAUSE_SAFE_SUPPORT_TOOLS (supportAgent.ts:1232+), so they run for any org member regardless of `canManageBilling` and even while Pax is paused — while `POST /api/billing/packs/checkout` (server/routes-billing.ts:637) requires `requirePermission("canManageBilling")`. And `retry_failed_jobs`, `unlock_stuck_jobs`, `apply_self_healing_fix`, `fix_data_integrity_issue`, `resolve_alert` are in NEITHER ALWAYS_ASK_SUPPORT_TOOLS (shared/pax-controls.ts:167-173, five entries) nor the pause-safe set — so at the default stance they execute on the model's decision with no tap and no scope. This is the exact population blind spot CLAUDE.md documents for paxToolsReportRealEffects.test.ts, still open on the same file.
- **Fix:** Lift the scope block out of tools.ts into a shared `assertToolScope(toolName, org, userId)` and call it from BOTH switches; thread `userId` through processSupportChat (which already has it, supportAgent.ts:5278-5283) and resolveTicketWithPax into every executeSupportTool call. Then enumerate the dispatch switches in a `TOOL_SWITCHES` list inside the gate's test, with a per-member vacuity assertion, so a third switch fails the build rather than being silently uncovered.
- **Comparable:** Intercom Fin and Zendesk's agent copilot both execute privileged actions under the requesting agent's own role, not the workspace's — the assistant is never a higher-privileged principal than the person talking to it.
- **Evidence:** Read executeSupportTool's gate preamble (supportAgent.ts:1329-1420); grepped supportAgent.ts for userHasScope/scopeForIntent/requirePermission — zero hits. Read paxSupportResolver.ts:295 (four args, no options). Read shared/pax-controls.ts:167-173 and supportAgent.ts:1232-1275 for the two classification sets. Read tools.ts:1235-1283 for the gate that exists.

#### [high/S] viewerReadOnlyGate exempts /api/support/, which is the door to side-effecting Pax support tools

- **Where:** `server/middleware/viewerReadOnlyGate.ts:72 (VIEWER_WRITE_EXEMPT_PREFIXES) → server/routes-support-tickets.ts:180-205 and :215-255`
- **Defect:** The gate's own header states each exemption 'is a hole in a read-only guarantee', and the entry's stated intent is narrow — 'contacting support about what they can see'. But `/api/support/` also carries `POST /api/support/tickets/:id/messages` (→ processSupportChat) and `POST /api/support/tickets/:id/pax-resolve` (→ resolveTicketWithPax), both of which drive the 74-case tool switch that can `resync_stripe`, `apply_billing_fix`, `reset_user_preferences`, `unlock_stuck_jobs` and `retry_failed_jobs` against the org. So the role an owner chose specifically to deny CRM writes can, by typing a sentence into a support ticket, cause org-wide state changes — including approving the always-ask ones, since nothing checks the approver's role either. The exemption is doing exactly what the header warned an exemption does.
- **Fix:** Narrow the entry to the paths that are genuinely about reaching a person (`/api/support/tickets` create + `/api/support/tickets/:id/messages` only if the resolver is not invoked), and refuse the tool-driving routes for viewers. Cleanest: gate on the TOOL rather than the path — once finding 8's shared `assertToolScope` exists, a viewer holds no write scope and every side-effecting support tool refuses on its own, and this exemption stops being load-bearing.
- **Evidence:** Read viewerReadOnlyGate.ts:1-90 (including VIEWER_WRITE_EXEMPT_PREFIXES) and routes-support-tickets.ts:180-260; confirmed both routes carry only isAuthenticated + getOrCreateOrg and call into the support agent.

#### [high/S] MFA is enforced on /api/admin/* but not on /api/founder/*, where the pricing and money hard-stops actually live

- **Where:** `server/routes.ts:1852 (`app.use("/api/admin", isAuthenticated, requireClerkMFA)`); unprotected examples at server/routes-admin.ts:3244, server/routes.ts:1830, :1986-1996`
- **Defect:** `requireClerkMFA` is mounted on exactly one prefix. Everything under `/api/founder/*` — a far larger namespace — bypasses it. That includes `DELETE /api/founder/pricing/:tier/promo` (routes-admin.ts:3244), which is a PRICING CHANGE, one of the four hard-stops CLAUDE.md declares founder-only forever; `/api/founder/finance` (routes.ts:1830); the Meta ad-spend surface; and the v10–v14 sovereign control plane (routes.ts:1986-1989) whose endpoints include `versions/:id/deploy`, `versions/:codename/rollback` and `trust/promote`. The comment at routes.ts:1832-1851 shows the team already learned that MFA ordering is load-bearing and fixed it for /api/admin; the namespace split was never revisited. Founder identity is asserted by email or Clerk user id (server/services/founder.ts via clerkAuth.ts:342-355), so a single compromised founder session — or a stolen `__session` cookie riding the JWT fallback described in finding 17 — reaches every hard-stop with no second factor.
- **Fix:** Add `app.use("/api/founder", isAuthenticated, requireClerkMFA)` immediately beside line 1852 (before any /api/founder handler registers — the ordering test at tests/unit/adminMfaOrdering.test.ts should be widened to cover this prefix too), and add `/api/founder/pricing`, `/api/founder/money`, `/api/founder/finance` and `/api/founder/v1*` to HIGH_TRUST_PATH_PREFIXES so MFA must be SET UP, not merely verified-if-present.
- **Comparable:** GitHub's sudo mode re-prompts for the second factor on security-sensitive actions within a rolling window; Stripe requires 2FA re-entry to change payout or pricing settings; AWS supports MFA-conditioned IAM policies on individual privileged actions. All three treat 'authenticated at login' as insufficient for the highest-blast-radius operations.
- **Evidence:** Read routes.ts:1826-1860 and middleware/requireClerkMFA.ts:1-75 (decision matrix + HIGH_TRUST_PATH_PREFIXES). Grepped requireClerkMFA call sites: exactly one mount, on /api/admin. Confirmed routes-admin.ts:3244 registers the pricing-promo delete under an /api/founder path with isFounderAdmin only.

#### [high/S] Unauthenticated /api/docs/openapi.json enumerates the whole app through a deny-list, undoing the deliberate 404-hiding of founder routes

- **Where:** `server/routes.ts:1577 (`app.use('/api/docs', apiDocsRouter)`) → server/routes-api-docs.ts:30-47 → server/openapi-reflector.ts:137-190`
- **Defect:** The docs router has no auth (the comment at routes.ts:1574-1576 says the mount is placed above the /api catch-all precisely so it stays open) and `reflectAppPaths(appRef)` walks the LIVE Express stack, emitting an operation for every registered route with its verb and path-parameter names, served with `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`. Filtering is a 15-entry PRIVATE_PATH_PREFIXES deny-list. Deny-lists are allow-by-default: `/api/scp/v2/` is NOT on it, so the sovereign control plane (`/trust/promote`, `/trust/demote`, `/evolution/rollback`, `/evolution/pause`) — endpoints whose own guard comment at routes.ts:1993-1996 records that they were once completely unauthenticated — are published to anonymous callers. Neither are `/api/dsar/`, `/api/legal-hold/`, `/api/dunning/`, `/api/data-api/`, `/api/mcp`. This directly contradicts the design decision two files over: requireFounder returns **404 to hide the existence of founder-only routes from non-founders** (clerkAuth.ts:335-337). One endpoint hands out the map the other spends a status code concealing. Note the Swagger HTML at routes-api-docs.ts:52+ loads assets from unpkg.com, which is absent from the CSP script-src/style-src allowlist (security.ts:60-102) — so the human-facing UI is broken while the machine-readable leak works fine. There is also a tension with the standing 'no public API before ~50 customers' decision: a public, CORS-open OpenAPI document is a public API surface in everything but name.
- **Fix:** Invert the polarity to match the rest of the codebase (viewerReadOnlyGate's header makes exactly this argument): emit ONLY paths present in the hand-curated `generateOpenAPISpec()` allow-list and drop the reflector from the public response, or gate the reflected merge behind isAuthenticated + an org API key. Add a test that fails if any reflected path is absent from the hand spec. Either fix the Swagger UI's asset origins or delete the HTML route rather than shipping a page that cannot render.
- **Comparable:** Stripe, Twilio and GitHub all publish a hand-curated OpenAPI document that is a product artifact, never a reflection of the live router. Nothing internal can leak into it because nothing internal is ever added to it.
- **Evidence:** Read routes.ts:1574-1577, routes-api-docs.ts:1-60, and openapi-reflector.ts:118-200 including the full PRIVATE_PATH_PREFIXES list; confirmed /api/scp is absent and that routes-scp-v2.ts registers app-level `/api/scp/v2/*` paths the reflector will see. Compared against clerkAuth.ts:335-345 and security.ts CSP directives.

#### [high/M] 97 storage methods take the tenant key as an OPTIONAL parameter; 115 call sites omit it, and nothing forces the safe shape

- **Where:** `server/storage/*.ts (97 declarations matching `organizationId?: number`); measured omissions include server/routes-campaigns.ts:714, server/routes-analytics.ts:363, server/routes-ai.ts:1770`
- **Defect:** The pattern is uniform: an audit finds an IDOR, the repo method gains `organizationId?: number` and `if (organizationId) conditions.push(...)`, one call site is fixed, and the parameter stays optional forever. Every existing and future call site silently opts out by default, and TypeScript is happy. I scanned all storage declarations for an optional tenant parameter and matched call-site arity: 97 methods, 115 call sites that pass fewer arguments than the parameter's position. Most are currently saved by an org-scoped pre-fetch immediately above (e.g. server/routes-crm-extras.ts:404-412 does it correctly), which is why this reads as an architecture finding rather than 115 bugs — but findings 2, 4 and 5 above are the ones where the pre-fetch is simply absent, and there is no mechanism that would have caught them. The comment in automationRepo.ts:115-119 is the tell: it describes the tenant scoping as conditional ('when organizationId is supplied'), i.e. the fix ships with its own opt-out.
- **Fix:** Make the parameter REQUIRED on every method where the table carries `organization_id`, and let `npm run check` produce the exhaustive worklist — that turns 115 invisible risks into 115 compiler errors resolved once. Do it repo by repo. Where a genuinely cross-org call exists (jobs, founder surfaces), give it a separately named method (`…AcrossOrgs`) so the crossing is legible at the call site. This is strictly cheaper than the per-route hunt and it is the only version of the fix that is drift-proof.
- **Comparable:** Django's `Model.objects` on a multi-tenant app is conventionally replaced by a tenant-bound manager so the unscoped queryset is unreachable; Supabase/Postgres RLS achieves the same by making the database refuse. Both remove the option rather than documenting it.
- **Evidence:** Wrote and ran a scanner over server/storage/**: parsed each `async name(...)` for an optional organizationId/orgId parameter, recorded its index, then matched `storage.<name>(...)` call sites across all of server/ with balanced-paren argument counting. Output: 97 methods, 115 omitting call sites. Hand-verified a sample of ~12 (campaigns, tasks, inbox, workspace presets, notifications, briefings) to separate 'safe by pre-check' from genuinely unscoped.

#### [medium/M] `omitProtectedFields` — the canonical update-payload guard — has 3 importing files, and its documented alternative does not strip the tenant key

- **Where:** `server/utils/updatePayload.ts:1-42; only importers are server/routes-integrations.ts:15, server/routes-va-engine.ts:13, server/routes-communications.ts:15`
- **Defect:** The helper's own header says it was written because 'the tenant-isolation audit found ~25 PATCH/PUT handlers piping raw req.body into storage.update* calls' and that it 'is the minimum seam every raw-body update site must pass through'. Three files import it. The header then names the preferred alternative — `schema.partial().parse(...)` — which does NOT protect the tenant key, because 221 of 224 `createInsertSchema` declarations in shared/schema.ts leave `organizationId` in place (finding 2 is the live consequence). So the documented house rule points engineers at a seam that does not hold, and the seam that does hold is nearly unused. This is CLAUDE.md's second law — authoritative semantics without production adoption — on a security helper.
- **Fix:** Add `organizationId: true` to the `.omit()` of every insert schema whose table has an `organization_id` column (a mechanical edit; leads/properties/deals already show the shape at shared/schema.ts:3614-3640), and add a test that derives the list from the Drizzle table definitions so a new table cannot ship without it. Then either retire `omitProtectedFields` or make it the enforced seam via a lint that flags `storage.update*(…, req.body)`. Do not leave a third state where both exist and neither is required.
- **Evidence:** Read server/utils/updatePayload.ts in full; grepped importers (3 files). Counted `createInsertSchema` occurrences (224) against `organizationId: true` inside omit lists (3) in shared/schema.ts, and read the three that do it right at lines 3614-3640.

#### [medium/S] client/src/lib/sanitize.ts is a bypassable regex sanitizer, documented as the house standard, with zero callers

- **Where:** `client/src/lib/sanitize.ts:1-33`
- **Defect:** The file's doc comment presents `sanitizeHtml` as the client-side sanitizer with a copy-paste usage example (`<div dangerouslySetInnerHTML={{ __html: sanitizeHtml(html) }} />`). It is a four-regex string rewriter and is trivially defeated — `EVENT_HANDLERS` requires quoted values, so `<img src=x onerror=alert(1)>` survives intact; nested tags defeat `BLOCKED_TAGS`; SVG/MathML vectors are not considered at all. It has no importers anywhere in client/src. Every real render site correctly uses `DOMPurify.sanitize` instead (native-mail-panel.tsx:321, inbox.tsx:592, offers.tsx:476, buyer-blasts.tsx:267, field-note-detail.tsx:110, documents.tsx:1194, AssignmentPanel.tsx:609). The risk is not today's code — it is that the next engineer follows the doc comment.
- **Fix:** Delete the file. If a shared entry point is wanted, replace its body with a thin `DOMPurify.sanitize` wrapper carrying the project's allowed-tag profile, and add a lint rule that forbids `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` whose value is not that wrapper — which would also lock in the currently-good state.
- **Evidence:** Read client/src/lib/sanitize.ts in full; grepped `from "@/lib/sanitize"` and `sanitizeHtml(` across client/src — zero call sites outside the file's own doc comment. Read all 11 dangerouslySetInnerHTML sites and confirmed each is DOMPurify-wrapped or renders internal chart/JSON-LD config.

#### [medium/S] crossOrgAdminGuard is defined and never used, while 22 /api/founder/v14/:orgId routes take an org id from the URL

- **Where:** `server/routes-admin.ts:745-754 (definition); the routes it was written for at server/routes-founder-self-running-company.ts:48-259`
- **Defect:** `crossOrgAdminGuard` carries the comment 'Apply this middleware on any route that accepts :orgId in URL path' and has zero call sites in the entire server tree. Meanwhile routes-founder-self-running-company.ts registers 22 handlers that read `:orgId` straight from the URL. Those are currently safe — routes.ts:1989 mounts `isAuthenticated + getOrCreateOrg + requireFounder` on the `/api/founder/v14` prefix BEFORE they register, and a founder reading any org is the intended behaviour — but the guard's existence advertises a protection nothing has, which is how the next `:orgId` route outside a founder prefix gets written. Same class as `publicMaturityOf()` and `isFounderUserId()` in CLAUDE.md's second law.
- **Fix:** Delete it, or apply it to the non-founder `:orgId`/`:id`-taking admin routes it was meant for and add it to the file's exports so a test can assert the set of `:orgId` routes it covers. A guard with no callers should not survive a review.
- **Evidence:** Read server/routes-admin.ts:745-754; grepped `crossOrgAdminGuard` across server/ — one hit, the definition. Confirmed the /api/founder/v14 prefix guard registers at routes.ts:1989 before registerFounderV14Routes(app) at :2015, so ordering is currently correct.

#### [medium/S] WebSocket entity channels are broadcast on names no client is permitted to subscribe to — three live-update paths are dead

- **Where:** `server/websocket.ts:298-316 (isAllowedChannel) vs server/services/executionEngine.ts:88 and server/websocket.ts:386, :393`
- **Defect:** `isAllowedChannel` permits subscribing only to `deal:{orgId}:…`, `listing:{orgId}:…`, `negotiation:{orgId}:…` — an org-prefixed form added by an audit to close a prefix hole. But every producer emits the UNPREFIXED name: `wsServer.broadcast(\`deal:${dealId}\`, …)` (executionEngine.ts:88), `this.broadcast(\`negotiation:${sessionId}\`, …)` (:386), `this.broadcast(\`listing:${listingId}\`, …)` (:393). No subscriber can ever match, so those three real-time streams have been silently dead since the fix. Security posture is fine (the guard fails closed); the defect is that the guard is 'safe by being broken' and nobody noticed, which means the audit's own claim to have preserved the feature was never checked. It also means a future engineer 'fixing' the deadness by loosening the guard reintroduces the cross-org hole.
- **Fix:** Change the producers to emit the org-prefixed name (they all have the org id in scope) and add a test that derives the set of broadcast channel names from the source and asserts every one is accepted by isAllowedChannel for its owning org and rejected for another — deriving the population rather than listing it, per CLAUDE.md's third law.
- **Evidence:** Read websocket.ts:263-316, :380-400 and executionEngine.ts:88; grepped client/src for a subscribe message carrying any of these channel names — none exists, confirming the feature is inert on both sides.

#### [medium/M] The Clerk JWT fallback verifies signature/exp/sid but not issuer, audience or azp, and fails open on revocation

- **Where:** `server/auth/clerkAuth.ts:155-212 (fallback), :106-120 (checkRevokedSid)`
- **Defect:** When clerkMiddleware does not populate req.auth, hydrateUser manually verifies each `__session*` cookie: RSA-SHA256 over header+payload against CLERK_JWT_KEY, `payload.exp` with a 30s grace, and a revoked-`sid` cache lookup. It never checks `iss`, `aud`, `azp` or `nbf`. Any token signed by the same Clerk instance key is therefore accepted as a full session — which includes JWTs minted from Clerk JWT *templates* for third-party integrations, whose `sub` is the same user id. That is not a privilege escalation between users, but it collapses the distinction between 'a session cookie for acreos.io' and 'any token this Clerk instance ever signed', which is exactly the distinction `azp`/`aud` exist to preserve. Separately, `checkRevokedSid` returns 'ok' whenever the revoked-sid cache cannot be refreshed (documented at :90-104 as a deliberate reversal after a 2026-05-27 auth-loop incident) — a reasonable availability trade, but it means 'Sign out everywhere' is not enforced on this path, and the cache is capped at the 100 most recent revocations.
- **Fix:** Validate `iss` against the Clerk instance issuer and `azp` (or `aud`) against the app origin(s) in the fallback — a three-line addition that does not change availability. For revocation, keep the fail-open but bound it: record a metric when the cache is stale so a broken refresh is visible, and page on it rather than silently trusting. This path is the one an attacker with a stolen cookie rides, and it is the same path finding 10's missing MFA leaves ungated for founder routes.
- **Evidence:** Read server/auth/clerkAuth.ts:139-232 and :85-132 in full; the verification block checks only signature, payload.sub, payload.exp and payload.sid. Marked as hypothesis: I did not confirm this deployment issues JWT templates, so the concrete template-token path is theoretical — the missing claim validation is not.

#### [medium/S] CSRF double-submit token is unbound to the session and compared non-constant-time; two mutating routes register above the mount

- **Where:** `server/middleware/csrf.ts:94-148; mount at server/routes.ts:1017; routes above it at server/routes.ts:550 and :649`
- **Defect:** Three separate weaknesses in one gate. (1) The token is a random value stored in a non-httpOnly cookie and compared to a header — with no binding to the session. Any attacker who can set a cookie on the parent domain (a subdomain takeover, or one of the white-label custom domains handled by whiteLabelDomainMiddleware/customDomainRouter) can plant a known `csrf_token` and satisfy the check. (2) `cookieToken !== headerToken` is a non-constant-time comparison. (3) `app.use("/api", csrfProtection)` registers at routes.ts:1017, but `POST /api/health/uptime-probe` (:550) and `POST /api/waitlist` (:649) register above it and are therefore unprotected — both are low-value, but the ordering hazard is the same one that cost the team five admin surfaces of MFA (documented at routes.ts:1839-1849) and nothing prevents a recurrence.
- **Fix:** Bind the token to the session — HMAC the Clerk session id with a server secret and compare that — so a planted cookie value is useless; use `crypto.timingSafeEqual` on equal-length buffers. Add a registration-order test in the shape of tests/unit/adminMfaOrdering.test.ts that fails if any mutating /api route registers above the csrfProtection mount, so the guarantee is checked rather than assumed.
- **Evidence:** Read server/middleware/csrf.ts in full. Located the mount at routes.ts:1017 and enumerated method registrations between the start of registerRoutes (:378) and that line — exactly two POSTs, at :550 and :649.

#### [medium/S] The org-scoped-fetch gate is RED on HEAD: one false positive it cannot see through, plus two stale allowlist entries

- **Where:** `scripts/check-org-scoped-fetch.mjs; flagged unit server/services/paxReceiptsReader.ts:122; stale entries `alerting.ts::runDailyAlertCheck`, `leadNurturer.ts::scoreLead``
- **Defect:** Running the lint on HEAD fails. Its rule-3 finding — `listPaxReceipts()` reading `activityLog` without naming the organization — is a FALSE POSITIVE: the function does scope correctly, but composes the predicate into a local (`const scope = and(eq(activityLog.organizationId, organizationId), …)`) and the scanner only inspects the literal chain. That matters more than the red build: a load-bearing tenancy gate that cannot follow a one-hop variable teaches engineers that its failures are noise, and the documented remedy for a rule-3 hit is 'add the key to RULE3_BASELINE with the reason' — i.e. the natural response to a false positive is to widen the baseline, which is how a real offender gets admitted next time. The two stale BASELINE_OFFENDERS entries are the healthy half of the ratchet doing its job and should just be deleted.
- **Fix:** Teach the scanner to resolve a single-assignment local used as the `.where()` argument within the same function — that is the dominant idiom in this codebase and covers the whole class. Delete the two stale entries in the same commit. Do not baseline paxReceiptsReader.
- **Evidence:** Ran `node scripts/check-org-scoped-fetch.mjs`: 'rule 3 … baseline 72, new 1' plus two stale entries, exit non-zero. Read server/services/paxReceiptsReader.ts:117-145 and confirmed the org predicate is present inside the composed `scope` variable.

#### [medium/S] Three moderate npm advisories, one of them (qs) on the request path of every single HTTP request

- **Where:** `package-lock.json — express@5.2.1 → qs@6.15.2; mammoth@1.12.0 → @xmldom/xmldom@0.8.13; jspdf/posthog-js → fflate`
- **Defect:** `npm audit --production` reports 3 moderate, 0 high, 0 critical across 1,139 production dependencies. `qs@6.15.2` carries 'Denial of Service via Attacker Controlled isBuffer' and an array-limit bypass, and it parses the query string of every inbound request — unauthenticated reachability, no application code required. `@xmldom/xmldom` (XML fragment injection) is pulled by `mammoth`, which server/ai/executive.ts:1081 uses to extract text from customer-uploaded .docx files, so the parser sits directly downstream of untrusted upload content. `fflate` is client-only (jspdf, posthog-js) and lower concern.
- **Fix:** Bump express to the release that pins a patched qs (or add an npm override for qs) and bump mammoth/@xmldom/xmldom. Then wire `npm audit --production --audit-level=high` into CI so the count is a ratchet rather than something an audit discovers — the repo has excellent ratchet discipline everywhere else and none on dependencies.
- **Evidence:** Ran `npm audit --production --json` (3 moderate / 0 high / 0 critical, 1,139 prod deps) and `npm ls qs fflate @xmldom/xmldom` to establish the dependency paths. Confirmed mammoth's use at server/ai/executive.ts:26 and :1081.

#### [low/S] POST /api/territories/:id/assign accepts any userId without checking org membership

- **Where:** `server/routes-territories.ts:102-118`
- **Defect:** The route reads `userId` from the body and writes it to `territories.assignedUserId` with no verification that the user is a member of the caller's organization. The territory row itself is correctly org-scoped, so nothing crosses a tenant boundary in the write — but the org can now hold a row pointing at a foreign user id, which is the dangling-reference condition that server/routes-leads.ts:675-679 explicitly guards against for `assignedTo` (using `assertUserIsOrgMember`), for the stated reason that it creates 'dangling notifications + reporting confusion'. One route learned the lesson; its sibling did not.
- **Fix:** Add `const ok = await assertUserIsOrgMember(String(userId), org.id); if (!ok) return Errors.badRequest(res, "…")` before the update, mirroring routes-leads.ts. Then grep for the other `assignedTo`/`assignedUserId`/`ownerId` write sites and give the check a single shared helper so the seventh one cannot be missed.
- **Evidence:** Read server/routes-territories.ts:30-120 in full and compared against the guarded equivalent at server/routes-leads.ts:672-680; `assertUserIsOrgMember` exists in server/utils/orgScope.ts and is unit-tested.

## Head of Data / Analytics

**AcreOS has world-class *governance artifacts* around its data (an orphan-table register, a schema→DDL mirror gate, an honest erasure sweep) sitting on top of a measurement layer that cannot currently answer its own thesis — the autonomy funnel is entirely uninstrumented, the customer's Analytics door renders two structurally-zero KPIs and two fabricated ones, and the Pax audit trail that proves "what happened and why" is hard-deleted by a nightly cron at 90 days.**


### Already at or above the bar — do not "fix" these

- `scripts/check-schema-migrate-mirror.mjs` with an EMPTY allowlist. Getting ~165 gapped tables to zero, and *proving* it by standing up a real PostgreSQL 16, rebuilding from migrations/*.sql + migrate.mjs, and diffing 746 tables against the schema — that is a higher standard of evidence than most Series-B data teams ever apply to a migration gate. Do not touch it; only extend it downward to constraint level.
- `scripts/schema-migrate-mirror.orphans.json`. A register of every table the ORM does not model, each with a written reason, a may-only-shrink rule, and — for the fee tables — an explicit refusal to drop on the strength of "no reachable consumer" alone because production row state is unknown from this environment. This is the single best data-governance artifact in the repository and it is better than what most companies have.
- `server/services/orgDeletion.ts`. Dynamic information_schema enumeration instead of a hand-ordered list, multi-pass draining, founder-gated, refuses founder orgs, requires the exact org name typed back, documents its Art. 17(3) retentions with a lawful basis on the same line, and returns residual tables honestly rather than reporting success. Fix only its `org_id` blind spot — the method is right.
- `scripts/check-measurement-defaults.mjs`. "A measurement may not be replaced by a constant", falsified against the behaviour rather than a symbol, with its own blind spot (bare locals) written down in the file rather than left to be discovered from a false green. That self-honesty is rarer than the gate.
- The `audit_events` design (shared/schema.ts:5647-5680): UUID PK plus a `bigserial seq` added specifically because a random PK cannot order a hash chain, a global rather than per-org chain because the targets are cross-org, `prev_hash`/`row_hash` computed before insert because an append-only trigger forbids the UPDATE. Someone thought this through properly.
- `activation_events`' unique `(organization_id, event_name)` with `onConflictDoNothing` (schema.ts:6081, services/activation.ts:66) — first-occurrence-wins idempotency enforced by the database rather than by application discipline. Exactly right, and it is why moving `trial_to_paid` and `first_value_reached` off the client to server-truth actually worked.
- `server/services/paxReceiptsReader.ts`' handling of pre-marker rows: it lists them, and leaves origin/group **null** rather than guessing, with the reasoning written at the top of the file. Refusing to infer attribution that was never captured is the correct instinct and it should be the template for every backfill in this codebase.
- `getConversionRates` in server/storage/analyticsRepo.ts:334-340 returning honest-empty for per-stage rates and loss reasons because they are not tracked — in the same file as the ×1.1 projection. The right standard is already present locally; it just needs to be applied to its neighbours.
- The `marketing_spend` schema comment (shared/schema.ts:8816-8826): "Amounts are ACTUALS — never record budgets/commitments here; a budget is not spend (no-fabrication)." A constraint expressed where the next engineer will actually read it.

### Benchmarked against

- Stripe (events + Sigma): one immutable `events` namespace where every state change emits exactly one typed event with `previous_attributes` — making a "documented but never emitted" event type structurally impossible. AcreOS has 25 event tables and 19 log tables with five overlapping answers to "what happened to this record". Steal: one canonical event spine per entity, everything else a projection off it.
- Amplitude / PostHog (taxonomy governance): a tracking plan where a declared-but-unseen event is flagged as *not implemented* and rendered visually distinct from a measured zero. AcreOS pads five uninstrumented activation events as 0% bars. Steal: the not-instrumented state, and cohort-maturity filtering on every funnel denominator.
- dbt metrics / Cube / LookML (semantic layer): a metric like `conversion_rate` is defined once with an explicit grain and every surface compiles from it. AcreOS re-derives conversion rate in at least six places with different denominators, two of them on one screen. Steal: a `shared/metrics/` module that the analytics repo and every founder surface must import — the same pattern `shared/lifecycle/pipeline-status.ts` already establishes and the analytics layer already ignores.
- Ramp / Mercury (audit trail): actor + before/after retained for the life of the account on anything money- or authority-adjacent, kept structurally separate from operational exhaust that ages out. AcreOS puts Pax receipts and stance changes in the same table as page-noise and purges the lot at 90 days. Steal: the two-tier retention split.
- Linear (activity history): the feed is derived from the same immutable history rows the API returns, so a feed category cannot outlive its producer. AcreOS ships three /activity filter tabs with zero writers. Steal: derive the UI's event vocabulary from the writers, and gate it.
- Segment Protocols / Snowplow (schema-on-ingest): an event whose type is not in the plan is rejected at the door. `routes-communications.ts:805` writes a client-supplied `eventType` verbatim into the table the customer timeline groups by. Steal: validate every enumerated write against the shared Zod schema that already exists.
- Supabase / PlanetScale / Atlas / Squawk (migration hygiene): an applied-migrations ledger is mandatory and migrations are linted in CI for constraints that the deploy path will not execute. AcreOS has no ledger, a deleted journal, and 561 index names in migrations/*.sql that migrate.mjs never runs. Steal: the ledger table first — it converts archaeology into a query.
- Notion / Figma / Stripe (DSAR): both the export and the erasure plan are generated from the schema catalog and asserted for completeness in CI. AcreOS's erasure is dynamic and excellent; its export is a seven-table hand list. Steal: make the two share one enumeration helper.
- BigQuery policy tags / Snowflake object tagging / AWS Macie (classification): sensitivity is applied by scanning the catalog, not by a curated file, because a curated file fails silently. AcreOS classifies 46 columns and gates 4 tables out of 724.

### Findings


#### [critical/M] Every Pax receipt — the entire "what happened and why" record — is hard-deleted at 90 days by a nightly cron

- **Where:** `server/jobs/dataRetention.ts:19 (rule) + server/services/paxReceipts.ts:131 (writer) + server/services/paxReceiptsReader.ts:29 (reader)`
- **Defect:** AUTONOMY_SPEC.md §4.7 puts every Pax effect into `activity_log` via `recordPaxEffect` → `storage.logActivity({agentType:'pax'})`. `dataRetention.ts:19` runs `DELETE FROM activity_log WHERE created_at < now()-90d` with no `agent_type` filter, nightly at 03:30 UTC, registered `critical: true` in `server/jobs/jobRegistry.ts:287`. So: the receipt for a send Pax made 91 days ago is gone, but the append-only `pax_sends` row it describes survives (pax_sends is not in the retention list) — the system retains the fact that a send happened and destroys the attribution explaining who authorised it, under which stance, and why. The same purge destroys the only record of a stance change: `routes-pax-controls.ts:653` writes the before/after of a `PATCH /controls` as a receipt into `activity_log` and nowhere else. "Did this customer tighten Pax after it made a mistake?" is the single most important behavioural signal in the autonomy program and it self-destructs on a 90-day rolling window. It is also customer-data deletion performed by a job rather than by the founder, against a hard-stop that names customer-data deletion as founder-only.
- **Fix:** Split the receipt lane out of `activity_log` before wave 2 lands more writers on it. Either (a) exclude `agent_type='pax'` rows from the retention rule and give receipts their own longer floor tied to the org's subscription lifetime, or (b) give `pax_receipts` its own append-only table (mirroring `pax_sends`) so the purge cannot reach it. Separately, record stance changes as their own durable rows (`pax_controls_history`: orgId, before, after, actorUserId, at) — a jsonb column that overwrites itself is not a history. Whatever window is chosen, state it verbatim on the "What Pax did" surface; a receipts list that silently shortens is a truth defect under the no-fabrication rule.
- **Comparable:** Ramp and Mercury retain the actor+before/after audit row for the life of the account for anything money- or authority-adjacent, and separate "operational exhaust" (which ages out) from "the record of a decision" (which does not). Linear keeps immutable `history` rows per issue forever and derives the activity feed from them, so the feed can never be more truthful than the store or vice versa.
- **Evidence:** Read dataRetention.ts:16-34 (the rules array, no agent_type predicate), jobRegistry.ts:287 (`{ name: "data_retention", intervalMs: DAY, critical: true, cron: "30 3 * * *" }`), paxReceipts.ts:6-8 and :131, paxReceiptsReader.ts:1-30, routes-pax-controls.ts:617-676. `pax_sends` does not appear in retentionRules.

#### [critical/S] "Deals in Pipeline" and "Pipeline value" filter on deal statuses that are not in the canonical vocabulary and that nothing writes — two customer KPIs are structurally zero

- **Where:** `server/storage/analyticsRepo.ts:50 and :312-317; canonical list at shared/lifecycle/pipeline-status.ts:72-80; column default at shared/schema.ts:1388`
- **Defect:** `deals.status` defaults to `"negotiating"` (schema.ts:1388) and the canonical `DEAL_STATUSES` is `[negotiating, offer_sent, countered, accepted, in_escrow, closed, cancelled]`. `getExecutiveMetrics` counts the pipeline with `or(eq(deals.status,'negotiation'), eq(...,'pending'), eq(...,'due_diligence'), eq(...,'under_contract'))` and `getPipelineValue` repeats the identical four-value list. **None of those four strings is in DEAL_STATUSES**, and `'negotiation'` is a typo for the default `'negotiating'`. A grep across server/ for `'negotiation'` finds only these two analytics queries and an unrelated union type in voiceLearning.ts:50 — nothing writes it. `POST` stage changes are validated against DEAL_STATUSES at routes.ts:1625-1627, so a deal can never legitimately hold three of those four values. Net effect: the "Deals in Pipeline" KPI card and the pipeline-value chart read 0 for every organization, forever. `getDealMetrics`:200-205 has the mirror defect — it counts lost deals as `status IN ('dead','cancelled')`, and `'dead'` is a LEAD status (pipeline-status.ts LEAD list), not a deal status, so the win-rate denominator is wrong too.
- **Fix:** Import `DEAL_STATUSES` / a derived `ACTIVE_DEAL_STATUSES` from `shared/lifecycle/pipeline-status.ts` into `analyticsRepo.ts` and delete every inline status literal there (and the re-declared `ACTIVE_DEAL_STATUSES` at routes-founder-bridge.ts:93). Then add the gate that would have caught it: a test that asserts every status literal compared against `deals.status` anywhere in server/ is a member of `DEAL_STATUSES`, with the file list enumerated in the test so a new analytics file must be added to it. Mutation-probe it by renaming one canonical member and watching it go red.
- **Comparable:** This is the exact shape the constitution's second law names — a canonical projection with no adoption in the surface that needs it. Looker/LookML and dbt metrics exist precisely so a stage list is declared once; Cube's semantic layer makes `status IN (...)` in a chart query a compile error.
- **Evidence:** Read analyticsRepo.ts:46-51, :110-119, :200-206, :303-320; shared/lifecycle/pipeline-status.ts:70-98; shared/schema.ts:1383-1389. Grepped `'negotiation'` across server/ — two hits, both in analyticsRepo. Grepped `DEAL_STATUSES` importers — routes.ts, contractAssembly.ts, routes-founder-bridge.ts (which re-declares its own list instead).

#### [critical/M] The right-to-erasure sweep is blind to 40 tables because it enumerates only `organization_id`, not `org_id`

- **Where:** `server/services/orgDeletion.ts:56-67 (`tablesWithOrgColumn`, `WHERE c.column_name = 'organization_id'`)`
- **Defect:** The sweep is well designed — dynamic information_schema enumeration, multi-pass, honest residual reporting — but its population is defined by one literal column name. 40 pgTables in the schema name the tenant key `org_id` instead: `borrower_messages` (schema.ts:8179 — borrower message bodies), `user_sessions` and `user_activation_events` and `user_feedback` (14715-14745 — per-user page-view history), `integration_credentials` (16362 — encrypted credential store), `agent_episodic_memory` / `agent_semantic_memory` / `agent_working_memory_v13` (the AI's memory of the org), `founder_interactions`, `scp_*` memory tables, `onboarding_funnel_metrics`. None of these is in `RETAINED_TABLES` (which lists only audit_events, financial_ledger, mrr_snapshots with a documented lawful basis), so they are not deliberate retentions — they are invisible. A deletion run returns `residualTables: []` and `success: true` while borrower message content and the agent's memory of that tenant remain. The identical blind spot exists in `scripts/check-org-leading-index.mjs`, which documents it explicitly ("Tables that use a column name other than organizationId … aren't picked up") — so two independent gates share one population hole.
- **Fix:** Widen the enumeration to `c.column_name IN ('organization_id','org_id')` and re-run against a restored snapshot to see the new residual set. Then close the root cause: add a schema lint that fails on any new `pgTable` whose tenant FK is named `org_id`, and ratchet the existing 40 down. Add a completeness assertion to the deletion test that derives the expected table set from `shared/schema*.ts` (every table with a tenant column) rather than from the deleter's own query — otherwise the test agrees with the deleter about the population, which is the failure mode.
- **Comparable:** Notion and Figma generate the DSAR/erasure plan from the schema catalog, not from a query the deleter wrote; the erasure job's coverage is asserted against the catalog so a new table is a failing build, not a silent survivor.
- **Evidence:** Read orgDeletion.ts:1-67 in full. Enumerated `orgId: <type>("org_id"` across shared/schema.ts + shared/schema/*.ts with a script — 40 tables, listed above. Read check-org-leading-index.mjs:56-61 where the same limitation is written down.

#### [critical/M] Payment- and webhook-replay UNIQUE constraints exist only in migrations/*.sql, which is not the path production runs

- **Where:** `migrations/0023_payment_race_condition.sql:9 (`payments_transaction_id_unique`), migrations/0034_twilio_messagesid_unique.sql (`messages_external_id_unique`), migrations/0025_credit_allowance_month_unique.sql; vs scripts/migrate.mjs (the Fly release_command)`
- **Defect:** drizzle.config.ts:1-11 states plainly that migrations do NOT run through the drizzle migrator, that the journal was deleted on 2026-05-11, and that `scripts/migrate.mjs` is the authoritative path. `migrations/meta/` holds only four stale snapshots and no `_journal.json`. So a statement that lives only in a `migrations/*.sql` file has no execution path to production — the orphans register confirms this pattern in its own words ("migrations/0016 was never mirrored into migrate.mjs (the only path production runs)"). I scanned all 255 migration files for index names and cross-referenced migrate.mjs: 561 of 967 distinct index names appear nowhere in migrate.mjs, and 19 of those files are numbered past the deleted journal's last entry, i.e. they never ran under any migrator either. Three of the unmirrored ones are not performance indexes but data-integrity constraints written explicitly as race/replay defenses: the partial unique on `payments.transaction_id` ("prevents duplicate payment records for the same Stripe session"), the partial unique on `messages.external_id` ("Twilio retries … a malicious replay would otherwise create duplicate rows … downstream side effects double-fire"), and the credit-allowance month uniqueness. Two of the three are at least declared in shared/schema.ts (schema.ts:2267, :2423) so a `db:push` may have created them; `payments.transaction_id` uses a column-level `.unique()` (schema.ts:1705) which is a different, non-partial shape than the migration's. Whether they exist in prod today is unknowable from this repo, which is itself the finding.
- **Fix:** Two things, in order. (1) Create an applied-migration ledger: a `schema_migrations` table that migrate.mjs writes a row into per statement-batch, so "what is actually in prod" is a query rather than an archaeology exercise. (2) Extend the schema-migrate-mirror gate from TABLE level to CONSTRAINT/INDEX level for the subset that carries integrity semantics (UNIQUE, CHECK, NOT NULL, FK) — those are the ones whose absence is silent and expensive. Its own header already names column-level drift as the deliberately-unscanned half; UNIQUE constraints are the part of that half that cannot wait for the DB-backed dry-run.
- **Comparable:** Supabase, PlanetScale and Atlas all treat the applied-migration ledger as non-optional, and Squawk/Atlas lint migrations in CI for exactly this class (a constraint added in a file that the deploy path does not execute). Stripe's schema tooling refuses a deploy whose declared constraints are not provably present.
- **Evidence:** Read drizzle.config.ts in full; scripts/migrate.mjs header lines 1-40; migrations/0023 and 0034 in full; `ls migrations/meta/` (no _journal.json). Wrote a script that extracted every `CREATE [UNIQUE] INDEX` name from migrations/*.sql and tested membership in migrate.mjs: 967 distinct, 561 absent; the post-0018 subset is 19 files, listed. HYPOTHESIS (not verified, no prod access): the constraints may exist in prod via an earlier `db:push`. The structural claim — no repository path applies them — is verified.

#### [high/S] Three of the four Executive KPI cards render a hardcoded 0 as a green "+0.0% from last period" trend, and export it to CSV

- **Where:** `server/storage/analyticsRepo.ts:73,75,77 → client/src/components/analytics-content.tsx:88-95 (KPICard) and :304-311 (CSV export)`
- **Defect:** `getExecutiveMetrics` returns `notesValueChange: 0`, `dealsChange: 0`, `conversionChange: 0` — never computed, hardcoded. `KPICard` renders `change !== undefined` as a trend row, and `change >= 0` selects `text-acr-pos` plus a `TrendingUp` icon and a `'+'` prefix. So Active Notes Value, Deals in Pipeline and Lead Conversion Rate each display a green upward-trending **"+0.0% from last period"** on every load, for every customer, forever. `handleExportReport` then pushes all three into the CSV as "Notes value change %", "Deals change %", "Conversion change %". This is a placeholder presented as a measurement, on a customer surface, in an exportable artifact — the no-fabrication hard-stop.
- **Fix:** Compute the three deltas the way `revenueChange` already is (a prior-window query), or return `undefined` and let KPICard omit the row — it already handles that. Do not ship a zero. Then extend `scripts/check-measurement-defaults.mjs`: it catches `measurement ?? constant` but is blind to `measurementField: 0` in a returned object literal; a rule over return-object keys whose names end in `Change`/`Rate`/`Score` bound to a numeric literal would catch this whole family.
- **Comparable:** Amplitude and Mixpanel render "—" and a "no comparison period" tooltip when a delta cannot be computed; neither will ever paint a green up-arrow for an uncomputed value. That distinction between "zero" and "not measured" is the core discipline of the discipline.
- **Evidence:** Read analyticsRepo.ts:69-78 and client/src/components/analytics-content.tsx:57-97, :295-315, :391-419.

#### [high/S] "Projected revenue" is `totalRevenue × 1.1` — an invented growth assumption on a customer surface and in the CSV export

- **Where:** `server/storage/analyticsRepo.ts:119`
- **Defect:** `projectedRevenue: totalRevenue * 1.1`. A hardcoded 10% growth multiplier presented to the customer as a projection and pushed into the exported report at analytics-content.tsx:315 as "Projected revenue". Neither fabrication gate sees it: `lint:no-fabrication` scans for `Math.random`, and `check-measurement-defaults` (by its own documented discriminator) matches `property ?? non-zero-literal`, not a multiplication. Note the same file gets this exactly right 40 lines later — `getConversionRates` returns honest-empty with the comment "Per-stage conversion rates … are NOT tracked, so we return honest-empty rather than fabricating". The standard exists in the file; this line violates it.
- **Fix:** Delete the field and the card, or replace it with the real thing — `computeProjectedMRR` in analytics-content.tsx already derives a growth rate from the observed 14-day-over-14-day series, which is a defensible method; if a projection ships, it must come from data and must carry its method and confidence. Add the multiplication shape to `check-measurement-defaults` (a measurement-vocabulary identifier multiplied by a literal in the 1.0–2.0 band is almost always a fabricated projection).
- **Comparable:** Every serious BI surface (Looker, Tableau, Amplitude forecasting) requires the projection to name its model and show an interval. A flat ×1.1 would not pass review at any of them.
- **Evidence:** Read analyticsRepo.ts:108-120 and :334-340 (the honest-empty counter-example); analytics-content.tsx:312-316; scripts/check-measurement-defaults.mjs:1-55 (its stated discriminator).

#### [high/M] Customer-facing "Attribution & ROI" computes cost from hardcoded industry averages × an assumed 3 touches, and labels share-of-conversions as "conversion rate"

- **Where:** `server/services/attributionService.ts:55-64 (`costPerChannel`), :175-183 (the ROI and rate assignment); rendered by client/src/components/attribution-analytics.tsx:95-97`
- **Defect:** Two independent defects in the same six lines.
