(1) `row.totalCost = costPerChannel(row.channel, row.conversions * 3); // assume 3 touches avg` — the cost, and therefore the ROI the customer reads and color-codes green/amber/red (`roiColor`, attribution-analytics.tsx:81-86), is `conversions × 3 × a "rough industry estimate"` ($0.85/mail piece, $0.003/email, $0.015/SMS). AcreOS holds the *real* numbers: `mailing_orders`/`mailing_order_pieces` carry actual Lob spend, `usage_records.total_cost_cents` carries per-action cost, `marketing_spend` exists specifically as "the CAC numerator" and its schema comment insists "Amounts are ACTUALS". The ROI panel ignores all of it. (2) `row.conversionRate = row.conversions / conversions.length` is the campaign's **share of total conversions**, not a rate — and `totalLeads` on the same row is initialised to 0 and never incremented, so the true denominator was never even collected. A campaign that converted 3 of 3 leads and one that converted 3 of 300 render identically.
- **Fix:** Join actual send counts and actual costs (mailing_order_pieces, usage_records, provider invoices) instead of `conversions * 3`; if a channel has no cost ledger, return `costAvailable: false` and refuse the ROI rather than estimating it — the pattern `getConversionRates` and the `marketing_spend` comment already establish. Populate `totalLeads` from the campaign's audience and make `conversionRate = conversions / totalLeads`; rename the current quantity to `shareOfConversions` if it is still wanted.
- **Comparable:** HubSpot and Ramp both refuse to render an ROI when the cost side is unconnected — they show "connect your spend source" instead of an estimate. Northbeam/Triple Whale treat modeled cost as a distinctly-labelled series, never as the headline number.
- **Evidence:** Read attributionService.ts:29, :55-64, :95-183 and attribution-analytics.tsx:81-118. Read the marketing_spend schema comment at shared/schema.ts:8816-8826 ("Amounts are ACTUALS — never record budgets/commitments here; a budget is not spend (no-fabrication)").

#### [high/S] Two different "conversion rate" numbers on one screen, computed from mismatched cohorts, one of which ignores the date-range picker entirely

- **Where:** `server/storage/analyticsRepo.ts:56-67 (executive) vs :137-147 (leads); both rendered by client/src/components/analytics-content.tsx (KPI card :414-418 and the Leads section)`
- **Defect:** Executive: numerator = leads with `status='closed'` AND `updatedAt >= startDate`; denominator = leads with `createdAt >= startDate`. Different cohorts — a lead created two years ago and closed today is in the numerator and not the denominator, so the KPI can exceed 100%. Leads: numerator = ALL closed leads, denominator = ALL leads, `dateRange` destructured and then never used for either. So (a) the two rates differ on the same screen, (b) both are exported to the same CSV under near-identical labels ("Lead conversion rate %" at :310 and "Conversion rate %" at :320), and (c) moving the range selector from 30d to 1y changes one number and not the other — which reads to the customer as a bug in the range picker. Separately at :85-95, daily revenue is bucketed with `DATE(payments.payment_date)` where `payment_date` is a `timestamp` without timezone (schema.ts:1702), so day boundaries follow the database server's timezone, not the organization's.
- **Fix:** Define `lead_conversion_rate` once — cohort-based: of leads *created* in the window, the share that reached `closed` (ever, or within N days; pick one and name it in the UI). Have both endpoints call that one function. Apply `dateRange` in `getLeadMetrics` or remove the range selector's influence over that section explicitly. For bucketing, store an org timezone and bucket with `DATE(payment_date AT TIME ZONE $orgTz)`.
- **Comparable:** This is what a semantic layer exists to prevent — dbt metrics / Cube / LookML define `conversion_rate` once with an explicit cohort grain, and every tile compiles from it. AcreOS has 272 route files re-deriving metrics by hand and no metric registry anywhere in shared/.
- **Evidence:** Read analyticsRepo.ts:21-78, :121-181, :80-120; analytics-content.tsx:295-330, :391-419.

#### [high/S] `user_activation_events` has no writer in the product — the growth agent's founder briefs and the admin activation dashboard report empty/0% forever

- **Where:** `server/services/betaAnalytics.ts:94-137 (writer + readers), server/routes-admin.ts:4556-4570 (the only writer route), server/agents/growth.ts:57-59 and :103-108`
- **Defect:** `user_activation_events` (schema.ts:14724) is a *second* activation store parallel to the canonical `activation_events` (schema.ts:6073). Its only writer is `betaAnalytics.trackActivation`, called from exactly one place: `POST /api/analytics/activation` (routes-admin.ts:4556). No client code calls that endpoint — `beta-activation-detector.tsx` (the only client of that route family) calls `/session/start`, `/session/end` and `/pageview` and never `/activation`. So the table is never written. Three surfaces read it anyway: the growth agent's *daily* founder brief reports `activations: {}` (growth.ts:57-59), its *weekly* brief reports an empty top-events list (:103-108), and `betaAnalytics.getActivationRates()` (:118-137) divides zero by `COUNT(organizations)` and returns a clean `0%` for every event — rendered through `GET /api/admin/beta-analytics` (routes-admin.ts:4636). A founder reading the daily brief sees "no activations yesterday" as a fact about the business, when it is a fact about the wiring.
- **Fix:** Delete `user_activation_events`, `betaAnalytics.trackActivation/getActivationEvents/getActivationRates` and the `/api/analytics/activation` route; repoint `growth.ts` and the beta-analytics endpoint at `activation_events` / `getActivationFunnel`, which is the instrumented one. One activation store, one definition. If the table holds rows in prod, that is a founder-gated drop under the deletion hard-stop — register it in `schema-migrate-mirror.orphans.json` with the reason, exactly as the existing register does.
- **Comparable:** Amplitude/PostHog taxonomy governance: an event defined in the tracking plan with zero volume for N days is flagged as "not implemented", never silently rendered as 0%. The distinction between an uninstrumented event and a measured zero is the first thing an analytics team enforces.
- **Evidence:** Read betaAnalytics.ts:80-137; routes-admin.ts:4515-4570 and :4629-4645; growth.ts:40-130; client/src/components/beta-activation-detector.tsx in full (three fetches, none to /activation); grepped `api/analytics/` across client/src.

#### [high/M] Five canonical activation events have no emitter, and the founder funnel pads them as 0% bars indistinguishable from a real zero

- **Where:** `shared/schema.ts:6036-6042 (ACTIVATION_EVENTS), server/routes-activation.ts:46-60 (the padding)`
- **Defect:** Of the 21 events in `ACTIVATION_EVENTS`, five have zero emitters anywhere in server/ (grepped both quote styles, excluding tests): `first_comp_run`, `first_offer_drafted`, `first_motivation_score_seen`, `first_pax_question_asked`, `first_payment_recorded`. These are precisely the seven the schema comment calls "the first true aha moments … the leading indicators that decide whether day-7 retention will hold." `routes-activation.ts:47-60` then deliberately pads every unfired canonical name into the response with `orgsHit: 0, pct: 0` "so the UI always shows the full event list (with 0% bars)" — so five bars render as measured zeros. `first_pax_question_asked` being uninstrumented is the sharpest one: the product's whole thesis is Pax, and the funnel cannot say how many orgs ever asked it anything.
- **Fix:** Either emit the five (each is a one-line `recordActivationEventAsync` at an existing seam: the comps route, the offer-draft path, the motivation-score render, the Pax chat entry, and the manual bookkeeping payment insert), or delete them from `ACTIVATION_EVENTS`. Do not pad. If a name must appear with no data, tag it `instrumented: false` in the payload and render it visually distinct from 0% — never as a bar.
- **Comparable:** Snowplow and Segment Protocols validate against a tracking plan and surface "declared but never seen" as a distinct violation class. A 0% bar for an uninstrumented step is the single most expensive lie an analytics surface can tell a founder, because it looks like a product problem and is a wiring problem.
- **Evidence:** Read shared/schema.ts:5995-6090 (the full list and its rationale comments); routes-activation.ts:1-60; services/activation.ts:47-78. Ran a per-event grep for `'name'|"name"` across server/ excluding tests: counts 0,0,0,0,0 for the five named; `trial_to_paid` → webhookHandlers.ts:503 (present); `first_value_reached` → approvalKernel.ts:479 (present).

#### [high/S] The activation funnel's denominator is every organization ever created, with no cohort-maturity filter and no founder-org exclusion — and a sibling surface uses a different denominator

- **Where:** `server/services/activation.ts:99-119 (`org_base`, `totals`), vs server/agents/growth.ts:50-56`
- **Defect:** `getActivationFunnel(windowDays)` computes `total_orgs = COUNT(*) FROM organizations` and divides every event's distinct-org count by it. Two problems. (1) No maturity filter: an org that signed up yesterday is in the denominator of the 30-day funnel even though it has 29 days left to convert, so every percentage is systematically understated and drifts downward as signups arrive — the funnel gets "worse" precisely when acquisition is working. (2) No `is_founder` exclusion — the founder's own org and any internal orgs are counted as customers. The growth agent, measuring the same business, *does* filter (`WHERE is_founder = false`, growth.ts:52 and :56). So two founder-facing surfaces report signup/activation numbers off two different populations and neither states which.
- **Fix:** Restrict `org_base` to `WHERE is_founder = false AND created_at <= now() - (windowDays * INTERVAL '1 day')` — a cohort-mature denominator — and return `cohortSize` plus the cohort's date range in the payload so the founder can see what population the percentage is over. Then make it the one definition: have `growth.ts` call `getActivationFunnel` rather than issuing its own COUNT.
- **Comparable:** Amplitude and Mixpanel funnels refuse to include users who have not had the full conversion window available, and label the cohort explicitly. Every retention/activation tool makes cohort maturity a first-class, visible parameter — it is the most common way a self-built funnel understates itself.
- **Evidence:** Read services/activation.ts:88-130 (the full CTE) and agents/growth.ts:48-80.

#### [high/M] The customer timeline is structurally incomplete: 8 of 12 activity writers have zero call sites, so three of the five /activity filter tabs are permanently empty

- **Where:** `server/services/activityLogger.ts (whole file) vs client/src/pages/activity.tsx:75-84 (FILTER_TABS)`
- **Defect:** `ActivityLoggerService` exposes 12 typed writers into `activity_events`. Call-site counts across server/ and client/ excluding tests: `logSMSSent` 0, `logDirectMailSent` 0, `logCallMade` 0, `logCallReceived` 0, `logNoteAdded` 0, `logStageChanged` 0, `logPaymentReceived` 0, `logDocumentUploaded` 0. `logEmailSent` has exactly one caller (inboundEmailService.ts:160) and `logEvent` three (routes-organization.ts ×3, all `playbook_*`). Meanwhile `/activity` ships tabs built on those very event types: **Contacts** = `[call_made, note_added, sms_sent]`, **Offers** = `[offer_sent, offer_accepted, offer_rejected, stage_changed]`, **Payments** = `[payment_received]`, **Communications** = `[email_sent, mail_sent]`. Not one of `call_made / note_added / sms_sent / offer_* / stage_changed / payment_received / mail_sent` has a writer. Three tabs are dead on arrival and the fourth is near-empty. The only event types actually written are `contact_recorded`, `playbook_started/step_completed/completed`, `email_sent` (inbound path only), and whatever a client sends — `routes-communications.ts:805` passes `eventType` from the request body straight into the column with no validation, so the vocabulary the UI keys on is client-writable free text.
- **Fix:** Pick the six seams that matter (SMS send, mail send, call logged, stage change, payment recorded, document upload) and call the existing writers there — the functions are already written and typed. Validate `eventType` in routes-communications against the `ActivityEventType` union with zod. Then install the gate that makes it stick: a test that asserts every event type named in `FILTER_TABS`/`EVENT_META` has at least one production writer, with the writer list enumerated in the test so a new tab without a writer fails.
- **Comparable:** Linear's activity feed is derived from the same immutable history rows the API and webhooks emit, so a feed category cannot exist without a producer. Stripe's `events` object has exactly one namespace and every state change produces one — the impossibility of a "documented but unemitted" event type is a design property, not a discipline.
- **Evidence:** Read services/activityLogger.ts in full; ran a per-method call-site count across server/ and client/ excluding tests (results above); read client/src/pages/activity.tsx:75-110 and components/activity-feed.tsx:28-67; read routes.ts:1243-1249, routes-communications.ts:800-815, routes-organization.ts:305-400.

#### [high/M] The autonomy funnel — the company's core thesis — has no instrumentation of any kind

- **Where:** `shared/schema.ts:2679-2707 (`pending_actions`); no aggregate reader exists anywhere in server/`
- **Defect:** `pending_actions` is the review-queue spine: it carries `status` (pending/approved/executed/expired/rejected), `origin`, `toolName`, `createdAt`, `executedAt`, `expiresAt`. Grepping every reference to `pendingActions` across server/ excluding tests, the only non-CRUD read is a single `select({ sourceRef })` at routes-pax-insights.ts:468. **Nothing anywhere aggregates status, origin or tool.** So the company cannot currently answer any of: the approval rate (do customers approve what Pax proposes?), the expiry rate (how many asks die unattended — i.e. how often Pax proposed and nothing happened), median time-to-decision, approval rate *by capability group* (which capabilities have earned trust and which have not), the distribution of stances across orgs, or how often orgs pause and for how long. Those six numbers are the entire evidence base for founder question #1 in AUTONOMY_SPEC §9 ("Pax-written messages — ever unattended?"), which is explicitly framed as revisitable "after N consecutive approvals with zero edits" — a threshold nothing counts. The schema is fine; the measurement is simply absent. Note the related schema choice: stance lives in `organizations.pax_controls` jsonb (schema.ts:253) with no index and no history table, so even a one-off stance census is a seq scan over a jsonb key, and stance *changes* survive only as 90-day `activity_log` receipts (see the retention finding).
- **Fix:** One founder-gated read model — a single `getAutonomyFunnel()` over `pending_actions` grouped by `status × origin × tool_name × week`, plus a stance census. Ship it behind the existing Controls door (`/founder/autopilot/control`) as a section, not a new route — the four-door rule holds. Emit `pax_ask_proposed / approved / rejected / expired / revised` as server-side rows too so the funnel survives the receipt purge. This is the highest-value analytics work available in the codebase right now: it is the only instrument that can tell the founder whether the autonomy thesis is landing, and it is roughly two days of work over data that already exists.
- **Comparable:** HubSpot's Agent Inbox and Zapier's agent runs both ship an approval-rate and time-to-decision view as the primary operator instrument; Relevance surfaces per-capability approval history as the input to widening autonomy. Anthropic's own agent-permission surfaces treat "how often was this approved" as the datum that justifies loosening a permission.
- **Evidence:** Read shared/schema.ts:2679-2730; grepped `pendingActions` across server/ excluding tests and filtered for select/count/group — one hit (routes-pax-insights.ts:468, a sourceRef read). Read routes-pax-controls.ts in full for the four control routes — none aggregates. Read AUTONOMY_SPEC.md §9 for the thresholds that would need counting.

#### [high/L] 81 tables declare `organization_id` with no foreign key — including the accounting ledger, ACH mandates, API keys and the Pax audit tables

- **Where:** `shared/schema.ts and shared/schema/*.ts — 81 tables; e.g. pending_actions:2681, pax_sends:2715, proof_receipts:2736, api_keys (schema/public-api.ts), account_ledger_entries + chart_of_accounts (schema/accounting-ops.ts), ach_mandates + ach_debit_attempts (schema/ach-autopay.ts)`
- **Defect:** Of the tables carrying `organizationId: integer("organization_id")`, 282 declare `.references(() => organizations.id)` and 81 do not. The missing ones are not peripheral: `account_ledger_entries` and `chart_of_accounts` are the accounting spine, `ach_mandates`/`ach_debit_attempts` are the ACH authorization record, `api_keys`/`api_key_usage` are credentials, and `pending_actions`/`pax_sends`/`proof_receipts` are the autonomy audit chain. With no FK there is no cascade, no ON DELETE semantics, and nothing prevents a row pointing at an org id that does not exist — so a bad write, a restore, or a partial erasure can leave ledger entries and ACH mandates orphaned from any tenant, which is both a correctness and a compliance problem. The erasure sweep does reach them (they use the canonical column name), but it reaches them by string match rather than by declared relationship, so the deletion order is discovered by trial rather than known.
- **Fix:** Add the FK to the money- and authority-carrying subset first (accounting-ops, ach-autopay, public-api, the three pax/proof tables) with `ON DELETE CASCADE` or `RESTRICT` chosen per table, mirrored into `scripts/migrate.mjs` in the same commit; validate against a restored snapshot before adding NOT VALID → VALIDATE. Then add a schema lint — the pattern of `check-org-leading-index.mjs` — that fails on a NEW table declaring `organization_id` without `.references()`, with the current 81 as a down-only baseline.
- **Comparable:** This is the gap migration 0024_cascade_critical_fks.sql already started closing for a subset. Stripe/Ramp-grade schemas treat the tenant FK as non-negotiable on anything ledger- or authority-shaped, because a row that cannot name its owner cannot be deleted, exported, or audited correctly.
- **Evidence:** Wrote a parser over shared/schema.ts + shared/schema/*.ts splitting on `export const X = pgTable("` and testing whether the `organizationId: integer("organization_id")` declaration is followed by `references(`: 282 with, 81 without; full list captured, representative names cited above.

#### [medium/M] The PII classification gate reads 4 hardcoded tables; 35 tables and 64 PII-shaped columns sit outside its population

- **Where:** `tests/unit/dataClassificationRegistry.test.ts:38-43 (`GUARDED_TABLES`), shared/data-classification.ts:62-129 (`CLASSIFICATION_REGISTRY`, ~46 entries)`
- **Defect:** The registry is the declared single source of truth for "how sensitive is this field", consumed by `redactByClassification` (one production caller: server/utils/customerAudit.ts). Its enforcement gate iterates a literal four-element list: `users`, `organizations`, `leads`, `support_tickets`. Scanning all 724 pgTables for PII-shaped column names outside those four finds 35 tables / 64 columns with no classification: `borrower_payment_profiles.email/phone`, `signatures.signer_email/ip_address`, `signing_consent_audit.signer_email/ip_address`, `parcel_snapshots.owner_address/mailing_address/site_address`, `buyer_reservations.buyer_email/buyer_phone`, `inbox_messages.sender_email/recipient_email`, `campaign_responses.contact_email/contact_phone`, `mailing_order_pieces.recipient_address_line_1/2`, `borrower_sessions.email/ip_address`, `team_members.email`, `audit_log.ip_address`. Adding a fifth table full of PII is invisible to the gate — which is exactly the third law: the predicate is right and the population is an unstated assumption.
- **Fix:** Derive `GUARDED_TABLES` from the schema instead of listing it: import every exported pgTable, run `looksLikePII` over every column, and require a registry entry. Add a per-table vacuity assertion (each table must contribute at least one checked column, or be explicitly exempted with a reason) so a parser that stops matching one table cannot read as that table being clean. Then classify the 64.
- **Comparable:** BigQuery policy tags, Snowflake object tagging and AWS Macie all classify by scanning the catalog, not by a curated file — because the failure mode of a curated file is silence. Also worth noting: `shared/data-classification.ts` and `shared/dataClassification.ts` are two unrelated concepts one character apart, and collide on a case-insensitive filesystem.
- **Evidence:** Read shared/data-classification.ts:1-60 and the registry block (counted 46 `"table.column": class` entries); read tests/unit/dataClassificationRegistry.test.ts:1-66 in full. Wrote a parser over all schema files matching column names against the same PII vocabulary the registry uses — 35 tables / 64 columns outside the four guarded ones.

#### [medium/M] GDPR data export is a hand-maintained list of 7 tables against a 724-table schema, while erasure next door is dynamic

- **Where:** `server/services/gdprService.ts:20-33 (imports), :80 (`exportUserData`), :40-56 (`GdprExportData` shape)`
- **Defect:** `exportUserData` compiles "all personal data for a user" from `users, leads, deals, properties, tasks, messages, supportTickets` — seven tables. `server/services/orgDeletion.ts` proves the right method is available in this codebase (enumerate from `information_schema`, sweep, report residuals honestly). The export does not use it. So a DSAR response omits, at minimum: `borrower_messages`, `signatures`/`signing_consent_audit`, `campaign_responses`, `inbox_messages`, `activity_events`, `lead_consent_events` (the TCPA consent exhibit the schema comment calls "the table the plaintiff's expert will subpoena first"), `call_transcripts`, and `pax_sends`. An incomplete DSAR is a regulatory exposure, and — unlike the erasure path — nothing in the response tells the requester what was omitted.
- **Fix:** Rebuild the export on the same dynamic enumeration the deleter uses (tables carrying a user or tenant key), route every value through `redactByClassification` so class labels travel with the export, and return an explicit `omittedTables` list with a stated lawful basis for each, mirroring `orgDeletion`'s `residualTables` honesty. The two functions should share one table-enumeration helper so they cannot drift apart.
- **Comparable:** Notion, Figma and Stripe all generate the DSAR bundle from the schema catalog; the completeness of the export is asserted in CI against the catalog rather than trusted to a list someone maintains.
- **Evidence:** Read gdprService.ts:1-80 (the header enumerating its own deletion order, the import list, the export type) and orgDeletion.ts:1-70 for the contrasting dynamic approach.

#### [medium/L] No enum types and almost no CHECK constraints — status vocabularies are free text, and one route writes a client-supplied event type verbatim

- **Where:** `shared/schema.ts (3 `pgEnum` references, all in a comment at shared/schema/ai-telemetry.ts:44-46 explaining why none exist); server/routes-communications.ts:805-812`
- **Defect:** Across 724 tables there is not one `pgEnum` and, by grep, ~34 CHECK constraints in migrations plus ~30 in migrate.mjs — i.e. essentially none relative to the number of enumerated columns. Every status, type, channel, event_name and event_type is `text` with the legal values in a trailing `//` comment. The database therefore cannot reject a typo, and the codebase has at least one live consequence of exactly that (the `'negotiation'` vs `'negotiating'` pipeline defect above). The sharpest instance is `routes-communications.ts:805`, which takes `eventType` off the request body and inserts it into `activity_events` with no validation, so a client can mint arbitrary event types into the table the customer timeline groups and filters on. The ai-telemetry.ts comment argues the case for text+Zod (adding a value shouldn't need DDL), which is defensible — but the corollary obligation, validate at every write, is not being met.
- **Fix:** Do not add pgEnums retroactively. Instead: (a) route every enumerated write through the shared Zod schema that already exists for that vocabulary (`dealStatusSchema`, `ActivityEventType`, `PaxAskOrigin`) — start with the unvalidated one at routes-communications.ts:805; (b) add CHECK constraints on the small set where a bad value is unrecoverable (`payments.status`, `pending_actions.status`, `pax_sends.channel`); (c) add a lint that flags a `text("...")` column whose declaration comment enumerates values but which has no corresponding exported const/Zod schema — that comment is the tell.
- **Comparable:** Segment Protocols and Snowplow reject an event whose type is not in the tracking plan at ingest — the analytics store is never allowed to contain a vocabulary the plan does not know. Postgres CHECK/enum is the equivalent for OLTP; the mid-point AcreOS should hit is Zod-at-every-write plus CHECK on the unrecoverable few.
- **Evidence:** Grepped `pgEnum` across shared/schema*.ts (3 hits, all inside the explanatory comment at shared/schema/ai-telemetry.ts:44-46); counted `CHECK (` in migrations/*.sql (34) and migrate.mjs (30); read routes-communications.ts:800-815; read shared/lifecycle/pipeline-status.ts:72-98 for the vocabulary that exists but is not enforced at the write.

#### [medium/L] Mixed `timestamptz` and `timestamp` (480 vs 1276) with no organization timezone — every daily bucket and cross-table time comparison is silently offset

- **Where:** `shared/schema*.ts: 1276 `timestamp("x")` vs 480 `timestamp("x", { withTimezone: true })`; e.g. activation_events.occurred_at (tz, schema.ts:6079) vs mrr_snapshots.captured_at (no tz, :8811) vs payments.payment_date (no tz, :1702); bucketing at server/storage/analyticsRepo.ts:85-95`
- **Defect:** Roughly 73% of timestamp columns are `timestamp without time zone`. Postgres compares a `timestamp` against a `timestamptz` by assuming the session TimeZone, so any query joining or filtering across the two families is offset by whatever the connection happens to be set to — and the offset changes with DST. This is not theoretical here: the activation funnel joins `activation_events.occurred_at` (tz-aware) against `organizations.created_at`, cohort and retention math spans both families, and `getRevenueMetrics` buckets revenue with `DATE(payments.payment_date)` on a naive column, so "revenue on Tuesday" means Tuesday in the database's timezone for every customer regardless of where they operate. There is also no `organizations.timezone` column to bucket by even if one wanted to.
- **Fix:** Do not convert 1276 columns. Do three things: (1) pin the connection to UTC explicitly and assert it in a startup check, so at least the implicit offset is known and stable; (2) add `organizations.timezone` and apply `AT TIME ZONE` in every customer-facing daily/weekly rollup — a report's day boundary should be the customer's, which is also a product improvement; (3) convert the analytics-load-bearing subset to `timestamptz` in one migration (payments.payment_date, deals.closing_date, leads.created_at/updated_at, mrr_snapshots.captured_at) and add a schema lint requiring `withTimezone: true` on any NEW timestamp column, ratcheting the naive count down.
- **Comparable:** Stripe stores every timestamp as UTC epoch and every dashboard renders in an explicitly-chosen account timezone; Amplitude and Mixpanel both make the reporting timezone a project-level setting shown on every chart. Mixed naive/aware timestamps is the single most common source of "the numbers moved overnight and nobody changed anything".
- **Evidence:** Counted both declaration shapes across shared/schema.ts + shared/schema/*.ts (1276 / 480). Read the specific declarations cited; read analyticsRepo.ts:83-96 for the `DATE()` bucketing. Searched for an org timezone column — none found.

#### [medium/S] The nightly retention purge reports success to the job supervisor even when every table's DELETE fails

- **Where:** `server/jobs/dataRetention.ts:39-63 (the catch), server/jobs/runScheduledJobs.ts:1611-1621 (`notifyResult(..., true)`)`
- **Defect:** Each table's `DELETE` is wrapped in a try/catch whose handler is `logger.debug("Skipped ...")` with the comment "Table may not exist yet — that's fine, skip it." That reasoning holds for a missing table and for nothing else: a lock timeout, an FK violation from a child row, a permissions change, or a statement timeout on a large `DELETE ... RETURNING id` all land in the same silent debug line. `runDataRetention` then returns `{ purged: 0 }` and `processDataRetentionJob` calls `jobSupervisor.notifyResult('data_retention', …, true)` — success. A retention job that has been failing on every table for a month is indistinguishable from a clean run with nothing to purge. Secondary: the statement is a single unbounded `DELETE … RETURNING id` per table with no batching, which on a large `activity_log` will hold a long lock and materialize every deleted row for no reason (the return value is only counted).
- **Fix:** Distinguish `undefined_table` (42P01 — genuinely fine) from every other SQLSTATE; count non-42P01 failures, return them in the result, and pass `false` to `notifyResult` when any occurred. Batch the deletes (`DELETE … WHERE id IN (SELECT id … LIMIT 10000)` in a loop) and drop `RETURNING id` in favour of `rowCount`. Emit purged-row counts per table as a metric so an anomalous zero is visible.
- **Comparable:** Datadog/Sentry-monitored ETL treats "job ran" and "job did its work" as separate signals; dbt and Airflow both fail the task on a non-zero error count rather than on an exception escaping the loop. A critical job that cannot fail is not monitored.
- **Evidence:** Read dataRetention.ts:36-75 in full and runScheduledJobs.ts:1611-1625; jobRegistry.ts:287 confirms it is marked `critical: true`, which makes the false-green worse — the supervisor is the thing meant to notice.

#### [medium/M] There is no applied-migration ledger and the only data dictionary is four months stale, so "what is actually in production" is unanswerable from the repository

- **Where:** `migrations/meta/ (four snapshots, no _journal.json), scripts/migrate.mjs (11,248 lines), shared/schema-inventory.md:3`
- **Defect:** Production DDL is an 11k-line hand-maintained idempotent patch script. Nothing records which of the 255 `migrations/*.sql` files were ever applied, and the drizzle journal was deleted on 2026-05-11 by design. The orphans register is full of sentences like "ABSENT IN PRODUCTION — verified by deploy #36's evidence line" — i.e. the team's only way to learn what is in prod is to print RAISE NOTICEs during a release and hope Fly's log forwarder does not drop them (the migrate.mjs header documents deploys #35, #42, #43 and #44-46 each losing evidence lines to that exact race). Meanwhile the sole data dictionary, `shared/schema-inventory.md`, is stamped "Last updated: 2026-05-04", cites line numbers that have moved (it puts ACTIVITY EVENTS at line 4092; it is 5052), and describes the schema split as a future event using bucket names that bear no relation to the 84 files that actually shipped in `shared/schema/`. For 724 tables there is no ERD, no column comments in the database, and no catalog.
- **Fix:** (1) A `schema_migrations` table written by migrate.mjs — one row per statement batch with a content hash and applied_at — turns every "is it in prod?" question into a query and makes the RAISE NOTICE lottery unnecessary. (2) Generate the data dictionary instead of writing it: a script that emits table → columns → types → nullability → FK → classification (from `data-classification.ts`) → row-count, run in CI, committed. Delete `schema-inventory.md`. Both are prerequisites for anyone new being able to reason about this schema at all.
- **Comparable:** Every managed Postgres platform (Supabase, PlanetScale, Neon) treats the applied-migration ledger as the primary artifact. For the catalog: dbt docs, Atlas schema inspect, or even a nightly `pg_dump --schema-only` diff committed to the repo would beat a hand-written markdown that rots in ten weeks.
- **Evidence:** `ls migrations/meta/` → four snapshots, no journal; read drizzle.config.ts (documents the deletion and why); read scripts/migrate.mjs:1-90 including the deploy-log-loss commentary; `wc -l` on migrate.mjs (11,248); read shared/schema-inventory.md:1-25 and `git log -1` on it (last touched 2026-07-16 by an unrelated PR).

#### [low/M] Money stored as floating point in the receipt whose hash is meant to seal it, and 251 unconstrained `numeric` columns

- **Where:** `shared/schema.ts:2745 (`proof_receipts.cost_usd: doublePrecision`), :12941 (`autopilot_policy_proposals.target_value_usd`), :13259 (`autopilot_settings.growth_budget_override_usd`); 251 bare `numeric("x")` vs 23 with precision`
- **Defect:** `proof_receipts` is the tamper-evident ledger — it carries `receipt_hash`, `prev_receipt_hash`, a constitution version hash, and the comment at :2789 explaining that `issued_at` is stored as a *text* ISO string specifically "so the sealed receiptHash round-trips byte-for-byte — a timestamp's tz/precision drift would break verification." The same table stores `cost_usd` as `double precision`. Float has precisely the round-trip/precision property the neighbouring column was designed to avoid, and it is a dollar amount inside a sealed payload. `growth_budget_override_usd` is worse in kind: it is the DB-backed spend cap the autopilot reads, and the >$500 spend threshold is a founder hard-stop — a cap compared in float is a cap with a fuzzy edge. Separately, 251 `numeric` columns are declared with no precision/scale, so nothing at the database level constrains a money column to two decimals.
- **Fix:** Convert the three USD floats to `integer` cents (the codebase already prefers cents elsewhere: `mrr_cents`, `unit_cost_cents`, `total_cost_cents`) — for `proof_receipts` that requires a receipt-version bump since the hash payload changes, which the `receiptVersion` column at :2750 already anticipates. Give money `numeric` columns explicit `(14,2)` as they are touched. Add a schema lint forbidding `real`/`doublePrecision` on any column whose name matches the money vocabulary (`_usd`, `_cents`, `amount`, `price`, `cost`, `balance`).
- **Comparable:** Stripe represents every amount as an integer in the smallest currency unit, universally. Any ledger or receipt system that hashes its payload (Sigma, blockchain-style audit chains, Ramp's ledger) uses fixed-point or integers because float serialization is not stable across languages or driver versions — which is the same argument this table already makes for its timestamp.
- **Evidence:** Read shared/schema.ts:2734-2770 (proof_receipts in full, including the issued_at precision comment), :12930-12950, :13250-13270. Counted `numeric("x")` without options (251) vs with (23) across the schema files; grepped `doublePrecision(`/`real(` and read each money-named hit.

## Accessibility (WCAG 2.2 AA and beyond)

**CLAUDE.md's three accessibility absolutes are genuinely enforced — but each gate reads a narrower population than its own rule claims, and the one thing no gate measures at all is the visible focus indicator, which is 1.70–2.47:1 in all twelve theme×mode combinations and therefore fails SC 1.4.11 everywhere in the product.**


### Already at or above the bar — do not "fix" these

- `tests/unit/accessibility.test.ts` is, for its chosen populations, better than most teams ever write: the icon-button and label-association checks are absolutes with zero registers, each carries a vacuity guard (`expect(checked).toBeGreaterThan(150)`, `expect(scanned).toBeGreaterThan(600)`), the prop-spreading exemption is pinned to the exact string `components/ui/sidebar.tsx:328` so it cannot be silently widened, and the `FormControl wraps the control itself` test (line ~390) catches a Radix Slot failure that almost nobody checks. Do not weaken these — extend their populations.
- `client/src/components/ui/button.tsx:31-49` — the size scale resolves density by POINTER TYPE (`pointer-coarse:min-h-11`), not viewport width, with a written explanation of why there is deliberately no `sm:min-h-*` (same-property media tie resolved by stylesheet order). A 768px iPad correctly gets 44px. This is better than shadcn upstream and better than most native apps.
- `client/src/components/ui/skeleton.tsx` — `role="status"` + `aria-busy` + injected sr-only text BY DEFAULT, with an `announce={false}` opt-out. One primitive made 1,259 call sites announce without touching any of them. Keep the default.
- `client/src/components/mobile/MobileBottomNav.tsx` — `aria-label` on the `<nav>`, `aria-current="page"`, `min-w-[48px] min-h-[48px]`, `aria-hidden` on every icon, `env(safe-area-inset-bottom)`, and the FAB carries `aria-haspopup="dialog"` + `aria-expanded`. Reference-quality.
- Reduced motion is handled in three independent layers: token collapse under `[data-motion="reduced"]` (index.css:80), `MotionConfig reducedMotion="user"` (App.tsx:2244), and a global `*` wildcard that zeroes every animation and transition (index.css:2336). Vestibular-sensitive users genuinely get a still interface.
- All 12 `<img>` elements have an `alt` — decorative ones use `alt="" aria-hidden="true"` (pwa-install-prompt.tsx:206), meaningful ones are descriptive (`Signature of ${signerName}`), and the icon fallback in listings.tsx:664 uses `role="img" aria-label`. Zero debt.
- WCAG 2.2 SC 2.4.11 (Focus Not Obscured) is handled PROACTIVELY at index.css:1658-1673 with `scroll-margin-top/bottom` sized to the topbar and bottom nav, plus a `[data-sticky-chrome]` escalation. Most teams have never heard of this criterion.
- `client/src/components/ui/table.tsx:72` — `TableHead` defaults `scope="col"`, so all 344 shadcn table headers are correct by construction.
- Zero positive `tabIndex` values anywhere in 870 client files; `index.html` allows zoom (no `user-scalable=no`, no `maximum-scale`); `<html lang="en">` is set.
- A user-facing accessibility panel (`client/src/pages/settings/accessibility.tsx`, `components/settings/accessibility-panel.tsx`) offering a dyslexia-friendly Lexend pairing and three reading densities — beyond AA, and rare in B2B SaaS.
- `PaxAskCard.tsx` gives each button a contextual name — `aria-label={`Approve and send: ${verbAria}`}` — so a screen-reader user in a list of five asks knows WHICH one they are approving. That is the right pattern and most products get it wrong.

### Benchmarked against

- GOV.UK Design System — the canonical AA focus indicator: a solid, full-opacity 3px bar at >3:1 against every background it can land on, plus `<main tabindex="-1">` as the skip-link target so focus actually moves. AcreOS's ring is alpha-composited to ~2:1 and its skip link lands on a plain div.
- Adobe React Spectrum — a control with no label logs a dev-time console warning and its Slider/Select refuse to render nameless. That is the fix shape for `client/src/components/ui/slider.tsx`, which currently makes naming impossible rather than merely optional.
- Stripe Dashboard — semantic status colours (warning amber, success green) are tuned to clear 4.5:1 on BOTH the card and page grounds before they ship; AcreOS's `--acr-warn` is 2.76:1 on the page ground in the default theme across 634 call sites.
- Linear (⌘K palette) — the selected row is a full-opacity fill plus a left accent bar. AcreOS's Select and Command items use `bg-primary/10`, which composites to 1.11–1.22:1 against the popover — an invisible keyboard cursor on the app's primary keyboard surface.
- GitHub / Primer — a consequential action (Merge) swaps the button for a status message inside a live region and keeps focus in place. PaxAskCard unmounts its Approve button on success with no live region, so focus falls to <body> and nothing is announced.
- Next.js built-in route announcer — a visually-hidden `role="alert"` region that reads the new page title on client navigation. AcreOS updates `document.title` but has no announcer and no focus management across all nine doors.
- Salesforce Lightning (SLDS) — every form control carries either a rendered `<label for>` or `slds-assistive-text`; there is no third option. AcreOS has 65 SelectTriggers whose only accessible name is their current VALUE.

### Findings


#### [critical/S] The visible focus indicator fails SC 1.4.11 in every theme and mode (1.70–2.47:1)

- **Where:** `client/src/index.css:1658-1660 (`*:focus-visible { @apply outline-none ring-2 ring-primary/40 … }`) and client/src/index.css:2107-2115 (`button,a,input,select,textarea,[role=button]:focus-visible { outline:none; box-shadow: 0 0 0 2px hsl(var(--background)), 0 0 0 4px hsl(var(--primary) / 0.5) }`)`
- **Defect:** Both global focus rules remove the outline and replace it with an ALPHA-COMPOSITED ring. Computed over all twelve theme×mode token blocks: the `ring-primary/40` ring measures 1.70–2.47:1 against its own background, and the `primary/0.5` box-shadow used for links/inputs/[role=button] measures 1.97–3.18:1 — only meadow.dark's variant clears 3:1. SC 1.4.11 Non-text Contrast (AA) requires 3:1 for a focus indicator. This is the one CLAUDE.md accessibility rule ('Every interactive element must have visible focus state') that has no contrast gate — `accessibility.test.ts`'s focus suite only asserts the rule EXISTS and that nothing writes `ring-0`; it never measures whether the ring can be seen. The concrete harm: every wouter `<Link>` in the desktop sidebar (e.g. layout-sidebar.tsx:1277) carries no Tailwind ring utility, so the whole primary navigation gets the 2.07:1 rule. In the default theme (bedrock, per theme-context.tsx:41) a keyboard user tabbing the nav sees essentially nothing move.
- **Fix:** Drop the alpha. `--ring` already equals `--primary` in all twelve blocks, and full-opacity `hsl(var(--ring))` measures 4.22–8.84:1 — comfortably over 3:1 in every theme. Change the global rule to `@apply outline-none ring-2 ring-ring ring-offset-2 ring-offset-background` and the button/link rule to `0 0 0 2px hsl(var(--background)), 0 0 0 4px hsl(var(--ring))`. Then add a computed ratchet next to the existing landing-Beta-badge contrast test: parse every `--ring`/`--background`/`--card` triple out of index.css and assert ≥3:1, so a future palette change re-derives the verdict instead of restating it.
- **Comparable:** GOV.UK Design System ships a solid, full-opacity focus bar specifically because alpha rings composite differently on every surface and cannot be reasoned about; Stripe and Primer both use a 2px solid full-opacity ring. Nobody ships a 40%-alpha focus indicator.
- **Evidence:** Parsed all 12 token blocks out of client/src/index.css with a script and computed WCAG relative luminance. ring-primary/40 over background: bedrock light 1.77, bedrock dark 1.70, homestead light 1.70, homestead dark 2.16, quarry light 1.91, quarry dark 1.75, nocturne light 1.80, nocturne dark 1.79, meadow light 1.82, meadow dark 2.47, slate light 1.91, slate dark 1.94. primary/0.5 variant: 1.97–3.18. Full-opacity ring for the same pairs: 4.22, 4.67, 4.22, 7.26, 4.94, 5.05, 4.45, 5.90, 5.77, 8.84, 6.25, 6.04.

#### [critical/M] 24 interactive elements are mouse-only — including selecting a Pax conversation and expanding a sidebar nav module

- **Where:** `client/src/pages/command-center.tsx:2004; client/src/components/layout-sidebar.tsx:1256 and :1610; client/src/pages/buyer-blasts.tsx:167; client/src/pages/double-close.tsx:227; client/src/pages/quiet-title.tsx:243; client/src/pages/founder/dispatches.tsx:542; client/src/components/founder/asks/terminal-ask-row.tsx:41; client/src/components/notification-center.tsx:221; client/src/components/phone-numbers-settings.tsx:235; client/src/pages/leads.tsx:422; client/src/components/workspace/WorkspaceManager.tsx:176; client/src/components/workflow-builder.tsx:683 (+11 more)`
- **Defect:** These carry `onClick` on a `div`/`span`/`tr` with NO `role`, NO `tabIndex` and NO key handler. WCAG 2.1.1 Keyboard, Level A — the lowest bar there is. Two are especially bad. (a) command-center.tsx:2004: the Pax conversation list row is a plain div; the only focusable child is the Delete button (line 2022) — so a keyboard user can DELETE a conversation but cannot OPEN one. (b) layout-sidebar.tsx:1256: the module row's `onClick` calls `toggleModule(module.id)` and the disclosure affordance is a bare `<ChevronDown/>` icon at line 1346, not a button — so a keyboard user cannot expand a nav module's children in the desktop sidebar, and there is no `aria-expanded` either. Five clickable `<tr>` rows navigate to detail pages with zero focusable content inside them.
- **Fix:** For rows that navigate (buyer-blasts, double-close, quiet-title, leads): put a real `<Link>` on the primary cell and let the row's hover style follow `:focus-within` — do not fake a row button. For rows that toggle/select (command-center, terminal-ask-row, dispatches, notification-center, phone-numbers-settings): change the element to `<button type="button">` with `aria-pressed`/`aria-current`, or add `role="button" tabIndex={0}` plus Enter/Space handling. For layout-sidebar: wrap the chevron in a real `<button aria-expanded={expanded} aria-controls={submenuId} aria-label={`${module.label} sections`}>`. Then add a gate: scan for `onClick` on non-interactive tags and require the role+tabIndex+key triple, seeded at the post-fix count of 0.
- **Comparable:** Linear's issue list rows are real focusable elements with roving tabindex; GitHub's notification rows and Gmail's message rows are keyboard-selectable with Enter. A list you can delete from but not open by keyboard is not shippable.
- **Evidence:** Scripted scan of all 870 client .tsx files (comments stripped): 44 non-button elements carry a real `onClick` (pure `e.stopPropagation()` handlers excluded); 24 have none of role/tabIndex/onKeyDown, 4 have only one or two. Read command-center.tsx:1998-2032, layout-sidebar.tsx:1250-1350 and buyer-blasts.tsx:160-182 directly to confirm no focusable child provides the same action.

#### [high/M] The only runtime WCAG audit in the repo is a permanent no-op — `@axe-core/playwright` is not installed

- **Where:** `tests/e2e/accessibility.spec.ts:26-50 (`runAxe`) and :74-88; .pa11yci.json`
- **Defect:** `runAxe()` does `await import("@axe-core/playwright").catch(() => ({checkA11y:null, injectAxe:null}))` and returns `null` when the module is absent. `@axe-core` is NOT in node_modules and neither `axe` nor `pa11y` appears in package.json or any of the 20 workflows in .github/workflows. So `expect(critOnly, ...).toHaveLength(0)` — the only assertion in the file that measures WCAG — has never executed. The suite passes while auditing nothing. Two further population holes even once axe is installed: (a) `checkPageAccessibility` returns early when `page.url().includes("/auth")`, which is every authenticated surface — Today, Map, Deals, Finance, Pax, Settings, all of /founder; and (b) it only fails on `impact === "critical"`, and axe classifies `color-contrast` as `serious`, so every contrast defect in this report would be annotated and then passed. `.pa11yci.json` configures WCAG2AA over ten URLs and is wired to no script and no CI job. This is exactly the third law in CLAUDE.md: a green result over a population the gate never read.
- **Fix:** Install `@axe-core/playwright`, delete the graceful-degradation branch entirely and let a missing dependency be a hard failure (a silent skip is what caused this). Run the audit against an AUTHENTICATED storage state so the five doors are actually scanned — the repo already has auth fixtures under tests/helpers. Fail on `critical` AND `serious`, with a named allowlist of known-open rule ids that may only shrink. Either wire `.pa11yci.json` into a CI job or delete it; a config nothing runs is worse than none.
- **Comparable:** Every design-system team that ships a11y at scale (Primer, Spectrum, Carbon, Polaris) runs axe in CI against authenticated states and treats a missing scanner as a build break, not a skip.
- **Evidence:** `ls node_modules/@axe-core` exits 2 (no such directory). `grep -n "axe|pa11y" package.json` returns nothing. `grep -rn pa11y .github/workflows/*.yml` returns nothing. Read tests/e2e/accessibility.spec.ts:20-100 in full to confirm the null-return path and the auth early-return.

#### [high/M] The default theme's warning/accent semantic colours fail 4.5:1 — 634 `text-acr-warn` and 190 `text-acr-accent` call sites

- **Where:** `client/src/index.css:265 (`--acr-warn: #B97A1E`), :262 (`--acr-accent: #B8842E`), :266 (`--acr-warn-soft`); rendered at e.g. client/src/components/confidence-interval.tsx:149, client/src/components/monthly-checkin.tsx:148, client/src/components/founder-chat/Composer.tsx:210, client/src/pages/settings/pax-controls.tsx:363`
- **Defect:** In bedrock — the default theme (theme-context.tsx:41) — `--acr-warn` (#B97A1E) measures 3.15:1 on the card/surface ground (#F7F0DD) and 2.76:1 on the page ground; `--acr-accent` measures 2.89:1 and 2.54:1. The `text-acr-warn` + `bg-acr-warn-soft` badge pattern composites to 2.72:1. All of it is small text (`text-xs`, `text-micro`), so the bar is 4.5:1. The same token fails in homestead (2.90 / 2.54), nocturne (3.37 / 3.04), meadow (2.47 / 2.17) and slate (3.91 / 3.39) — five of six light themes. In bedrock dark, `--acr-neg` (#BF4C2E) is 3.53:1 on surface (488 `text-acr-neg` call sites) and the destructive button label is 3.65:1. The existing contrast gate (accessibility.test.ts, `the landing Beta badge meets WCAG AA`) computes a real ratio — correctly — for exactly ONE colour pair on the marketing landing, and never opens index.css's twelve theme blocks.
- **Fix:** Darken the light-mode warn/accent ramps (e.g. `--acr-warn: #8A5A12`, `--acr-accent: #8C6320` clear 4.5:1 on both grounds) and lighten `--acr-neg` in dark. Because the `*-soft` badge pattern composites at 14–18% over the surface, tune the pair together, not the ink alone. Then generalise the Beta-badge test into a table-driven gate: parse every theme block, iterate a fixed list of (fg, bg, minimum) pairs including the `*-soft`-over-surface composites, and assert. Seed it at the post-fix zero — the point is that adding a seventh theme without contrast-checking it becomes the thing that fails.
- **Comparable:** Stripe and Shopify Polaris both publish their status ramps with measured light/dark ratios per surface. AcreOS already proved it knows how (the ink-2/ink-3 tokens carry comments saying they were darkened for AA) — warn/accent were simply never measured.
- **Evidence:** Scripted parse of all 12 blocks in index.css + WCAG relative-luminance computation (script in scratchpad). Usage counts by grep across client/src: `text-acr-warn` 634, `text-acr-pos` 738, `text-acr-neg` 488, `text-acr-accent` 190; `bg-acr-warn-soft` co-occurring with `text-acr-warn` on the same line: 139 (pos 121, neg 87).

#### [high/M] 181 colour utility classes reference `acr-*` tokens that do not exist and compile to nothing

- **Where:** `tailwind.config.ts:175-222 (the `acr` colour object); offenders include client/src/components/rentals/units-panel.tsx:922, client/src/components/note-compliance-card.tsx:51/53/289, client/src/components/note-assignments-card.tsx:56, client/src/components/founder/PulseStrip.tsx:114, client/src/components/settings/accessibility-panel.tsx:30, client/src/pages/data-import.tsx:83-85`
- **Defect:** The `acr` palette defines `warn`, `pos`, `neg` — but the codebase uses `acr-warning` (142 occurrences), `acr-success` (39), `acr-ink-1` (8), `acr-info` (5), `acr-primary` (3), `acr-error` (3), `acr-warn-foreground` (1), `acr-ok` (1). None of these are Tailwind classes, so they emit no CSS. Verified against the BUILT stylesheet: `text-acr-warning` appears 0 times in dist/public/assets/*.css while `text-acr-warn` is present. The accessibility consequence is that error and warning text renders in the inherited body colour with no chromatic distinction — `units-panel.tsx:922` is a CSV-import error message (`data-testid="bulk-preview-error"`) that looks identical to body copy, and `note-assignments-card.tsx:56` is `bg-acr-warning/10 text-acr-warning`, a badge with a transparent fill and default text. Users relying on colour to triage status get nothing; the repo's `no-hardcoded-color-literals` lint rule polices hex literals but not invented token names.
- **Fix:** Rename to the real tokens (`warning`→`warn`, `success`→`pos`, `error`/`danger`→`neg`, `ink-1`→`ink`, `info`→a deliberately added token or `brand`) — it is a mechanical find-and-replace across 181 sites. Then extend `eslint-rules/no-hardcoded-color-literals.cjs` (or add a sibling) that parses the `acr` key set out of tailwind.config.ts and errors on any `(text|bg|border|ring|fill|stroke)-acr-<name>` whose `<name>` is not in that set. Because the key list is read from the config rather than hardcoded, adding a token cannot rot the rule.
- **Evidence:** Scripted diff: extracted the `acrToken(...)` key set from tailwind.config.ts (37 keys) and matched every `*-acr-*` utility across client/src/**/*.{tsx,ts,css}. Confirmed against the compiled output: `grep -c text-acr-warning dist/public/assets/index-DMOc5MPd.css` = 0; `acr-success`, `acr-info`, `acr-error`, `acr-primary`, `acr-ink-1` all 0 in every built CSS file. `grep -n safelist tailwind.config.ts` returns nothing, so nothing rescues them.

#### [high/M] No slider in the product can be given an accessible name — the primitive blocks it

- **Where:** `client/src/components/ui/slider.tsx:21 (`<SliderPrimitive.Thumb className="…" />`); call sites include client/src/components/payment-calculator.tsx:120 and :145, client/src/components/seller-finance-calculator.tsx:230 and :254, client/src/components/gis-filters.tsx:282, client/src/components/property-map.tsx:592/2952/3182`
- **Defect:** Radix reads the accessible name off the THUMB (`node_modules/@radix-ui/react-slider/dist/index.mjs:475`: `"aria-label": props["aria-label"] || label` inside the `role="slider"` span). AcreOS's wrapper spreads `{...props}` onto `SliderPrimitive.Root` and renders the Thumb with only a `className` — so a caller-supplied `aria-label` lands on the root span (role `generic`, where it is a prohibited attribute) and never reaches the thing that has the slider role. All 16 sliders therefore announce as 'slider, 25' with no field name, and 0 of them even attempt a label. Worst instances: payment-calculator.tsx:112-121 and :139-148 have visible `<Label>Down Payment</Label>` / `<Label>Interest Rate</Label>` with no `htmlFor` and no way to associate — a screen-reader user setting a down payment cannot tell which slider they are on. WCAG 4.1.2 Name/Role/Value and 3.3.2 Labels or Instructions.
- **Fix:** Change the primitive to accept and forward the name: destructure `'aria-label'` and `'aria-labelledby'` out of props and pass them to `SliderPrimitive.Thumb`, or accept a required `label` prop. Then make it impossible to regress — React Spectrum's approach — by typing the props so one of `aria-label` / `aria-labelledby` is required, which turns the 16 unlabelled call sites into type errors rather than a hunt.
- **Evidence:** Read client/src/components/ui/slider.tsx in full (26 lines) — the Thumb receives no props. Confirmed Radix's naming site by grepping node_modules/@radix-ui/react-slider/dist/index.mjs:467-479. `grep -rn -A4 "<Slider" client/src --include=*.tsx | grep -c aria-label` = 0. Read payment-calculator.tsx:108-155 to confirm the orphaned visible labels.

#### [high/S] Approving a Pax ask sends real mail and announces nothing, then throws focus to <body>

- **Where:** `client/src/components/pax/PaxAskCard.tsx:216-220 (the status span), :341-346 (the outcome note), :348 (`{showButtons && !editing && (`)`
- **Defect:** `outcomeLine()` transitions 'Waiting for your tap' → 'Working…' → 'Approved and sent' / 'Rejected — nothing was sent' / 'Not completed — still waiting for your tap'. That text lives in a plain `<span>` with no `role="status"` and no `aria-live`, and the server's failure reason at :341 is likewise a plain `<p>`. WCAG 4.1.3 Status Messages (AA) — a status change that does not receive focus must be programmatically announced. Changing the container's `aria-label` (line 202) does not announce; live-region semantics are required. Compounding it: on success `showButtons` flips false and the entire button group at :348 unmounts, so the element that had focus disappears and focus falls to `<body>` — the user loses their position in a queue of asks and the screen reader restarts from the top of the document. For a card whose Approve button literally dispatches SMS/email to a counterparty, 'you tapped and heard nothing' is the wrong outcome. (This is a product-quality gap in the wave-1 surface, not a §8 wave-2/3 item — the revert route and per-lead mute are out of scope here.)
- **Fix:** Give the status span `role="status"` (or wrap status + note in one `<div role="status" aria-live="polite" aria-atomic="true">`) so the outcome sentence is spoken. For focus: keep a ref to the card root, and in `run()` after a terminal outcome move focus to the status region with `tabIndex={-1}` + `.focus()` — the pattern GitHub uses after Merge. Because PaxAskCard is the single component all four hosts render (paxAskHosts.test.ts already pins that), one fix covers the chat bubble, the /ai strip, Today's queue and support chat.
- **Comparable:** GitHub's Merge button and Gmail's Archive both keep focus and announce the result through a live region; Linear moves focus to the next queue item. All three treat 'the control you pressed vanished silently' as a bug.
- **Evidence:** Read client/src/components/pax/PaxAskCard.tsx in full (403 lines). `grep -n "aria-live|role=\"status\"" client/src/components/pax/PaxAskCard.tsx` returns nothing — the file's only a11y-name affordances are `role="group"` at :201, the sr-only edit label at :243, and per-button `aria-label`s. Confirmed `showButtons = status === "pending" || status === "failed"` at :183 and that the button `<div>` is inside `{showButtons && …}`.

#### [high/S] The 'Waiting for your tap' badge label is dropped by every browser, and the live count is never announced

- **Where:** `client/src/components/layout-sidebar.tsx:1308 and :1649 (`<Badge … aria-label={`${paxAskCount} waiting for your tap`}>`); client/src/components/mobile/MobileBottomNav.tsx:120-126 (`<span … aria-label={…}>`); client/src/components/ui/badge.tsx:34 (`<div className={…} {...props} />`)`
- **Defect:** Two defects on the same element. (1) `Badge` renders a bare `<div>` with no `role`, and the mobile badge is a bare `<span>`. Both map to ARIA role `generic`, on which `aria-label` is a PROHIBITED attribute (ARIA 1.2 / ARIA in HTML) — browsers do not expose it. So the carefully written 'N waiting for your tap' is dead code that reads as compliance; the user hears the nav link's name-from-content, i.e. 'Pax 3', with no indication of what 3 means. (2) `usePaxNeedsYou.ts:97-109` writes the count straight from the `pax.needs_you` websocket payload, so the badge changes live while the user is on any page — and it sits in no live region, so a new ask from the autopilot arrives in total silence for a screen-reader user. For a product whose premise is 'the autopilot asks you before it acts', that is the single most important notification in the app.
- **Fix:** Give the badge a role that permits naming and announces: `<Badge role="status" aria-live="polite" aria-atomic="true">` with the count as text and a sibling `<span className="sr-only">asks waiting for your tap</span>`; or drop `aria-label` and render `<span aria-hidden="true">3</span><span className="sr-only">3 asks waiting for your tap</span>`. Do it once in `Badge` by accepting an optional `srLabel` prop so the three call sites converge. Separately, add ONE app-level polite live region fed by `usePaxNeedsYouCount` that speaks 'Pax has 3 asks waiting for your tap' on increase only — mounted in App.tsx alongside the toaster, not per-door, so the three badges do not triple-announce.
- **Evidence:** Read client/src/components/ui/badge.tsx (48 lines) — `Badge` is `<div … {...props} />`, no role. Read MobileBottomNav.tsx in full — the badge is a `<span>`. Read usePaxNeedsYou.ts in full — `useNeedsYouLive()` sets the count from the socket payload and no consumer wraps it in a live region. Grep for `aria-live` in pax-controls.tsx / today.tsx returns nothing.

#### [high/M] `CardTitle` renders a `<div>`, so 735 section headings are missing from the document outline

- **Where:** `client/src/components/ui/card.tsx:37-50; 735 call sites across 276 files, including the wave-1 page client/src/pages/settings/pax-controls.tsx:482, :529, :653, :952`
- **Defect:** `CardTitle` is `<div className="text-2xl font-semibold …">`. It looks like a heading, is used everywhere as one, and is invisible to heading navigation — the primary way screen-reader users move around a page (WebAIM's survey consistently puts heading navigation first). There is no `asChild` and no `as` prop, so no call site can promote it: 0 of the 735 carry `role="heading"` and 0 use `asChild`. The wave-1 Pax controls page is the clean example: its outline is `h1` 'Pax controls' (line 355) then two small `h2`s inside the pause card (412, 428) — and the four MAJOR sections a user actually navigates between ('When Pax asks', 'Runs on its own', 'Never', 'Can use Pax') are not headings at all. Repo-wide the outline is also uneven: 15 page files open at `h3` or `h4`, and 17 files skip a level (`h1 → h3`, `h2 → h4`).
- **Fix:** Add an `as` prop to `CardTitle` (`as?: 'h2'|'h3'|'h4'|'div'`, defaulting to `h3`) or make it a Radix `Slot` so call sites can pass `<CardTitle asChild><h2>…</h2></CardTitle>`. Defaulting to `h3` fixes 735 sites in one commit and is correct under a page `h1` + optional `h2`. Then add a gate that walks each page file's heading sequence (counting CardTitle as its rendered level) and forbids skips, seeded at the post-fix count.
- **Comparable:** Primer's `Heading` and Polaris's `Text as="h2" variant="headingMd"` both separate visual size from semantic level precisely so a card title stays a real heading. shadcn upstream has the same div defect — this is a place to diverge from it deliberately.
- **Evidence:** Read client/src/components/ui/card.tsx:37-50. `grep -rn "<CardTitle" client/src --include=*.tsx | wc -l` = 735 across 276 files; `… | grep -c role=` = 0; `… | grep -c asChild` = 0. Heading scan over all 870 .tsx files: 318 contain headings, 197 contain an `h1`; 15 `pages/*` files open at h3+; 17 files skip a level (list includes pages/tools.tsx:41 h1→h3, pages/documents.tsx:1255 h1→h4).

#### [high/M] 108 form controls have no accessible name — they are outside the label gate's population

- **Where:** `tests/unit/accessibility.test.ts:224 (`for (const m of src.matchAll(/<(Input|Textarea)\b/g))`); offenders include client/src/components/settings/notification-quiet-hours.tsx:120 and :134, client/src/components/settings/accessibility-panel.tsx:41 and :62, client/src/components/settings/persona-panel.tsx:103, client/src/pages/command-center.tsx:391 and :621, client/src/components/pax-knowledge-panel.tsx:196, client/src/pages/founder-compliance-ops.tsx:582-586`
- **Defect:** The label-association gate is excellent and sits at zero debt — but its population is the regex `<(Input|Textarea)`. Every other control type is outside it. Scanning the same way over `SelectTrigger | Checkbox | Switch | RadioGroup | Slider | select | input | textarea` (441 elements, applying the same three legitimate mechanisms — aria-label, id↔htmlFor, FormControl, plus implicit `<label>` wrapping) finds 108 with no accessible name: 65 SelectTriggers, 10 Sliders, 5 Switches, 4 RadioGroups, 4 Checkboxes, 17 raw inputs, 2 selects, 1 textarea. Twenty-two of the 65 SelectTriggers are the exact failure the gate's own docstring names — a VISIBLE `<Label>` sitting right above with no `htmlFor` (notification-quiet-hours.tsx:115 `<Label>Quiet from</Label>` + `<SelectTrigger>` with neither id nor aria-label). A Radix SelectTrigger's only accessible name is its current VALUE, so the user hears '9:00 PM, combobox' and cannot tell which field. The four RadioGroups are named groups with unnamed `role="radiogroup"` containers — including, ironically, the two in the accessibility settings panel itself (font family, reading density) and the persona picker. Five raw inputs in founder-compliance-ops.tsx:582-586 are placeholder-only, and placeholder is not a label (WCAG 3.3.2 — it disappears the moment you type).
- **Fix:** Widen the existing test's regex to the full control set, keep every existing exemption, and work the 108 to zero the same way the 116 Input/Textarea cases were worked — `htmlFor`+`id` from a `useId()` prefix for SelectTrigger, `aria-label` on `RadioGroup`/`Slider`/`Switch` where the heading is the name. Then put the control list in a named constant in the test so adding a ninth control type without adding it to the list is what fails — the enumeration is the gate, not the regex.
- **Evidence:** Scripted scan reusing the test's own opener-parsing and exemption logic (aria-label/labelledby, id↔htmlFor, enclosing FormControl, enclosing `<Label>` or `<label>`, `components/ui/**` prop-spreading primitives), over 441 non-Input/Textarea controls. Read notification-quiet-hours.tsx:110-140, accessibility-panel.tsx:28-80 and payment-calculator.tsx:108-155 to confirm the pattern by hand rather than trusting the count.

#### [medium/S] The keyboard cursor in every Select dropdown and the command palette is a 1.15:1 tint

- **Where:** `client/src/components/ui/select.tsx:121 (`outline-none … focus:bg-primary/10 focus:text-foreground`); client/src/components/ui/command.tsx:120 (`outline-none … data-[selected='true']:bg-primary/10`)`
- **Defect:** Both item primitives set `outline-none` and substitute a background tint as the ONLY focus indicator. `primary` at 10% alpha composited over the popover ground measures 1.11–1.22:1 across all twelve theme×mode blocks. SC 1.4.11 requires 3:1 for the focus indicator. In practice this means keyboard-navigating a Select — or the ⌘K command palette, which is by definition keyboard-only — gives no visible indication of where you are. The existing focus suite in accessibility.test.ts explicitly and correctly reasons that `outline-none` is fine because the global ring covers it — but these two files raise specificity above the global rule by supplying their own `focus:bg-*`, which the gate's `INDICATOR` regex accepts (`focus…:bg-`) without measuring whether the resulting background actually differs.
- **Fix:** Raise the fill to a full-opacity `bg-accent`/`bg-primary` with a matching foreground token (Linear/Spotlight do this), or keep the tint and add a real ring: `focus:ring-2 focus:ring-inset focus:ring-ring`. Then strengthen the gate: where a component's focus indicator is a background change, compute the composited ratio against the surface it sits on and assert ≥3:1, rather than accepting the presence of a `focus:bg-*` class.
- **Comparable:** macOS Spotlight and Linear's ⌘K both use a saturated full-opacity selection fill; GitHub's palette adds a left accent bar so the cursor survives high-contrast and forced-colors modes.
- **Evidence:** Read select.tsx:121 and command.tsx:120. Computed `mix(primary, popover, 0.10)` vs `popover` for all 12 theme blocks: 1.15, 1.11, 1.14, 1.17, 1.17, 1.11, 1.16, 1.11, 1.15, 1.22, 1.17, 1.14.

#### [medium/S] The focused item in a dropdown/context menu is the least readable item in it (2.47–2.91:1)

- **Where:** `client/src/index.css:291-292 (`--accent: 36 61% 45%` / `--accent-foreground: 41 51% 92%` in bedrock) and :955-956 (meadow); consumed at client/src/components/ui/context-menu.tsx:81, menubar.tsx:139, toggle.tsx:8, navigation-menu.tsx:44, dropdown-menu.tsx:99`
- **Defect:** `bg-accent text-accent-foreground` is the keyboard-focus and hover treatment for menu items and the on-state for `Toggle`. In bedrock (the default theme) the pair is cream #F7F0DD-equivalent on a mid gold, measuring 2.91:1; in meadow it is 2.47:1. Both fail 4.5:1 for the item's own text, which means the item you have focused is harder to read than the ones you have not — the inverse of what a focus treatment should do. In these two themes `popover-foreground` on `accent` would measure 5.29:1 and 6.64:1 respectively, so the fix is a token swap, not a redesign. (The other four themes' accents are near-black or near-white and pass comfortably at 4.56–19.80:1, which is why this was easy to miss.)
- **Fix:** Darken `--accent` or set `--accent-foreground` to the dark ink in bedrock and meadow light; the computed alternative already in the palette clears AA. Fold the `accent-foreground`-on-`accent` pair into the theme contrast ratchet from the semantic-colour finding so all six themes are checked together.
- **Evidence:** Computed from the parsed token blocks: accent-foreground on accent — bedrock light 2.91, meadow light 2.47, homestead light 4.56, slate light 5.10, quarry light 18.73, nocturne light 19.80, and all four dark variants ≥7.44. popover-foreground on the same accent: bedrock 5.29, meadow 6.64.

#### [medium/S] Input and control borders are 1.18–1.62:1 against their background in all twelve themes

- **Where:** `client/src/index.css:295-296 (`--border` / `--input`), repeated in all 12 theme blocks; consumed by client/src/components/ui/input.tsx:12 (`border border-input`), select.tsx:22, textarea.tsx:12, button.tsx variant `outline``
- **Defect:** SC 1.4.11 requires 3:1 for 'visual information required to identify user interface components' — the boundary of a text field is the canonical example. `--input` against `--background` measures 1.57 (bedrock light), 1.48 (bedrock dark), 1.23 (homestead light), 1.39 (homestead dark), 1.35/1.62 (quarry), 1.18/1.29 (nocturne), 1.18/1.45 (meadow), 1.21/1.36 (slate). The inputs also use `bg-background/70` with `inset-input`, so the fill barely differs from the page either — a low-vision user cannot see where the field is until they click it. Nocturne and meadow light are the worst at 1.18:1, essentially invisible.
- **Fix:** Introduce a distinct `--input-border` token (keep `--border` soft for decorative dividers, which are exempt) tuned to ≥3:1 in every theme, and point `input.tsx`, `select.tsx`, `textarea.tsx` and the `outline` button variant at it. Splitting the token is what lets the hairline dividers stay beautiful while the interactive boundaries become perceivable. Add the pair to the theme contrast ratchet at a 3.0 threshold.
- **Evidence:** Computed for all 12 parsed theme blocks; every single one fails, range 1.18–1.62:1. Read input.tsx:12 and select.tsx:22 to confirm both consume `border-input` with a near-background fill.

#### [medium/S] Onboarding placeholder text renders at 2.02–2.48:1 — the first screen a customer ever sees

- **Where:** `client/src/components/onboarding/onboarding.css:249-251 (`.ob-input::placeholder { color: var(--acr-ink-4) }`), client/src/pages/styles/onboarding-v2.css:236-238 (`.ob2-input::placeholder`), :414-416 (`.ob2-link-sep`), client/src/pages/today.css:234; token defined at client/src/index.css:258 and 11 sibling blocks`
- **Defect:** `--acr-ink-4` is the quaternary ink and measures 2.48:1 (bedrock light), 2.21 (homestead light), 2.19 (quarry light), 2.07 (nocturne light), 2.02 (meadow light), 2.19 (slate light) and 2.03–2.32 in the dark themes, against its own surface. It is used as the placeholder colour on the onboarding inputs and as a text colour on Today. Placeholder text is text under WCAG 1.4.3 and needs 4.5:1. Because onboarding is the first authenticated experience, this is the worst possible place for it. `.acr-sugg-dismiss` (onboarding.css:230-238) also uses ink-4 as the colour of an icon-only dismiss button, which additionally fails the 3:1 non-text bar. Notably `--acr-ink-2` and `--acr-ink-3` both carry comments in index.css saying they were darkened specifically for AA — ink-4 was never given the same treatment.
- **Fix:** Either darken `--acr-ink-4` to ≥4.5:1 in all twelve blocks (it will still read as clearly recessive), or — better — stop using it for text: introduce `--acr-ink-placeholder` at the AA floor and leave ink-4 for genuinely non-informational strokes. Add ink-4-on-surface to the theme contrast ratchet so the next theme cannot reintroduce it.
- **Evidence:** Computed for all 12 blocks (2.02–2.48:1). Read onboarding.css:230-252, onboarding-v2.css:233-241 and :410-420, today.css:228-240 to confirm the token is used for placeholder and body text, not just borders.

#### [medium/M] 100 `aria-label`s sit on role-less span/div/p and are discarded by every browser

- **Where:** `client/src/components/comps-analysis.tsx:293, :307, :321, :335; client/src/components/finance/PersonaFinanceHero.tsx:151 and :355; client/src/components/finance/FinanceBook.tsx:193; client/src/components/due-diligence-panel.tsx:563 and :567; client/src/components/timeline/TimelineView.tsx:187; client/src/components/founder-bridge/AgentRowTile.tsx:108; client/src/components/solene/MessageBubble.tsx:182 (+ ~88 more)`
- **Defect:** ARIA 1.2 makes `aria-label` a prohibited attribute on the roles `generic` (`<span>`, `<div>`) and `paragraph` (`<p>`); user agents must not expose it. 100 such labels exist. They are not neutral — they are load-bearing text that the author believed was reaching the user: comps-analysis.tsx:293-335 labels four `<p>` price stats as 'Average / Median / High / Low price per acre' and the user hears only four bare dollar amounts in a row with no way to tell them apart; PersonaFinanceHero.tsx:151 pairs `aria-busy="true"` with `aria-label="Loading finance summary"` on a `<div>`, so neither the busy state nor the label is announced. The pattern reads as a11y work and delivers none.
- **Fix:** Three mechanical rewrites depending on intent: add the role that permits the name (`role="status"` for the loading wrappers, `role="img"` for a composed glyph, `role="group"` for a labelled cluster); or convert the label into a visually-hidden `<span className="sr-only">` sibling with `aria-hidden` on the visual part (the right answer for the stat cards and badges); or attach the description to a real control with `aria-describedby`. Then add a lint rule: `aria-label`/`aria-labelledby` on `span|div|p` with no `role` is an error — the ARIA prohibition list is short and stable, so the rule cannot rot.
- **Evidence:** `grep -rnE '<(span|div|p|li)\b[^>]{0,400}aria-label=' client/src --include=*.tsx | grep -v 'role='` = 100 (the `<li>` matches are legitimate — `listitem` is not naming-prohibited — so the true count is slightly lower, but the span/div/p majority is confirmed by reading the cited lines).

#### [medium/S] Client-side navigation is silent — no route announcer and no focus management across all nine doors

- **Where:** `client/src/App.tsx (routing tree; no announcer present), client/src/hooks/use-document-title.ts, client/src/components/page-shell.tsx:127-131`
- **Defect:** AcreOS is a wouter SPA. When a user activates a nav link, the DOM swaps but the browser performs no navigation: focus stays on an element that is about to unmount (and then falls to `<body>`), and no screen reader announces the new page. `useDocumentTitle` correctly updates `document.title`, but title changes are not reliably announced on client-side transitions in NVDA/JAWS/VoiceOver. `grep` finds no `.focus()` call, no announcer component and no route-change effect in App.tsx. The result: a screen-reader user tapping Deals → Finance hears silence and then has to re-explore from the top of the document, on every one of the five customer doors and four founder doors.
- **Fix:** Add one visually-hidden route announcer mounted once in App.tsx — a `role="status" aria-live="polite"` span whose text is set to the resolved page label on every `useLocation()` change (this is exactly what Next.js ships built-in and what Reach Router's `<Announcer>` popularised). Pair it with moving focus to the page heading: give PageShell's `<main>` `tabIndex={-1}` and `.focus()` it on location change, guarded so it does not fire on the initial load. Roughly 30 lines and it fixes all nine doors at once.
- **Comparable:** Next.js, Reach Router and GOV.UK all treat the route announcer as table stakes; Gmail and Linear both move focus into the new region on navigation.
- **Evidence:** `grep -rn "focus()|announcePageChange|RouteAnnouncer" client/src/App.tsx` returns nothing. Read use-document-title.ts in full and page-shell.tsx:120-135 — the `<main>` has `id` and `aria-label` but no `tabIndex`. Hypothesis marker: I did not run the app, so the silence is inferred from the absence of any announcer/focus code, not observed in a screen reader.

#### [medium/S] `id="main-content"` is duplicated, so the skip link lands on a `<div>` instead of the `<main>` landmark — and the test accepts either

- **Where:** `client/src/App.tsx:1888 (`<motion.div … id="main-content">`), client/src/components/page-shell.tsx:127-129 (`<main id="main-content" aria-label={resolvedLabel}>`), skip link at client/src/App.tsx:2111; gate at tests/unit/accessibility.test.ts:65-77`
- **Defect:** Two elements carry `id="main-content"` on every authenticated page, and App.tsx's page-transition `motion.div` is the ANCESTOR — so `href="#main-content"` resolves to the div, not the landmark. The skip link therefore never delivers the user to the `<main>` region, and because neither element carries `tabIndex={-1}`, focus is not programmatically moved at all (browsers set a sequential-navigation start point, which mostly works, but nothing is announced and Safari's behaviour is inconsistent). Duplicate ids also break any future `aria-labelledby`/`aria-describedby` pointing at that id. The existing gate is written as `expect(inApp || inComponents).toBe(true)` — an OR — so having BOTH, which is the actual defect, passes it. A textbook case of the gate measuring presence rather than the property.
- **Fix:** Remove `id="main-content"` from the App.tsx transition layer (it is a visual wrapper, not a landmark), keep it only on PageShell's `<main>`, and add `tabIndex={-1}` there so the skip link moves real focus. Rewrite the test to assert the id appears EXACTLY ONCE across the rendered tree — i.e. count occurrences in App.tsx plus components/ and require the total to be 1 — so the both-present state is what fails.
- **Evidence:** Read client/src/App.tsx:1880-1895 and client/src/components/page-shell.tsx:120-135 — both set the id verbatim, and PageShell's main renders inside App's routed children. Read the gate at tests/unit/accessibility.test.ts:65-77 to confirm the OR.

#### [medium/S] The icon-button gate and its ESLint rule are both blind to conditional icon children — and one live button is labelled wrong
