- **Where:** `tests/unit/accessibility.test.ts:123 (`src.indexOf('size="icon"')`); eslint-rules/icon-button-needs-aria-label.cjs:76-88 (`hasIconOnlyChildren` returns false on any `JSXExpressionContainer`); escapees at client/src/components/pax-artifact.tsx:220 and client/src/pages/marketplace.tsx:368; mislabel at client/src/components/pax-artifact.tsx:226`
- **Defect:** CLAUDE.md states the rule absolutely ('Every icon-only button must have aria-label') and both enforcers have the same population hole from opposite directions: the unit test's population is literally the substring `size="icon"`, and the ESLint rule bails out of `hasIconOnlyChildren` the moment a child is a JSX expression. So the very common form `{open ? <ChevronUp/> : <ChevronDown/>}` is invisible to both. Scanning all 870 files for buttons whose children reduce to icons only (including conditional branches) finds two genuine unnamed survivors: pax-artifact.tsx:220, the collapse toggle on every Pax artifact card, and marketplace.tsx:368, the table-row expander — both announce as 'button' and nothing else. Separately, its sibling at pax-artifact.tsx:226 IS labelled — `aria-label="Artifact Card"` on a button whose `onClick` is `onDismiss` and whose child is `<X/>`. The gate counts that as compliant; a screen-reader user hears 'Artifact Card, button' for a Close control. A label that does not describe the action is worse than none because it defeats the check. The ESLint rule also returns early on ANY `JSXSpreadAttribute` ('assume the spread carries the label'), an exemption nothing measures.
- **Fix:** Widen both populations: in the unit test, parse `<Button`/`<button`/`<ToggleGroupItem` openers and reduce children to a residual after stripping self-closing components, treating a ternary/logical whose branches are all icons as icon-only (the working scanner is ~40 lines); in the ESLint rule, recurse into `ConditionalExpression` and `LogicalExpression` children instead of returning false. Fix the two escapees, and change pax-artifact.tsx:226 to `aria-label="Dismiss artifact"`. Count and pin the spread exemption the same way the label test pins `components/ui/sidebar.tsx:328`.
- **Evidence:** Scripted scan reusing the test's own opener parser over 533 icon-only candidates; after filtering residuals that resolve to text (Verbs.*, {label}, etc.), the unnamed non-`size="icon"` survivors are pax-artifact.tsx:220 (`residual="{collapsed?:}"`) and marketplace.tsx:368 (`residual="{open?:}"`). Read both files at those lines, plus pax-artifact.tsx:226, to confirm by hand. Read eslint-rules/icon-button-needs-aria-label.cjs in full.

#### [medium/M] 206 Skeletons announce simultaneously inside repeat loops — up to 20 'Loading content' on one panel

- **Where:** `client/src/components/ui/skeleton.tsx:31-40 (`announce` defaults true); offenders include client/src/components/activity-timeline.tsx:293-297, client/src/components/borrower/StatementsPanel.tsx:96-102, client/src/components/comment-thread.tsx:243-246, client/src/components/compliance-settings.tsx:219/324/381/538`
- **Defect:** The Skeleton primitive is genuinely excellent — but its own docstring says to pass `announce={false}` 'inside a list whose parent already announces loading state', and 206 call sites inside `.map()`/`Array.from()` repeats do not. activity-timeline.tsx:285-297 is the clean example: a parent `<div role="status" aria-busy aria-label="Loading activity timeline">` wrapping `[1,2,3,4,5].map()` that renders FOUR skeletons each — twenty nested `role="status" aria-live="polite"` regions, each with sr-only 'Loading content'. NVDA and JAWS queue nested live regions, so the user hears the phrase up to twenty times before the content arrives. The result is that the good default becomes noise precisely on the heaviest pages.
- **Fix:** Invert the ergonomics rather than editing 206 sites by hand: add a `SkeletonGroup` (or a React context) that sets `announce=false` for every Skeleton beneath it, and have the existing `role="status"` loading wrappers provide it. Then add a gate that flags a `<Skeleton>` without `announce={false}` whose nearest ancestor already carries `role="status"`, seeded at the post-fix zero. Also correct the docstring's stale '257 callers' — the real number is 1,259.
- **Evidence:** Scripted scan: 1,259 `<Skeleton>` usages, 431 with `announce={false}`, 206 rendered inside a `.map(`/`Array.from(` repeat without the opt-out. Read activity-timeline.tsx:285-300 to verify the nesting by hand.

#### [medium/M] 133 of 143 data tables have no accessible name

- **Where:** `client/src/components/ui/table.tsx (no caption/name affordance in the `Table` wrapper); 72 raw `<table>` and 71 `<Table>` call sites across client/src`
- **Defect:** Screen-reader users navigate tabular data by jumping table-to-table and rely on the table's name to know which one they landed in. Only 5 elements carry `<caption>`/`<TableCaption>` and only 5 carry `aria-label`, leaving ~133 unnamed. Rows of financial data (Finance, Bookkeeping, notes pipelines, buyer blasts) are exactly the case where 'table with 7 columns and 40 rows' with no name is unusable. Separately, 305 of the 411 raw `<th>` elements lack `scope` — the shadcn `TableHead` primitive correctly defaults `scope="col"` (table.tsx:72), so this is confined to the hand-rolled tables.
- **Fix:** Make the name unavoidable rather than optional: require an `aria-label` (or a `TableCaption` child) in `Table`'s props type, defaulting to a `sr-only` caption. Migrate the 72 raw `<table>` elements onto the primitive, which also picks up `scope="col"` for free. Gate: assert every `<Table>`/`<table>` opener has a caption child or an aria-label, seeded at the post-migration zero.
- **Evidence:** `grep -rn "<table\b" client/src --include=*.tsx | wc -l` = 72; `<Table\b` = 71; `<caption|<TableCaption` = 5; `<table` with an inline `aria-label` = 5. `<th\b` = 411, of which 106 carry `scope`. Read table.tsx:69-83 to confirm TableHead's default.

#### [low/M] 10px body text (`text-micro`) is used 405 times, 221 of them on muted-foreground

- **Where:** `tailwind.config.ts:236 (`micro: ["10px", "12px"]`); 405 call sites, e.g. client/src/components/founder-chat/Composer.tsx:210, client/src/components/pax-artifact.tsx:246, client/src/components/solene/ThinkingBlock.tsx:35`
- **Defect:** 10px is below every mainstream minimum — Apple HIG's 11pt floor, Material's 12sp caption, and the practical readability floor for users with mild low vision who are not yet zooming. 221 of the 405 uses pair it with `text-muted-foreground`, i.e. the smallest type in the lowest-contrast colour. This is not a strict WCAG failure (1.4.4 is satisfied because it scales under zoom), but at the bar this directive sets, shipping 10px secondary text as a system token is a deliberate choice that should be re-examined rather than inherited. A further 40 sites use raw `text-[10px]`, bypassing the token entirely.
- **Fix:** Retire `micro` as a body-text token: keep it for tabular badge counts and pill labels only, and raise everything conveying prose to `caption` (11px) or `text-xs` (12px). Because it is a Tailwind token, the sweep is mechanical. Fold the 40 `text-[10px]` ad-hocs into the token in the same pass so the count is measurable thereafter.
- **Evidence:** `grep -rn "text-micro" client/src --include=*.tsx | wc -l` = 405; the same list filtered for `muted-foreground` = 221; `text-\[10px\]` = 40. Token definition read at tailwind.config.ts:230-238.

#### [low/S] Autonomy toggle descriptions are not linked to their switches, so the load-bearing sentence is never read

- **Where:** `client/src/pages/settings/pax-controls.tsx:726-740 (`SwitchRow`), consumed at :578, :591, :601`
- **Defect:** `SwitchRow` renders `<Label htmlFor={id}>{label}</Label>` — correct — and then the explanatory `line` in an unassociated `<div>`. A screen-reader user tabbing to the switch hears only the terse label; the sentence that actually says what the autopilot will be permitted to do (the rate, the scope, the current state) is never announced. WCAG 3.3.2 Labels or Instructions. Given that these switches govern what Pax does without asking, the description is the consequential half.
- **Fix:** Add `id={`${testId}-line`}` to the description div and `aria-describedby={`${testId}-line`}` to the `<Switch>`. Two tokens, one component, covers all three rows. The same pattern applies to the loading wrapper at :321, which carries `aria-busy="true"` on a role-less div (see the aria-label-on-generic finding).
- **Evidence:** Read client/src/pages/settings/pax-controls.tsx:715-741 and the three call sites at 578/591/601.

#### [low/S] A user who explicitly picks 'full motion' still gets a frozen UI if their OS says reduce

- **Where:** `client/src/index.css:2336-2360 (`@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) { *, *::before, *::after { … } }`) versus client/src/index.css:76-92`
- **Defect:** The token block at :76-92 is explicit that a manual preference wins — `:root:not([data-motion="full"])` is guarded precisely so a user who opted into full motion in Settings keeps it, with the comment 'Manual pick wins per Apple-native rule'. The global wildcard override at :2336 has no such guard: it zeroes every animation and transition whenever the OS reports `reduce`, regardless of `[data-motion="full"]`. The two mechanisms in the same file disagree, and the unguarded one wins because it is `!important`. The user-visible effect is that the Settings toggle silently does nothing for anyone whose OS preference is set — which is most of the people who would look for it.
- **Fix:** Add the same guard: `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) { :root:not([data-motion="full"]) *, … }`. One selector change, and it makes the Settings control honest.
- **Evidence:** Read client/src/index.css:74-95 and :2330-2362. The first block carries the `:root:not([data-motion="full"])` guard and an explanatory comment; the second does not.
